## Full text

<details><summary><b>DSI-5</b> — Sprint 5 — Scenario Lab & Draft Management</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`/scenarios` plus `lib/squad-score.ts`: save, compare and time-travel squad drafts, scored against each other.

Defines **SquadScore**, the points-equivalent term sum that still binds the rest of the app, and `riskPoints` — the single risk→points exchange rate every other engine reuses rather than re-deriving.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-05.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-6</b> — Sprint 6 — Player Comparison Engine</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`/compare` over `lib/scoring.ts` — head-to-head comparison returning strengths and weaknesses in named terms, not a single opaque score.

Two gaps left open at build time and tracked separately: the EO column (needed Sprint 10) and FPL's `form` term, dropped and disclosed via `COMPARISON_MODEL_NOTE` while FPL zeroed it between seasons. `form` was restored in Sprint 30 once GW1 populated it.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (sprint index)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-7</b> — Sprint 7 — Replacement Finder</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`findReplacements` and the `/builder` replacement panel: given a player to move on from, the affordable, legal alternatives ranked with a rationale string for each.

The rationale strings here are the pattern every later recommendation follows — recommendation, expected gain, confidence, risk, explanation, alternatives.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (sprint index)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-8</b> — Sprint 8 — Transfer Simulator</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`/transfers` over `lib/transfers.ts` — simulate a transfer and see the cost in separated terms: `+8.5 xP − 8 hit − 0.2 risk = +0.3`, never a bare net figure.

Standing decisions defined here: **TransferGain**, the sell-price rule, and the shared risk exchange rate.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-08.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-9</b> — Sprint 9 — Transfer Optimizer (up to 5 banked FTs)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`lib/transfer-optimizer.ts` and the `/transfers` plan panel: search transfer combinations across banked free transfers, scoring every branch through `simulateTransfers` rather than a second scorer.

Standing decision: `decisionMargin` **is a disclosed, user-set input**, not a modelled `FutureFlexibility` — when a term cannot be estimated honestly it becomes an input, never an invented coefficient tuned until the answer looks reasonable.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-09.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-10</b> — Sprint 11 — Captain & Bench Optimizer</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`lib/lineup.ts` — `optimiseLineup` picks the starting XI, captain and bench order, and is the one primitive later reused by the chip engine (`benchBoostAt`, `tripleCaptainAt`) and the Deadline Hub.

The spec's TeamAttack term is dropped and disclosed (`CAPTAIN_MODEL_NOTE`): FPL's team strength fields are zero for all 20 clubs. Still blocked — tracked separately.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (sprint index)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-11</b> — Sprint 12A — Manager Percentile Profile</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`lib/manager-profile.ts`, rendered on `/team`: the owner's history turned into a percentile profile off `manager_season_history`.

Watch the convention — FPL's `rank_percentage` is its own "top X%", where **lower is better**; the module flips it to a 0–100 higher-is-better `percentileScore`. Never mix the two on one screen.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-12.md` (§12A)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-12</b> — Sprint 12 — Chip Strategy Engine</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`/chips` over `lib/chips.ts`: when each chip is worth playing, scored as **ChipValue** — a one-primitive reuse of `optimiseLineup`/`optimizeSquad` rather than a separate chip scorer.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-12.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-13</b> — Sprint 12.5 — PL club manager intelligence (buildable slice)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-07. Twenty club tactical profiles seeded into `pl_managers` from `docs/pl-manager-profiles.json`, surfaced as `ClubTacticsGrid` — phases 1/2 and 7–9 of the source plan only.

Phases 3–6 (the System Fit multiplier) are **blocked, not deferred**: the manager-side tactical thresholds are transcribed opinion, not measured data. Half-unblocked by Sprint 15.6 on the player side.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-12.md` (§12.5)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-14</b> — Sprint 12.6 — Defensive Contribution engine fix, plus five surface fixes</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-08-08. xP engine **v1.4.0** — the Defensive Contribution scoring rule was mis-modelled; fixing it re-fitted the position calibration factors that still bind: `GKP 1.265 / DEF 1.2241 / MID 1.2238 / FWD 1.2576`.

Those factors are fitted **in-sample** — a refit against real 2026-27 results is its own open item.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-12.md` (§12.6)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-15</b> — Cold-start patch phase 1 — empirical-Bayes rate priors</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

Shrinks a player's per-90 rates toward a position/archetype prior by evidence weight (`n_eff` / `w_prior` / `mu_post`), so a player with 200 career minutes is not scored as if his rate were known.

Two rules came out of it that still bind: **a prior fitted on a filtered sample answers a different question** (fitting a playing-time prior only on 450+ minute seasons conditions on the very thing being predicted), and **a variance needs far more data than a mean** — estimate spread at the coarsest level that is still meaningful.

Phase 2 (external-league enrichment) is partly delivered by Sprint 15.6; the rest is gated. Docs: `docs/sprints/cold-start-patch.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-16</b> — Squad reconciliation — start/minutes water-fill (phases 1 & 2)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M1 · Core engines · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

xP engine v1.2.0 and v1.3.0. Per-player start probabilities and expected minutes have to add up to what a club can actually field; `solveWeightedWaterFill` reconciles them under an **evidence-weighted water-fill invariant** — a player with more evidence moves less.

Docs: `docs/sprints/squad-reconciliation.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-17</b> — Deadline Hub — /deadline</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-14. One place for every pre-deadline decision: countdown, `validateSquad` legality, per-player availability alerts, an `optimiseLineup` captain/XI recommendation diffed against the draft, an `optimizeTransfers` run behind an explicit button, `benchBoostAt`/`tripleCaptainAt` for this gameweek, and a squad-scoped `change_feed` slice.

Pure read/render over trusted engines — no new migration, function or table. Also de-duplicated two helpers found copy-pasted six and four times (`availabilityFromStatus`, `loadSeasonContext`).

Docs: `docs/sprints/additional-info.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-18</b> — Squad view on Deadline Hub and My Team</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-15. Both pages default to the squad actually imported from FPL (`TeamState.entryId`, `resolveRequestedDraft`) and draw it on a read-only pitch — `PitchView` generalised from `LineupResult` to a `SquadLayout` so one component renders either a projection or a known XI.

`lib/manager-picks.ts` sums per-fixture for double gameweeks and takes the XI from `position`, not `multiplier`, so Bench Boost cannot be mistaken for the starting XI. The displayed total is deliberately never reconciled with FPL's own score, since `automatic_subs` isn't synced.

Docs: `docs/sprints/additional-info.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-19</b> — Chip strategy planning — pin a chip to a gameweek</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-16. `TeamState.chipPlan` + `lib/chip-plan.ts` let a chip be pinned to a future gameweek; `optimizeTransfers`/`simulateTransfers` gained chip-aware branches, and `lib/transfer-path.ts` sequences a bounded forward transfer path around the planned chips.

Extends Sprint 12's chip engine and Sprint 9's optimiser rather than either alone, so it carries no sprint number of its own.

Docs: `docs/wiki/chip-plan.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-20</b> — Sprint 15.5 — Hidden Gems (value-discovery filter)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

`player_rate_profile` + `lib/hidden-gems.ts`, wired into the `/players` and builder-picker filters: an archetype filter for under-owned players whose rates justify more than their price.

Also records why `worldfootballR` does not help the cold-start problem — checked, not assumed.

Docs: `docs/sprints/hidden-gems.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-21</b> — Sprint 15.6 — Championship cold-start priors (FootyStats drop)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

xP engine **v1.5.0**. 33 players with zero PL minutes re-primed from real Championship stats via `external_player_seasons`, extracted auditably by `scripts/extract-footystats.ts`.

Two rules that still bind came from this: every external drop must clear a **three-check gate** (per-player values, genuine origin, no overlap with players who already carry PL minutes); and a **league-translation λ is fitted per metric off a real cross-league cohort, never invented or shared across metrics** — xg90 0.200 / xa90 0.280 / yellow90 0.844, with `dc90` left untranslated because no cohort could fit it.

Docs: `docs/sprints/championship-priors.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-22</b> — Sprint 15.8 — Gameweek planning view, unified filters, comparison fixes</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

A gameweek dropdown on `/builder` (`squadEventAgg`, `projectionAtEvent`), `components/player-filters.tsx` shared by `/players` and the builder picker instead of two filter implementations, and tie handling fixed in `/compare`'s table.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (sprint index)

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-23</b> — Sprint 13 — Live Matchday Hub</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built and **verified live against the real GW1 opener**, 2026-08-21. `lib/gameweek-state.ts` drives a live card group on `/deadline`; `sync-live-gameweek` left its `skipped` branch and a spot-check matched FPL's own live feed exactly.

The dry run found a real gap too: `sync-fixtures` only ran hourly, so `fixtures.started` lagged kickoff by up to ~55 minutes — fixed the same day with a self-gated 2-minute cadence.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-13.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-24</b> — Sprint 10 — Ownership Intelligence (exact mini-league slice)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-21, the deadline that made any entry's picks public. `sync-league-picks` + `lib/ownership.ts` compute **exact** effective ownership for the leagues in `manager_leagues` — no sampling, no estimate. Rendered on `/leagues` in Sprint 29.

The top-1k sample is **not** an engineering blocker: a 2026-08-30 load test synced league 314 (9.9M entries) end to end — 2000 rank-ordered entries, 30,000 picks, 0 failures, 47s, two real bugs found and fixed at that scale. The test rows were deleted afterwards, so sampling it for real is an open item, not an unproven pipeline.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-10.md`, `docs/sprints/sprint-29.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-25</b> — GW1 live-hub follow-ups</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Improvement

Built 2026-08-21, the same evening — four gaps found by using the app during the real opener, all fixed against data already in the database (one new column, `player_live_stats.explain`):

* Live fixture event detail (`fixtures.stats` already carried goals/assists/cards/bonus) as a shared `LiveFixtures` component on `/deadline` and `/fixtures`.
* `/fixtures` stopped collapsing the gameweek being played — `gameweeks.is_next` flips hours before kickoff.
* Player detail panel gained FPL's own points breakdown (`explain`, stored verbatim — never a second implementation of `scoring_rules`), season totals and DC action counts.
* News duplicates: BBC's `<guid>` carries a changing `#fragment`, so 46% of `news_items` were re-fetched copies. Fixed at ingestion, 150 existing rows cleaned.

Docs: `docs/sprints/additional-info.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-26</b> — Sprint 14 — Authentication & Team Sync (+14.1–14.4)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Supabase Auth (Google OAuth primary, magic link as fallback), owner-scoped cloud drafts, and squad import. `mergeDrafts` is the one merge rule shared by file import and cloud sync — unseen draft wins, else newer `updatedAt`.

Includes the **FPL-login probe**, whose finding still binds: PingOne offers no password grant and its one reachable flow opens with bot detection, so automated credential login is impossible and a pasted cookie authenticates nothing. Real purchase prices come from the owner pasting `my-team` JSON, parsed by `teamStateFromMyTeamJson`. `app/settings/fpl` and the `fpl-session`/`fpl-my-team` functions are superseded but deliberately still deployed.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-14.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-27</b> — Sprint 17a — Model validation (walk-forward backtest)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

Built and run 2026-08-18: `ingest-fpl-archive` Edge Function + `scripts/backtest-walkforward.ts`.

**The finding is a null result, and it is the point of the sprint.** Out of sample, the xP model **underperforms a naive last-5-gameweeks baseline in every season tested**. This is the evidence base every later model question is gated against — and the reason `positionCalibration`'s refit was deliberately *not* rushed into the same pass.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-28</b> — Sprint 18 — Squad structure & chip sequencing</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-19. Effective Starting XI budget (`lib/squad-budget.ts`), a real chip-sequencing bug fixed in `planTransferPath`, calendar-derived chip-sequence presets on `/chips`, and transfer reversibility (`Replacement.exitRoutes`) on `/builder` and `/transfers`.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-18.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-29</b> — Sprint 20 — RSS news ingestion</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Feature

Built 2026-08-21. `sync-news` Edge Function over a table-driven `news_sources`, tiered player/club entity resolution, `/changes` renamed to `/news` with a Feeds tab, an "In the news" block on the player detail panel, and a team-news strip on `/deadline`.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-20.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-30</b> — Sprint 21 — Squad-view fix, leagues list, FDR sorting, league table</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M2 · Season launch · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-08-21. Fixed `/team`'s stale-XI player-dropping bug **at the root** (`hasConsistentLineup` in `lib/team-state.ts`) and removed the broken "Current squad" mode rather than patching around it. Added a leagues list on `/team` off `manager_leagues`, search/sort on `/fixtures`' FDR, and a Premier League Table tab.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-21.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-31</b> — Sprint 19 — Design system & interaction feedback</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M3 · Design, density & navigation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Improvement

Built 2026-08-19/20. Fixed Geist Sans never actually applying, adopted `focus-visible` states across `/transfers`/`/chips`/`/builder`, stopped `optimizeTransfers`/`runChipEngine` freezing the tab, ranked the cards on `/deadline`/`/transfers`/`/chips`, and promoted xP to the dominant number in the player detail panel.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-19.md`, `docs/wiki/design-system.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-32</b> — Rivals fixes & card density</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M3 · Design, density & navigation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-08-20, prompted by the owner using the app. `/team` rivals now sync before writing (a candidate no longer has to be loaded through the Manager ID form first), a manager with no completed seasons is shown rather than silently dropped, and rivals gained a This season / Career tab.

New `CollapsibleCard` primitive repacks `/deadline`, `/transfers` and `/chips` — Sprint 19 ranked those cards but left the dead space beside short ones.

Docs: `docs/sprints/rivals-and-card-density.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-33</b> — AI design-audit response</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M3 · Design, density & navigation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Improvement

Built 2026-08-22. Sticky header + `ContextBar` (deadline countdown, bank, FT) on every page, current-season xG/xA and an xMins/Start% column on `/players`, nine hover-only tooltips converted to touch-accessible `TapToReveal`, focus rings closed on the six pages Sprint 19 didn't reach, and mobile fixes on `/transfers` and `/compare`.

Worth keeping: **most of the audit's own P0–P3 findings were checked and found false** (no gradients, no scroll hijacking, no hover-dimming, the FDR palette already correct), and its neutral-palette recommendation was rejected with reasons.

Docs: `docs/sprints/design-audit-response.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-34</b> — Mobile one-handed reachability</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M3 · Design, density & navigation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-08-22, prompted by the owner's own phone screenshot. `NavLinks` split into `DesktopNav`/`MobileNav` and the mobile trigger moved to the header's left edge (it had been floating mid-header from two competing `ml-auto`s); the drawer became a bottom sheet so all 11 links land in the thumb zone.

<details><summary><b>DSI-44</b> — Sprint 30 — xP form-blend attempt 2 + the money-accounting bug chain</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M4 · In-season correction · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-08-30/09-02. Two halves:

**Model (null result).** `/compare`'s `COMPARISON_WEIGHTS` restored to the plan's full five-term weighting once `form` data exists. A per-position bias-correction sweep was added to the walk-forward harness and **no weight clears the gate** — the correction learned from two under-predicting seasons overshoots on a third. Not shipped; the sweep now reports this permanently.

**The real find.** "Squad value is already correct" turned out to be one level too shallow, and chased into a bug chain: Bank and affordability mixing sell-value with purchase-price bases, FPL's own `transfers.value` disagreeing with per-player selling prices, `validateSquad`'s over-budget false positive, and `freeTransfers` reading `limit` instead of `limit − made`. Ended with `TeamState.bank` **becoming the stored primitive** instead of a residual derived from a frozen total.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-30.md`

<details><summary><b>DSI-45</b> — Sprint 31 — Chip awareness end to end, display-only FDR, public-repo pre-flight</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M4 · In-season correction · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Bug

Built 2026-09-03. The owner played Triple Captain, pasted the JSON, and nothing acknowledged it. The parse was fine; three things downstream were not — nothing rendered `activeChip`, `chipAt` (the fact-beats-plan reconciler) had **zero callers** so the projection scored the captain as merely doubled, and the chip had no gameweek of its own so a stale draft would claim it live in the *next* gameweek.

Fixed with `TeamState.activeChipEvent`, a `multiplier`-based cross-check proving 3xc/bboost from the payload's own arithmetic rather than an unverified enum, `fplActiveChipAt`/`chipEntriesInForce`, and surfacing on `/deadline` and the ContextBar. Also: a strength-derived FDR on `/fixtures`, **display-only because it cannot be backtested at all** (`teams` carries no strength history), and the three public-repo blockers settled.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-31.md`

<details><summary><b>DSI-47</b> — Sprint 32 — Edge Function lockdown</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M5 · Platform hardening & model validation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Improvement

Built 2026-09-05; **fully deployed and verified 2026-09-06**. Every `/functions/v1/` endpoint was callable by anyone holding the publishable key, which is public by design.

Split by caller: eight cron-only functions now require an `x-cron-secret` minted from Vault (`_shared/cron-auth.ts`), and the two browser-invoked ones get `verifyUser` plus a per-user rate limit derived from the busiest 10-minute window ever observed (27 calls), not a tuned constant. The scope doc named one `?force=1` self-gate bypass; grepping found **five**. `sync_runs`' public-read policy would have published user ids — fixed with a column-level grant.

The verification gate, run for the first time, caught a real regression: `sync-manager` failing to boot on a bad import — a bug `tsc` structurally cannot catch, since `supabase/functions/**` is excluded from tsconfig.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-32.md` §5c

<details><summary><b>DSI-48</b> — Sprint 34 — Post-GW3: accuracy scoreboard, 2026-27 in the model, harness fixes</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M5 · Platform hardening & model validation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

Built 2026-09-06. GW3 finishing cleared the accuracy scoreboard's own stated blocker (≥2 gameweeks archived *and* scored), so `lib/prediction-accuracy.ts` finally has a caller — a 1000-row cap bug in `loadArchivedEvents` would have silently halved its sample.

2026-27 added as a fourth walk-forward target plus a narrow `currentSeasonScope: "minutes"` blend. `scope=all` **clears 2 of 4 seasons,** `scope=minutes` **2 of 4 — neither ships.**

More important than the sweep: **the harness was corrupting its own inputs.** `fetchAll` paged with no `ORDER BY`, so offset pages repeated ~26% of rows; and position was resolved from today's roster rather than that season's archived value. Fixing both restored the published backtest tables — the tables were right, the harness was drifting against itself. Two consecutive runs are now byte-identical.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-34.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-49</b> — Sprint 35 — Results-derived FDR (measured; deliberately not shipped)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M5 · Platform hardening & model validation · **Priority:** No priority · **Labels:** Model

Built and measured 2026-09-06. **The method works, and it still does not ship.**

`lib/fdr-derived.ts` reconstructs both sides of every fixture from `player_gameweek_stats` (`fixtures` holds only 2026-27), rates opponents rolling and shrunk toward the league mean, and bands to 1–5 by quintile. It improves MAE and Pearson r against the harness's neutral `fdr=3` in **all four seasons**, and a shuffled control — same rating values, team mapping destroyed — is worse than neutral everywhere, so the signal is real.

**Why it doesn't ship:** the baseline is neutral, but production `fdrRun` uses FPL's **official** FDR. Derived-beats-official is untested. Also fixed a venue double-count that had roughly halved the effect. `priorGames` is deliberately not tuned from these numbers.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-35.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-50</b> — GW5 check-in — bias sign read at n=4. Re-read at locked data; next read GW6</summary>

**State:** In Progress · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M6 · GW5 check-in · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Model · **Relations:** related DSI-54

> **Status 2026-09-21 (Sprint 38).** Re-read at locked GW5 data — 10/10 fixtures `finished`, bonus applied. **The gate is answered: the sign did not flip**, and the figures are identical to the 2026-09-20 run, so bonus confirming moved nothing. Pooled bias −0.080 (SE 0.044, t=−1.82) over n=2,587.
>
> The decisive finding is structural rather than in the headline: the residual decomposes into a −0.714 no-show cohort (n=1,364) cancelling a +0.627 appeared cohort (n=1,223), with expected minutes right in aggregate (30.6 vs 30.4) and wrong per player (47.1 vs 64.2 on appearers). A multiplicative points scale cannot separate those, so [DSI-53](<https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53>) stays written out of the plan and the question moved to expected-**minutes** calibration — spun out as [DSI-178](<https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178>).
>
> **Held open only for a fifth read at GW6 (2026-10-10)**, after the three-week international break. Nothing between now and then is informative. Full tables in the 2026-09-21 comment below and `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §1.

---

**After GW5 is scored, \~2026-09-21** (deadline 2026-09-18). Cheap, \~20 minutes, and it is a *look*, not a decision.

The scoreboard reaches n=4 (GW2–5 archived and scored). **Read the sign, not the magnitude:** the shipped model over-predicted by 0.248 pts/player-fixture across GW2+GW3. If that has held across four gameweeks it is probably a real calibration offset and refitting `positionCalibration` at GW10 is worth scheduling. If it has flipped sign, GW2+GW3 was noise and the GW10 refit needs rethinking rather than running.

**Nothing else is worth touching yet.** The last-5 baseline needs six *played* gameweeks and cannot fire before GW6 — **2026-10-10**, with a three-week international break in between. Do not read a GW5 blend or FDR re-run as informative.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` "Next up", `docs/sprints/sprint-34.md` §1

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## Re-read at locked GW5 data (2026-09-21) — conclusion unchanged, issue stays open for GW6
> 
> GW5 is now fully locked: 10/10 fixtures `finished`, bonus applied, `player_gameweek_stats` resynced 2026-09-21 05:50. Re-ran the scoreboard read over GW2–5, archived `xp` vs `total_points`, fixtures with `finished = true`. Sign convention is the scoreboard's — **actual − predicted**, so negative = over-prediction.
> 
> | | n | bias | MAE | Pearson r |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | GW2 | 620 | −0.150 | 1.396 | 0.518 |
> | GW3 | 652 | −0.089 | 1.323 | 0.468 |
> | GW4 | 656 | −0.079 | 1.379 | 0.501 |
> | GW5 | 659 | **−0.006** | 1.348 | 0.490 |
> | **Pooled** | **2,587** | **−0.080** (SE 0.044, t = −1.82) | 1.361 | 0.494 |
> 
> **The gate's answer: the sign did not flip.** But the magnitude collapsed — Sprint 34's −0.248 over GW2+GW3 is −0.080 over four, ~1.8 SE from zero. These figures are identical to the 2026-09-20 run, so GW5's bonus confirming moved nothing. The coverage-gate fix (PR #30, commit `33b666b`) is what made the earlier reading trustworthy; without it the panel showed −0.589.
> 
> **Cohort decomposition — the finding that matters:**
> 
> | cohort | n | bias | mean xP | mean actual | mean exp. mins | mean actual mins |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | appeared | 1,223 | **+0.627** | 2.384 | 3.011 | 47.1 | 64.2 |
> | no-show | 1,364 | **−0.714** | 0.714 | 0.000 | 15.8 | 0.0 |
> | all | 2,587 | −0.080 | 1.504 | 1.424 | 30.6 | 30.4 |
> 
> Two large opposite biases that nearly cancel. A multiplicative points scale cannot separate them, so **the GW10 `positionCalibration` refit (DSI-53) stays written out of the plan.**
> 
> Per position, only GKP survives as its own bias: **−0.319** (n=281, t=−2.78). DEF −0.033, MID −0.052, FWD −0.095 are all inside noise.
> 
> Expected minutes are right in aggregate (30.6 vs 30.4) and wrong per player (47.1 vs 64.2 on appearers, 15.8 vs 0 on no-shows) — a **discrimination** failure, not a calibration offset. Spun out as its own issue.
> 
> **Next read: GW6, 2026-10-10** (three-week international break in between). Nothing between now and then is informative — the last-5 baseline still needs six *played* gameweeks.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-20
>
> ## GW5 check-in, run 2026-09-20 — the bias has not flipped sign, but it has collapsed
> 
> Run a day early, while GW5's last four fixtures were still syncing. **Re-check tomorrow once `fixtures.finished` flips** — GW5's bonus is still provisional and four of its ten fixtures had not landed at the time of this run.
> 
> ### A data defect found first, which changes the reading
> 
> `player_gameweek_stats` had `minutes = 0, total_points = 0` for every player in the four GW5 fixtures that kicked off on the 20th (ids 44, 45, 49, 50 — 257 player-fixtures). `sync-player-history` does a full pass only every 20h, so a played-but-unwritten window is routine. Those rows *join*, so the scoreboard turned them into genuine blank returns and reported **GW5 bias −0.589**. Scored against `player_live_stats` (refreshed every 2 min) the real figure is **−0.006**.
> 
> Fixed in this session — `scoreEvent` now gates on per-fixture completeness and the panel states coverage ("GW5 (6 of 10 fixtures)"). Detector validated across the whole warehouse: over four settled seasons (~150 gameweeks) every fixture with stats rows also had minutes; GW5 mid-sync is the only exception anywhere. `fixtures.finished_provisional` was **true** for all ten GW5 fixtures including the four with no stats at all, so it is not a usable proxy for "actuals are in".
> 
> ### The scoreboard at n=4
> 
> Two independent routes agree: `player_live_stats` for all of GW5 gives **−0.080 over n=2,587**; the shipped completeness gate (GW5's 6 landed fixtures only) gives **−0.084 over n=2,330**.
> 
> | Cohort | n | bias | MAE | r |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | All | 2,330 | **−0.084** | 1.370 | 0.483 |
> | GKP | 251 | −0.352 | 1.236 | 0.573 |
> | DEF | 766 | −0.033 | 1.623 | 0.412 |
> | MID | 1,031 | −0.047 | 1.266 | 0.504 |
> | FWD | 282 | −0.113 | 1.182 | 0.540 |
> 
> Per gameweek: **−0.150, −0.089, −0.079, −0.006** (GW5 on live data; +0.020 over its 6 landed fixtures).
> 
> ### Reading the sign, per this issue's own gate
> 
> Same sign four times running, so it has **not** flipped — but it is shrinking monotonically toward zero, and the pooled figure sits ~1.8 standard errors from zero. Two things ate Sprint 34's −0.248:
> 
> 1. **Half of it was provisional bonus.** GW2+GW3 on the *identical 1,272 rows* now reads **−0.119**, not −0.248, because GW3's bonus has since been confirmed. The original figure was measured while GW3 was `finished_provisional`.
> 2. **GW4 and GW5 came in near zero.**
> 
> ### The structural finding — `positionCalibration` is the wrong term
> 
> | | n | bias |
> |---|---|---|
> | Did not play (min = 0) | 1,364 | **−0.714** |
> | Played (min > 0) | 1,223 | **+0.627** |
> 
> (GW2–5, live actuals for GW5.) The model over-predicts non-appearances and under-predicts appearances, and the two very nearly cancel. `positionCalibration` is a multiplicative points scale, so it cannot separate those: fitting it to the ≈0 aggregate bakes in noise, and fitting it to the over-prediction would make the played cohort worse.
> 
> **Recommendation: do not run the GW10 `positionCalibration` refit as specified** (roadmap "GW10 batch" item 3). The live question moved from points calibration to **expected-minutes calibration**. Items 1 (blend sweep) and 2 (derived vs official FDR) are untouched by this.
> 
> Only GKP survives as a separate non-trivial bias (−0.352, ~3 SE) and is worth a look on its own at GW10.
> 
> Related: DSI-54 was also re-checked against fresh data in the same session.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-51</b> — GW10 — re-run the current-season blend sweep</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Model

**After GW10 is scored, ~2026-11-09.** Shares one harness run with the other two GW10 items.

`scope=all` clears 2 of 4 seasons and `scope=minutes` 2 of 4; both fail on 2026-27, which had two gameweeks of blend evidence. At GW10 it has ~9, and the last-5 baseline finally produces rows for it too. The narrow minutes-only hypothesis is **untested, not refuted** — its apparent 3-of-4 advantage in Sprint 34 came from the corrupted harness inputs and did not survive the fix.

Reproduce with `npx tsx scripts/backtest-walkforward.ts`, which sweeps and reports this permanently.

**Do not narrow the gate.** Report the run the same way whether or not it passes — the project's record is that the null result was the finding three times over.

<details><summary><b>DSI-52</b> — GW10 — run the derived-FDR gate that matters: derived vs official</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Model

**After GW10 is scored, ~2026-11-09.** Shares one harness run with the other two GW10 items.

Sprint 35 measured derived vs *neutral* (`fdr=3`) and it cleared in all four seasons with a shuffled control proving the signal is real. But production `ScoredPlayer.fdrRun` uses FPL's **official** FDR, so derived-beats-official is what would justify replacing it. Official FDR *is* available for the current season in `fixtures` (`team_h_difficulty`/`team_a_difficulty`) — this only ever needed enough scored gameweeks. n=471 at GW3 was far too thin.

**Do not narrow the gate**, and do not tune `priorGames` off these numbers (2 and 8 clear 4/4, 4 clears 3/4, all within 0.012 on the thinnest season — noise, not structure).

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-35.md` §5

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-53</b> — GW10 — refit positionCalibration, if the GW5 sign held</summary>

**State:** Canceled · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Canceled:** 2026-09-21 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Model

**Conditional on the GW5 check-in** showing the bias direction holding. Shares the GW10 harness run.

The shipped factors (`GKP 1.265 / DEF 1.2241 / MID 1.2238 / FWD 1.2576`, Sprint 12.6) are fitted **in-sample** on a 209-player full-season cohort. Three gameweeks is far too thin to replace them — refitting on ~1,200 player-fixtures would bake this season's noise into a permanent constant.

**Do not narrow the gate.** If the GW5 check-in showed the bias flipping sign, rethink this rather than running it.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (Blocked table), `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-20
>
> ## Precondition answered by DSI-50 (2026-09-20) — and the answer is "don't refit this"
> 
> This issue is gated on "DSI-50's sign holding". The GW5 check-in ran on 2026-09-20 and the gate resolved in a way neither branch of it anticipated. **Leaving the status alone — this needs re-scoping, not running.** Full working: `docs/sprints/gw5-check-in.md` §2.
> 
> ### The sign held; the magnitude did not
> 
> Four negatives in a row (−0.150, −0.089, −0.079, −0.006), so technically the precondition is met. But the aggregate a refit would be fitted against is now **−0.084 over n=2,330**, ~1.8 standard errors from zero — down from Sprint 34's −0.248 at n=2. Roughly half of that drop is not new data at all: GW2+GW3 on the **identical 1,272 rows** now reads −0.119, because GW3's bonus has since been confirmed. Sprint 34 flagged the provisionality in its own panel copy; this is that caveat coming true at about half the headline.
> 
> ### The real reason not to run it
> 
> | | n | bias |
> |---|---|---|
> | Did not play (`minutes = 0`) | 1,364 | **−0.714** |
> | Played (`minutes > 0`) | 1,223 | **+0.627** |
> 
> The model over-predicts non-appearances and under-predicts appearances, and the two nearly cancel — which is why the aggregate reads ≈0 while neither half does.
> 
> `positionCalibration` is a **multiplicative scale on points**. There is no value of it that fixes both halves, because they point in opposite directions: fit the ≈0 aggregate and you bake in noise as a permanent constant; fit the over-prediction half and the played cohort gets worse. The parameter cannot represent the error that was measured.
> 
> ### What this should become instead
> 
> 1. **Expected-minutes calibration** — `mpg` / `start_share`, the terms that actually set the non-appearance half. This is where the error lives.
> 2. **GKP separately** — the one cohort still carrying a non-trivial bias of its own, −0.352 (≈3 SE), against DEF −0.033 / MID −0.047 / FWD −0.113.
> 
> Neither is shaped into a gate yet, so neither is a ready replacement for this ticket — but both are better questions than the one in the title.
> 
> ### Docs updated to match
> 
> - `docs/roadmap.md` — GW10 batch item 3 rewritten from "refit `positionCalibration`" to "do not", and the Blocked-table row reworded.
> - `docs/architecture.md` and `docs/phase-4-model.md` — both said the parameter "must be refitted once real 2026/27 results exist"; those results now exist and say otherwise.
> - `docs/wiki/xp-model.md`, `blocked-and-data-gaps.md`, `methodology.md` ("A correction is only worth fitting if it can represent the error").
> 
> One caveat worth holding: GW5's bonus was still provisional when this ran, and provisional bonus is exactly what halved the Sprint 34 figure once already. DSI-50 stays In Progress for a re-read once `fixtures.finished` flips. The expected movement is small and upward, which would only strengthen the conclusion here.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-54</b> — Price-change prediction step 3 — falls half run and FAILED; rises still held</summary>

**State:** In Progress · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Model

> **Status 2026-09-21 (Sprint 38,** [Dsinha97/fpl-app#31](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/sprint-38-the-player-profile-the-price-reading-and-a-shortlist-9433c2f7d644)**).** The falls half ran. **It failed its gate at every budget**, so per the gate's own instruction the heuristic ships and nothing is wired in. Rises remain held to \~GW10–12. This issue stays open for that half.
>
> The run also corrected an earlier result posted on this issue: a first pass appeared to pass at K=10/K=20, on a feature that differenced `transfers_in_event` across gameweek resets. That handicapped the naive baseline, which shares the same input — fixing it lifted the baseline more than the model. **When a fitted model beats a naive baseline that shares its inputs, check the inputs before believing the model.**
>
> Two shipped bugs found along the way, both in the *descriptive* tool this issue's step 2 delivered:
>
> 1. `loadPriceProgress` was unpaged against the 1000-row cap and had been reading \~2% of its data since Sprint 29 — it looked quiet rather than broken.
> 2. `transfers_in_event`/`transfers_out_event` are per-gameweek counters FPL zeroes at every deadline; differencing across one inverted the sign for **89%** of players.
>
> Thresholds are now fitted rather than flat guesses — falls `3,577 + 31,870` per 1% owned (R²=0.811), rises flat 378,000 (R²=0.022) — and the verdict ladder was renamed to describe position rather than predict tonight, since crossing a threshold is only \~10% predictive. See the comments below for the full tables, and `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §2–§3.
>
> **Remaining work on this issue:** the rises classifier, once \~GW10–12 gives enough positives to resolve rather than merely fail.

---

Steps 1–2 shipped in Sprint 29: the sampling fix (a bounded \~2h watchlist) and the descriptive tool (`priceProgress()` reports a direction and 0–1 progress toward a documented, user-adjustable threshold — `"unknown"`, not a guess, below two post-change samples).

Step 3 stays out until enough watchlist history has accumulated, and is gated the way the xP blend was: **walk-forward over accumulated price history, scored on precision/recall of "rises tonight" against a naive top-N-by-net-transfers baseline.** If it does not beat the baseline, ship the heuristic and say so.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-29.md`

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## §2c gate re-run as a three-arm ablation
> 
> The first run compared scaled against the pre-Sprint-38 constants and returned MIXED. That comparison could not separate two different claims — that ownership *scaling* helps, and that the old *levels* were simply wrong. They were wrong (rises fire at ~378k against a 200k default), so scaled was being handed a badly-levelled opponent and any win was ambiguous.
> 
> Three arms now, the middle one being the honest baseline:
> 
> | arm | what it is |
> |---|---|
> | `legacy` | the pre-Sprint-38 constants |
> | `flat` | the best single constant per direction, refit walk-forward on the same training events the scaled arm sees, **minus ownership** |
> | `scaled` | the ownership line |
> 
> `flat` vs `scaled` is the ablation that matters: same data, same walk forward, differing only in whether ownership is consulted.
> 
> **Ranking (recall at budget):**
> 
> | | K=10 | K=20 | K=40 | K=80 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | legacy | 10.6% | 16.1% | 26.5% | 34.1% |
> | flat | 6.1% | 10.1% | 22.2% | 42.3% |
> | scaled | 10.3% | 19.0% | 31.5% | 51.3% |
> | nights scaled-v-flat | 13/7 | 13/6 | 16/7 | 14/5 |
> | sign-test p | 0.2632 | 0.1671 | 0.0931 | 0.0636 |
> 
> **The decomposition is the result:**
> 
> | direction | legacy F1 | from the level | from the shape | final |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | rise | 25.6% | **+3.5** | +0.8 | 29.9% |
> | fall | 10.1% | +1.4 | **+4.6** | 16.0% |
> 
> For rises the *level* does the work and the shape adds almost nothing — matching the window probe's R²=0.022. For falls the *shape* does the work, +4.6 against the level's +1.4 — matching R²=0.811.
> 
> **That is exactly the split that shipped — scaled falls, flat rises — corroborated by an arm the first run did not have.**
> 
> ### The verdict did not change
> 
> **Still MIXED.** Scaled now beats the fair baseline at every budget (the first run lost at K=10) and every p-value improved against a *harder* opponent — but the best is 0.0636 against a Bonferroni alpha of 0.0125. 23 scored nights is not enough power.
> 
> The ranking evidence is short of significance and I am not claiming otherwise. What shipped rests on the window probe's direct measurement of the mechanism (R²=0.811, n=248), with this ablation as corroboration of its shape — not on this gate passing, because it doesn't.
> 
> ### One incidental finding worth keeping
> 
> The fitted-flat arm is **worse than legacy at tight budgets** (6.1% vs 10.6% at K=10). Its fall level (~71k, the mean firing magnitude) fires for far more players than legacy's 150k, which ranks badly when the budget is small. A better-centred constant is not automatically a better one.
> 
> Still open: the rise *level* is a mean over 55 events rather than a mechanism, and this gate should be re-run again as nights accumulate.
> 
> Harness: `scripts/price-threshold-gate.ts`. Full tables: `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §2f.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## Ownership-scaled threshold: gated 2026-09-21. MIXED — not shipped.
> 
> `scripts/price-threshold-gate.ts`. Walk-forward: for night *t* the ownership→threshold line is fitted on price changes from nights **before** *t* only. Scored two ways, because a threshold does two jobs — and a win needs **both**, since better ordering is worthless if the verdict it drives is still wrong, and a better verdict on worse ordering is luck.
> 
> 23 scored nights, 412 change events. Fits:
> 
> ```
> rise:   336,900 + 1,740 per 1% owned  (n=58)   -- essentially FLAT
> fall:     3,577 + 31,790 per 1% owned (n=341)  -- steeply ownership-scaled
> ```
> 
> **Ranking** (recall at budget, paired per-night sign test, Bonferroni across four budgets as the classifier gate does):
> 
> | K | flat | scaled | won/lost | p |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | 10 | 10.6% | 10.3% | 8/9 | 1.0000 |
> | 20 | 16.1% | 19.0% | 10/9 | 1.0000 |
> | 40 | 26.5% | 31.5% | 10/7 | 0.6291 |
> | 80 | 34.1% | 51.3% | 15/5 | 0.0414 |
> 
> Best p = 0.0414, Bonferroni needs < 0.0125 — **does not survive.**
> 
> **Classification** at the boundary the UI renders:
> 
> | | precision | recall | F1 |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | rise flat | 14.7% | 97.1% | 25.6% |
> | rise scaled | 21.9% | 47.1% | **29.9%** |
> | fall flat | 8.7% | 12.0% | 10.1% |
> | fall scaled | 9.8% | 43.7% | **16.0%** |
> 
> **Verdict: MIXED.** Classification improves in both directions; ranking does not survive correction. The flat defaults stay and stay user-adjustable.
> 
> ### Two things worth carrying forward
> 
> 1. **Rises barely scale with ownership at all** (slope 1,740/pct ≈ 0). The bucket table I posted earlier suggested they did; a proper fit over all 58 events says that was noise. What is wrong for rises is the **level** — ~337k measured against a shipped 200k — not the shape. Don't read a trend off buckets.
> 2. One methodological change, disclosed in the script: each direction is fitted and scored **independently** rather than waiting for both to have 25 training events. Requiring both held falls (341 events) hostage to rises (60) and cost two thirds of the scorable nights. That widens the test rather than narrowing it — each direction is still scored only on nights its own fit came from strictly earlier events.
> 
> ### The finding that needed no fit, and shipped
> 
> **Precision of the best threshold obtainable is 9.8% (falls) and 21.9% (rises).** Crossing a threshold is followed by a change that night about one time in ten — and the ladder was calling that "Expected to fall tonight". That is how Palmer wore the label for three days.
> 
> The percentage was always documented as progress rather than probability. The **label** was what contradicted it:
> 
> | was | now | reads |
> |---|---|---|
> | `expected` | `past` | "Past your fall threshold" |
> | `very likely` | `close` | "Close to your fall threshold" |
> | `possible` | `approaching` | "Moving toward a fall" |
> | `not tonight` | `far` | "Well short of a fall" |
> 
> `isImminent` → `isNearThreshold`. `PRICE_WATCH_MODEL_NOTE`, the `/players` tooltip and the card copy now carry the measured hit rate and the ownership caveat.
> 
> This is the more useful outcome of the whole thread: the threshold is a tuning problem more data may settle, but a label promising 90% while delivering 10% was wrong at *any* threshold.
> 
> Re-run the gate once more nights accumulate. Falls are the half with the evidence; rises need a level correction rather than scaling.
> 
> Method and full tables: `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §2c–2d.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## Withdrawing the result above — the gate FAILS. Ship the heuristic.
> 
> The run I posted earlier used a feature computed the wrong way, and the error flattered the model. Corrected figures:
> 
> | K | model (before) | base (before) | **model (correct)** | **base (correct)** | sign-test p |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | 10 | 6.0% | 3.1% | **8.8%** | **8.2%** | 0.5000 |
> | 20 | 10.2% | 6.8% | **13.1%** | **12.8%** | 1.0000 |
> | 40 | 15.3% | 13.4% | **25.0%** | **23.0%** | 0.1796 |
> | 80 | 26.7% | 27.0% | **40.6%** | **42.0%** | 0.7744 |
> 
> **Nothing is significant at any budget.** The gate's own instruction applies: ship the heuristic and say so. `priceProgress` remains the reading; nothing is wired in.
> 
> ### Why the first run was wrong
> 
> `transfers_in_event` / `transfers_out_event` are **per-gameweek counters FPL zeroes at every deadline.** Both the shipped `netTransfersSinceLastPriceChange` and the harness's `netSinceChange` feature differenced the first and last sample since the last price change — valid only when no deadline falls between them, which was true for **11%** of players.
> 
> Fixing it helped the *baseline* far more than the model (K=40 recall 13.4% → 23.0%, K=80 27.0% → 42.0%), because the naive top-N-by-net-transfers rule **is** that feature. The apparent win was a broken input handicapping the thing the model was being compared against.
> 
> Worth keeping: when a fitted model beats a naive baseline that shares its inputs, check the inputs before believing the model.
> 
> ### How it surfaced
> 
> The owner asked why Cole Palmer read "expected to fall tonight, −321%" for three days without falling. His anchor was 2026-09-10, two deadlines back. First post-anchor sample **+446,406** (a gameweek of heavy buying), latest **−41,347** (GW6's fresh counter) — the difference is almost entirely GW4's inflow being subtracted. Summed correctly: **+364,022, a net inflow.** The sign was inverted, and 89% of players were affected.
> 
> Fixed without needing deadline data: the in-counter only increases within a gameweek, so a decrease between consecutive samples is a reset. One disclosed approximation remains — transfers between the last pre-reset sample and the deadline are lost, because the counter zeroes before the next sample sees them.
> 
> This was a Sprint 29 bug that Sprint 38's paging fix *exposed*. Before, the reading returned mostly nulls and nobody saw it; after, it returned confident nonsense, which is worse.
> 
> ### The other half of the Palmer question — and the next piece of work
> 
> Even corrected, heavily-owned players read far past 100% without moving, because **the threshold is not flat — it scales with ownership.** Measured at every real price change this season (reset-aware net since the previous change, vs ownership at that moment):
> 
> | direction | ownership | n | median net at the change |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | fall | 0–9.6% | 316 | 7,490 |
> | fall | 10–18% | 27 | 203,646 |
> | fall | 20–28% | 8 | 482,554 |
> | fall | 38.9% | 1 | 845,253 |
> | rise | 3–8% | 23 | 267,071 |
> | rise | 10–20% | 21 | 348,662 |
> | rise | 21–24% | 5 | 552,743 |
> | rise | 31–36% | 3 | 882,901 |
> 
> Roughly linear at **~22,000 net transfers per 1% owned**, with rises carrying a ~150k floor on top. The shipped flat `DEFAULT_FALL_THRESHOLD = 150_000` is off by ~5.6× for a 39%-owned player.
> 
> **Deliberately not shipped.** Switching the default to a fitted ownership curve is the move CLAUDE.md reserves, and this fit has not been gated walk-forward. Recorded as measurement; the threshold stays the documented user-set input. **Gating it should come before any further classifier work** — the classifier is trying to predict an event whose trigger we are currently mis-specifying.
> 
> Evidence and method: `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §2a, §2b, §3.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## Step 3, falls half — gate run 2026-09-21. Passes at tight budgets.
> 
> Harness: `scripts/price-falls-walkforward.ts`. Walk-forward over 36 scored nights, 631 candidates a night — for night *t*, train only on nights before *t*. Both model and baseline pick K players; scored on how many of that night's actual falls each caught, compared with a **paired per-night sign test** so one freak night cannot carry the result.
> 
> Falls only, as the 2026-09-20 read concluded: 352 recorded falls against 60 rises, and 55–60 positives leaves ~25–30 in a test fold — unresolvable, not negative. Rises stay held to ~GW10–12.
> 
> | K | model recall | baseline | random | nights won/lost | sign-test p |
> |---|---|---|---|---|---|
> | 10 | 6.0% | 3.1% | 1.6% | 7/0 | 0.0156 |
> | 20 | 10.2% | 6.8% | 3.2% | 9/0 | **0.0039** |
> | 40 | 15.3% | 13.4% | 6.3% | 6/2 | 0.2891 |
> | 80 | 26.7% | 27.0% | 12.7% | 9/8 | 1.0000 |
> 
> **The model beats the baseline where the budget is tight and ties once it is loose**, and the two converge monotonically as K grows — the shape a better *ranking* produces, not scattered wins. At K=10 and K=20 it never lost a night. Four budgets were tested, so the script applies a Bonferroni correction and reports which survives it: **K=20 does, K=10 does not.**
> 
> Logistic regression on five features (net transfers since last change, net over 24h, ownership, cumulative cost change, days since last change), standardised on each training fold's own statistics — standardising on the full sample would leak the test fold's distribution.
> 
> ### What this does not claim
> 
> - **Absolute recall is low at every budget.** Most falls are missed because most players are off the ~2h watchlist and their features are stale. The model is better than the baseline without either being good.
> - **Nothing is wired into the app.** That needs somewhere to keep and refresh weights plus a nightly job — its own piece of work. The shipped reading stays the descriptive heuristic (`priceProgress`).
> 
> ### Also shipped this sprint (step 2.5, no fitting, outside this gate)
> 
> `progressRaw` (signed, unclamped, so a player past the threshold reads `+111%` rather than pinning at 100%), a verdict ladder, and `projectToCutoffs` — which carries the trailing 24h transfer rate to the next nightly cutoffs. The cutoff hour is **measured**: all 412 recorded changes this season landed in the 23:00 UTC hour.
> 
> ### Two bugs found
> 
> 1. **`loadPriceProgress` had been reading ~2% of its data since Sprint 29.** Unpaged, against the 1000-row cap, ordered ascending — it got the *oldest* 1000 of ~42,000 post-anchor samples, nearly all discarded by the anchor filter. The price column read "unknown" or a flat 0% for almost everyone, which looked quiet rather than broken. Now paged concurrently.
> 2. **`cost_change_event` is FPL's cumulative change for the gameweek, not a per-night delta.** Labelling a fall by its sign marks a player already down on the week as falling every night. Direction now comes from consecutive prices.
> 
> Full write-up: `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §2–3.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-20
>
> ## Feasibility re-checked against live data, 2026-09-20 — startable, and the baseline is now measured
> 
> Checked while running the DSI-50 GW5 check-in. **Status unchanged (still Backlog, still M7)** — this is evidence for the gate, not a decision to build.
> 
> ### The history has accumulated
> 
> `player_price_history` (change-on-write) now covers **26 nights**: **55 rises, 226 falls**. `player_ownership_history` holds 65,095 rows across 667 players over 49 days, ~3.5 samples/player/day.
> 
> So the blocker this issue was parked on — "not enough watchlist history" — has cleared for falls and is marginal for rises.
> 
> ### The naive baseline this issue gates against is now measured
> 
> Ranking every player each night by their 24h net-transfer delta (the "top-N-by-net-transfers" baseline named in the description):
> 
> | | recall | mean rank of a real changer (of ~660) |
> |---|---|---|
> | Rises, top 5 | 19/55 (35%) | — |
> | Rises, top 15 | **39/55 (71%)** | 88 |
> | Falls, bottom 15 | 23/226 (10%) | — |
> | Falls, bottom 40 | 46/226 (20%) | 171 |
> 
> Random would put a changer at rank ~330. So the raw signal is genuinely strong for rises and weak for falls.
> 
> ### A null result on the obvious improvement
> 
> FPL's threshold is known to scale with ownership, so the natural first feature is net-transfer delta **normalised by `selected_by_percent`**. Measured:
> 
> | | raw delta | ownership-normalised |
> |---|---|---|
> | Rises in top 15 | 39/55 | **15/55** |
> | Falls in bottom 40 | 46/226 | 59/226 |
> | Mean rank, riser | 88 | 106 |
> | Mean rank, faller | 171 | 150 |
> 
> Normalising **makes rises substantially worse** and barely moves falls. Recording it here so it is not re-derived as a good idea later.
> 
> ### Where this leaves the gate
> 
> - **Falls are the side with headroom** — 226 labelled events and a baseline that only catches 20% at N=40. A fall classifier is fittable now.
> - **Rises are still too thin to gate honestly.** 55 positives, walk-forward, leaves ~25–30 test positives; differences under roughly 15pp in precision/recall would not be distinguishable from noise. Per this issue's own bar ("if it does not beat the baseline, ship the heuristic and say so"), a run at n=55 would most likely produce an unresolvable verdict rather than a negative one — which is the wrong kind of null.
> 
> **Suggested shape when this comes up at M7:** run the walk-forward on falls, and hold rises until ~GW10–12 doubles the positive count. Do not narrow the gate to let rises through early.
> 
> No code written. Queries were ad hoc `execute_sql`, nothing committed.
> 
> See DSI-50 for the same session's model-accuracy findings.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-55</b> — Confirm outside-collaborator fork-PR workflow-approval setting</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-24 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement

**Rescoped 2026-09-21** — the repo is already public (`gh repo view` → `isPrivate: false`) and of the "two post-flip hardening steps," secret scanning + push protection are already enabled (`security_and_analysis` on the repo). The `main` ruleset and `.github/CODEOWNERS` were applied pre-flip, 2026-09-03.

The only remaining piece from the original branch-protection runbook ([sprints/sprint-31.md](<../../docs/sprints/sprint-31.md>)) is whether GitHub Actions is set to require approval for workflow runs from outside-collaborator fork PRs — a Settings → Actions UI toggle with no git-history footprint, unverifiable read-only via `gh api`. This was blocked while the repo was private (fork-PR approval settings don't apply to private repos); it's unblocked now.

**Action:** a single owner check in Settings → Actions → "Fork pull request workflows from outside collaborators" — confirm it requires approval before running (protects `secrets.NEXT_PUBLIC_*` from an untrusted fork PR). Not sprint work.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-24
>
> Verified 2026-09-24. It turns out this *is* readable via `gh api`; the "unverifiable read-only" note in the description is out of date:
> 
> ```
> gh api repos/Dsinha97/fpl-app/actions/permissions/fork-pr-contributor-approval
> {"approval_policy":"all_external_contributors"}
> ```
> 
> `all_external_contributors` is the strictest setting: every fork PR from someone outside the repo waits for approval before any workflow runs. `ci.yml` also triggers only on `pull_request` (never `pull_request_target`), so a fork PR's workflow wouldn't receive repo secrets even after approval. Nothing to change. Closing.

<details><summary><b>DSI-59</b> — Sprint 32 leftovers — verify_jwt posture, scratch-path-test, the 429 itself</summary>

Three things Sprint 32 left open **deliberately**, recorded so they are decisions rather than oversights:

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-14
>
> **Status check 2026-09-14.** Two of the three are closed; one remains, and that is why this issue stays open.
> 
> ### 59a — the `verify_jwt` posture. **Done, and verified live.**
> 
> Deployed 2026-09-14 00:00:47 UTC via `npx.cmd supabase functions deploy sync-news generate-predictions --project-ref fyxyqxpscmqjyjxsyhms`.
> 
> | Function | verify_jwt | Version |
> |---|---|---|
> | `sync-news` | false → **true** | 4 → 5 |
> | `generate-predictions` | false → **true** | 16 → 17 |
> 
> `config.toml` and the live project now agree, so the repo has stopped asserting a posture the project did not have — which was the actual defect here, not the flag itself.
> 
> **The cron path was verified rather than assumed**, because the flip moves rejection to the gateway and that is the thing that could have broken:
> 
> - `sync-news` ran post-flip at 00:20:09, **success**, 6.7s, `sync_runs` row written.
> - `generate-predictions`' first post-flip run (00:05) came back **401** — and it is *not* the flip. The 401s carry `execution_time_ms` of 6295–7891, meaning the function booted and ran for 6–8 seconds before refusing; a gateway rejection returns in ~0ms and never starts the function. That is positive proof `verify_jwt = true` accepts the cron caller. Filed separately as DSI-142; three of those 401s predate the deploy.
> 
> Two notes worth keeping:
> 
> - The CLI was never installed on this machine — only `@supabase/supabase-js`, which is the client library. `npx.cmd supabase` needs no install (v2.117.0). The `.cmd` matters: PowerShell's execution policy blocks the `npx.ps1` shim.
> - The deploy also re-uploaded both functions' **source** from the repo (version bump plus a changed bundle hash), not just the flag — so it reconciled the §3 comment divergence in passing, and `generate-predictions` is now running repo source.
> 
> ### 59b — `scratch-path-test`. **Done** (2026-09-10). Confirmed again today: it is not in the project's function list at all.
> 
> ### 59c — the 429 render. **Still open.** This is the only thing keeping this issue alive.
> 
> The counting half is proved in SQL (threshold flips at exactly 30, window and per-function scoping correct). What is unproven is that the refusal *renders* as a 429 carrying `rateLimitMessage` in the browser. The cheap path is unchanged: `max_calls` is a `game_settings` row rather than a constant, so temporarily setting it to 2 makes it three clicks instead of thirty-one — then restore the row.
> 
> Also still true, and still by design: `checkRateLimit` **allows** the call when its own count query errors. The limit bounds cost; it does not guard data.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-10
>
> Left open on purpose while the rest of Sprint 36 closes — two of the three parts are settled, one is not, and closing this would claim the third.
> 
> **Settled:**
> - `scratch-path-test` — deleted 2026-09-10, endpoint 404s.
> - The 429's *counting* half — proved in SQL against synthetic rows: allowed at 29 used, refused at exactly 30, 50 rows outside the window still counted 30, a different function counted 0.
> 
> **Not settled:**
> - **The `verify_jwt` flip is prepared, not applied.** `config.toml` now says `true` for `sync-news` and `generate-predictions` (PR #17), but `verify_jwt` is applied by `supabase functions deploy`, so the repo currently asserts a posture the project does not have. That is deliberate and documented rather than silent — the deploy is bundled with DSI-55, since the invocation cost the flip saves only becomes real once the repo is public and the endpoints get enumerated.
> - **The 429's HTTP render** is still unproven. Cheap when someone wants it: `max_calls` is a `game_settings` row, not a constant, so setting it to 2 makes the proof three clicks instead of thirty-one.
> 
> So this issue is now a two-item residue, both waiting on DSI-55 or a spare five minutes. Closing it today would mean the next reader believes the flip is live.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-10
>
> Measured 2026-09-10 (Sprint 36 phase 4). Two of the three turned out differently from what the note implied.
> 
> **verify_jwt posture — buys no access control.** Probed live: with `verify_jwt = true` and no auth header the gateway 401s and the function never runs; with `false` the function runs and cron-auth 401s; and with the publishable key — which is public by design — both settings reach the same cron-secret check. So the only real difference is that `false` costs an invocation before rejecting. The flip is cost and consistency, not hardening. Doing it via a CLI deploy rather than an inline re-upload of two cron functions.
> 
> **scratch-path-test — still live.** ACTIVE v1, `verify_jwt = false`, returns `200` / `hi` to a completely unauthenticated request. Leaks nothing; it is an open endpoint on the bill that nothing calls. Owner-only delete.
> 
> **The 429 — counting half now proved.** `checkRateLimit` is a plain count over `sync_runs`, so it was testable in SQL against synthetic rows in a rolled-back transaction: allowed at 29 used, refused at exactly 30, 50 rows outside the window still counted 30, and a different function counted 0. Only the HTTP *render* is unproven, and the cheap route stands — `max_calls` is a `game_settings` row, so setting it to 2 makes the proof three clicks.
> 
> Also re-confirmed: `checkRateLimit` allows the call when its own count query errors. Best-effort by design, never to be described as a hard guarantee.
> 
> Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-36.md` §4

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-66</b> — Sprint 17 (part 1) — historical decision analytics (buildable now)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Feature

The half of Sprint 17 that needs no model trust: **descriptive analytics over decisions already made**, scored against results already in the database.

* Captain success — how often the armband beat the best available starter.
* Transfer success — what each transfer actually returned against the player sold, over the horizon it was made for.
* Chip ROI — what each played chip actually earned against a no-chip counterfactual.
* Rank progression — the season arc, against `manager_season_history`'s percentile convention (`rank_percentage` is FPL's own "top X%", lower is better; `lib/manager-profile.ts` flips it — don't mix the two on one screen).

**No new model, no new gate.** These are the same shape as `/review` (`lib/gameweek-review.ts`), which already reconstructs a finished gameweek from `lib/manager-picks.ts` + `lib/gameweek-state.ts`; reuse those rather than writing a second scorer. Report each in named terms rather than netted, per the project's standing rule.

**Deliberately excluded — see the gated half:** xP accuracy claims, recommendation accuracy, and the gradient-boosted minutes/injury models. Those depend on `positionCalibration` being trustworthy, which it is not until the GW10 refit.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (Sprints 15–17), `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`

</details>
