<details><summary><b>DSI-73</b> — Sprint 17 (part 2) — predictive models (split: DSI-185 minutes model, DSI-186 accuracy claims)</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Low · **Labels:** Model, Feature · **Relations:** related DSI-50

> **Restated 2026-09-24.** The gate below pointed at [DSI-53](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53/gw10-refit-positioncalibration-if-the-gw5-sign-held) (refit `positionCalibration`), which was canceled on 2026-09-21: the GW5 check-in showed the error is an expected-minutes discrimination failure that no multiplicative refit can reach. The two halves of this issue have different gates, so they're now separate sub-issues:
>
> * [DSI-185](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-185/expected-minutes-model-fix-the-appearedno-show-discrimination-failure)**: expected-minutes model** (the gradient-boosted minutes/injury model). Buildable now; faces the standing walk-forward gate against the last-5 baseline, per cohort plus start Brier.
> * [DSI-186](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-186/accuracy-claims-only-once-the-model-beats-the-last-5-baseline-out-of)**: accuracy claims.** Blocked by [DSI-185](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-185/expected-minutes-model-fix-the-appearedno-show-discrimination-failure), and must beat the last-5 baseline out-of-sample on MAE and r, pooled and per cohort, at the GW10 batch at the earliest.
>
> [DSI-50](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-50/gw5-check-in-bias-sign-read-at-n4-re-read-at-locked-data-next-read-gw6)'s bias-sign read is explicitly **not** a gate for either: the pooled bias is \~0 because the cohorts cancel. The original text is kept below for the record.

---

The half of Sprint 17 that **cannot start yet**, split out of [DSI-66](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-66/sprint-17-part-1-historical-decision-analytics-buildable-now) so that issue's High priority doesn't imply this is open.

* xP accuracy claims and recommendation accuracy — beyond the descriptive scoreboard already shipped in Sprint 34 (`lib/prediction-accuracy.ts`, `/settings` → Pipeline). Reporting a residual is fine today; *claiming the model is accurate* is not.
* Gradient-boosted minutes and injury models.

**The gate:** `positionCalibration` is fitted **in-sample** (`GKP 1.265 / DEF 1.2241 / MID 1.2238 / FWD 1.2576`, Sprint 12.6), and Sprint 17a found the model underperforms a naive last-5-gameweeks baseline out-of-sample in every season tested. Any accuracy claim built on top of that inherits the problem. The refit is [DSI-53](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53/gw10-refit-positioncalibration-if-the-gw5-sign-held), which cannot run before GW10 is scored (\~2026-11-09) — three gameweeks would bake this season's noise into a permanent constant.

New models here face the same standing gate everything else does: walk-forward, against a named naive baseline, reported the same way whether or not they pass. Do not narrow it.

Docs: `docs/roadmap.md` (Sprints 15–17), `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`, `docs/phase-4-model.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-74</b> — Sprint 36 — historical decision analytics, a two-way Telegram bot, and the Todo sweep</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Feature

Clears the whole Todo column — six issues, four phases, each phase a stopping point.

**Phase 1 ·** [DSI-66](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-66/sprint-17-part-1-historical-decision-analytics-buildable-now) — historical decision analytics. New `lib/decision-analytics.ts`: captain success, transfer success, chip ROI, rank progression, on `/team`. No migration; `manager_gameweek_history`, `manager_transfers` and `manager_chips` are already populated (the last has no reader today). The single-event captain computation moves out of `lib/gameweek-review.ts` rather than being reimplemented.

**Phase 2 ·** [DSI-61](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-61/rivals-auto-populated-from-a-leagues-standings) — rivals auto-populated from a league's standings. `loadLeagueStandings` → the existing `/team` rival pipeline. Bounded batch, sequential, respecting Sprint 32's per-user rate limit on `sync-manager`.

**Phase 3 ·** [DSI-65](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-65/sprint-16-notifications-and-automation) — Telegram, both directions, per the issue's own comment. 3a: a cron-only `notify` function over `change_feed`/`news_items`/`fixture_changes`/price thresholds/deadline, with a `notification_outbox` whose composed dedupe key is the feature. 3b: a `telegram-webhook` function answering `/team`, `/points`, `/fixtures`, `/leagues` — reads of already-computed rows only, never a second implementation of a modelled number. Linking via a one-time code, surfaced as a button on `/team` and in `/settings`.

**Phase 4 ·** [DSI-60](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-60/top-1k-ownership-sample-sync-league-314-for-real-and-keep-it)**,** [DSI-58](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-58/custom-smtp-for-supabase-auth)**,** [DSI-59](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-59/sprint-32-leftovers-verify-jwt-posture-scratch-path-test-the-429) — console and decision items. Sample league 314 and keep it; custom SMTP (unrelated to the Telegram work); Sprint 32's three deliberate gaps, including a cheap proof of the 429 now that the limit is known to be a `game_settings` input rather than a constant.

Also in this sprint: a `/start-sprint` skill that does this reading-and-shaping step on demand, paired with the existing `/linear-sync`.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-36.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-75</b> — One CLI functions deploy reconciles four separate drifts</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Relations:** related DSI-59

Four unrelated things have accumulated that a single `supabase functions deploy` from a linked CLI would settle. None is urgent on its own; together they are one command.

```
npx supabase login
npx supabase link --project-ref fyxyqxpscmqjyjxsyhms
npx supabase functions deploy sync-news generate-predictions notify telegram-webhook sync-manager
```

**1. The** `verify_jwt` **flip is claimed but not applied.** `supabase/config.toml` says `true` for `sync-news` and `generate-predictions` (PR [Dsinha97/fpl-app#17](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/dsi-58-done-custom-smtp-and-prep-the-dsi-59a-verify-jwt-flip-96df4043ae20)), but `verify_jwt` is applied by the deploy — so the repo currently asserts a posture the project does not have. That is the exact failure the file was written to prevent, and it is deliberate and documented rather than silent. See [DSI-59](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-59/sprint-32-leftovers-verify-jwt-posture-scratch-path-test-the-429).

**2.** `sync-manager` **has not picked up** `last_success_at`**.** It shares `_shared/manager-sync.ts` with `sync-claimed-managers`, which was redeployed 2026-09-10; `sync-manager` was not. So a browser-triggered sync does not stamp `last_success_at`. It degrades safely — the cron re-syncs and stamps within 24h — but the two callers of one shared module are running different versions of it.

**3.** `notify` **and** `telegram-webhook` **bundles differ from the repo.** Both were deployed through the Supabase integration, which required their `_shared/` dependencies inlined by hand; those copies carry trimmed comments, and `telegram-webhook`'s bundled `sync.ts` holds only the three helpers it uses. Behaviour is identical and the repo is the source of truth, but deployed source is not byte-identical to it.

**4.** `sync-claimed-managers` **was deployed the same way** and carries the same divergence.

**Why not now.** The only *cost* argument in the set is item 1, and it is near-zero while the repo is private and nobody knows the function URLs. It becomes real the moment the repo goes public and the endpoints get enumerated mechanically — so this belongs with [DSI-55](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-55/flip-the-repo-public-then-apply-the-two-post-flip-hardening-steps), which is when it starts paying for itself.

**Verify afterwards** against `sync_runs`: fresh `success` rows for `sync-news` (every 20 min) and `generate-predictions` before walking away. That is Sprint 32's rule and it is what catches a deploy silently 401ing every scheduled run.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-36.md` §3–4

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-76</b> — Gateway Timeouts cluster on cron boundaries — test whether staggering helps</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Low · **Labels:** Improvement

**A hypothesis with six data points, recorded so it is not re-derived from scratch.** Nothing here needs fixing today.

The sync-health panel (`/settings` → Pipeline, shipped 2026-09-10) surfaced `Gateway Timeout` failures across **six different functions in 24h**, on six different tables:

| Function | Runs / 24h | Timeouts | Table |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| `sync-live-gameweek` | 702 | 1 | `gameweeks` |
| `sync-claimed-managers` | 712 | 1 | `gameweeks` |
| `sync-news` | 71 | 1 | `gameweeks` |
| `sync-bootstrap` | 48 | 1 | `teams` |
| `notify` | 13 | 1 | `user_notification_prefs` |
| `sync-manager` | 5 | 1 | `manager_gameweek_history` |

So it is **project-wide infrastructure noise at roughly 0.4% of runs**, not a bug in any one function. That is already useful to have written down — an earlier note in `sprint-36.md` guessed the `notify` one might be `notify`-specific, and it is not.

**The pattern worth testing.** They are spread across the day rather than clustered in one incident (16:00:02, 18:00:01, 19:15:02, 19:59:59, 20:10:01, 20:40:01) — but **five of the six landed within two seconds of a clock boundary**. Several crons fire on the same boundaries, so the plausible mechanism is contention at the top of each interval rather than random flakiness.

**If it is worth chasing**, the test is cheap: stagger the cron schedules by a minute or two each (`*/2 * * * *` → offsets) and see whether the rate drops. The counter-evidence to look for first is timeouts landing *away* from boundaries as the sample grows — that would kill the hypothesis.

**Why this is low priority.** Every one of these is self-correcting: the next scheduled run picks the work up. And since `managers.last_success_at` landed (2026-09-10), a manager sync that dies on its first query no longer marks itself fresh, so a timeout can no longer compound into 24h of silent staleness. The infrastructure noise was always there; what changed is that it stopped mattering.

Watch it on `/settings` → Pipeline → Sync health. If the rate climbs materially, it is a Supabase-side signal rather than a code one.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-36.md` §2

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-77</b> — Two dead eslint-disable directives, and a lint check that hid an error</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-10 · **Completed:** 2026-09-24 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Low · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-184

Small, but the second half is the part that matters.

**Two** `eslint-disable` **directives now guard rules that no longer fire:**

* `app/leagues/page.tsx:61` — `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`
* `app/team/page.tsx:978` — `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps`

Both surface as `warning Unused eslint-disable directive`. Harmless, but a stale suppression is a claim that something needs suppressing, and the next person to touch those effects will believe it.

Also worth a look while in there: `app/team/page.tsx:220` has an unused `card` variable.

**The check that hid a real error.** On 2026-09-10 a lint error reached CI because the local check was `npm run lint 2>&1 | tail -2`, and eslint's *last* line is:

```
0 errors and 2 warnings potentially fixable with the `--fix` option
```

That counts **fixable** problems, not errors. The real summary — `✖ 168 problems (1 error, 167 warnings)` — is the line above it, and was never read. The two lines are easy to confuse and the wrong one is the more reassuring.

Worth considering: `npm run lint` currently exits non-zero on error (CI caught it correctly), so the fix is only ever about how a human or agent *reads* the output. Either grep the `✖` summary line, or add `--max-warnings` / a `lint:ci` script whose output has one unambiguous verdict line. The `/ship-check` skill should say which.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-36.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-118</b> — Design Audit - Landing Page</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-12 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

### 1. Landing Page (`/` signed-out)

#### Visual Hierarchy & First Impressions

* **Visual Anchor:** The high-contrast badge and the neon green `#00FF87` on "DECISION" draw the eye first. The green CTA button pulls immediate follow-up focus.
* **Secondary CTA Visibility:** The "Explore players" button has an almost invisible border against the dark background. Give it a distinct 1px border like `#4A2060` or `rgba(255,255,255,0.15)` with `rgba(255,255,255,0.85)` text so it functions as a recognizable secondary action.
* **Empty Viewport Problem:** A visitor without an account sees only a single value proposition sentence and two buttons. There is no proof of what the tool looks like inside.
  * Add a realistic preview: a mockup or cropped interface snapshot of the pitch view, solver output, or live gameweek feed directly beneath the CTA row.
  * Show, rather than tell, what "decided with data, not vibes" means. An actual model card showing an expected points delta (e.g., `Haaland (C) 6.1 xP vs Salah 5.8 xP`) establishes credibility immediately.

#### Copy & Credibility

* **Onboarding Friction:** The helper note *"You'll just need the numeric Manager ID from your team's URL on fantasy.premierleague.com"* is good, but many users do not know where to find that ID.
  * Add a modal or tooltip: `"Where do I find my ID?"` showing the exact URL string: `[fantasy.premierleague.com/entry/](https://fantasy.premierleague.com/entry/){ID}/event/...`.
* **Subtle Trust Signals:** "Data pipeline: connected" is great for engineers, but FPL managers care about freshness. Change this to: `"Synced with FPL API: GW4 active"` or display the exact timestamp of the last player data pull.

### Key Action Items

| **Area** | **Current Issue** | **Recommended Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Landing Page** | High bounce risk; no visual product preview. | Embed a responsive preview card of the squad pitch and solver recommendation above the fold. |
| **Landing Secondary CTA** | Low contrast ghost button blends into purple background. | Give "Explore players" a 1px `rgba(255,255,255,0.2)` border and lighter text. |

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-119</b> — Design Audit - Deadline Hub</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

### 2. Deadline Hub (`/deadline` upcoming view)

#### Information Density & Hierarchy

* **The Pitch Container:** The dark purple background on the pitch graphic blends into the container frame. Adding a subtle inner stroke or a very faint grass pitch outline gives the pitch graphic grounding without relying on bright greens that fight your `#00FF87` accent.
* **Player Cards on Pitch:**
  * You have two color badges per card: purple for name, yellow/green for fixture difficulty.
  * The small number under the name (e.g., `2.6`, `3.3`, `6.1`) is the expected points (xP), but it lacks an inline label. Add a tiny `xP` label next to or above the number. A new user might confuse `3.3` with cost, ownership, or form.
  * The captain indicators (`(C)` and `(VC)`) need clear visual pop. The small circle on B.Fernandes blends into the kit sleeves. Use a bright solid circle with high contrast text (e.g., gold background with black text) so the captain choice is unmistakable at a glance.

#### Right-Rail Decision Cards

* **Squad Readiness & Availability:**
  * When there are no flags, these two top cards ("This squad is legal...", "Nothing flagged...") take up roughly 140px of vertical height with passive green text.
  * Collapse these into a single compact alert strip above the pitch: `✓ Squad legal & 15/15 available`. This frees up above-the-fold real estate on the right rail for the core decision drivers: Captaincy and Transfers.
* **Captain & Starting XI Card:**
  * This is the best analytical section on the screen. However, the amber comparison block (`Your draft has Haaland captained (5.6 xP) vs B.Fernandes (6.1 xP) = +0.5`) gets pushed down.
  * Move that action comparison directly below the "Recommended captain" headline. Give it a distinct accent border so the user immediately sees the projected net gain.

### 3. Strategy / Action Controls (Plans & News Tabs)

#### Transfer Planning Flow

* **The "Plan the path" Trigger:**
  * The Horizon selector (`1 GW`, `3 GW`, `5 GW`...) is clear, but the "Plan the path" button sits isolated at the bottom right of an otherwise empty container.
  * Make this a unified action bar:
    * Left side: `Horizon [ 1GW | 3GW | 5GW | 8GW ]`
    * Middle: `Free transfers: [ 0 ]`
    * Right side: Primary CTA button `[ Plan Path → ]`
  * "Value of waiting for news: 1": This input lacks context. Add a hover tooltip or brief microcopy explaining whether `1` represents an xP penalty, a rank risk weight, or days before the deadline.

#### Chip Call Clarity

* The metrics on Bench Boost (`+5.0`) and Triple Captain (`+5.6`) use green text. While green indicates positive gain, an xP gain of 5.6 on a Triple Captain is generally below the season optimal threshold (where a Double Gameweek TC can yield +12 to +18 xP).
* Differentiate between an **absolute positive number** and a **recommended trigger**:
  * If the engine says "Do not play chip this week," style the chip as neutral or warning (e.g., `Bench Boost: +5.0 xP (Hold for DGW)`), rather than highlighting the `+5.0` in the same primary green you use for actionable calls.

#### News & Price Watch

* **Card Scannability:** The two-column split (`Price & News Watch` vs `Team News`) is structured well.
* **Visual Categorization in Price Watch:**
  * Price rises and drops are currently grouped together using purple pill icons with subtle directional arrows (`Calafiori £5.7m → £5.8m` vs `E.Le Fée £6.0m → £5.9m`).
  * Price changes are time-critical for team value. Use distinct status indicators: a clear green up-arrow/text for rises, and a red down-arrow/text for drops to make price trends readable in half a second.

### 4. Live Gameweek Mode (`Live — GW3`)

#### Score Display & BPS Race

* **Hero Score:** The big green `43` is clean and immediately establishes context. The subtext (`Live points, starters + captain...`) gives good technical clarity.
* **Provisional Bonus Points (BPS):**
  * The list (`Haaland 40 bps · +3 bonus so far`) is well-formatted, but it lacks team badges or player positions.
  * Adding a small 16px club crest or position badge (`FWD`, `MID`) next to each player helps users track their assets against rival players in real time without cognitive drag.
* **Match Cards:**
  * Match status (`FT`) and scores (`0 - 2`) are well-balanced.
  * The event list beneath the score (`Isak (2)`, `Gakpo (2)`) aligns all scorers to the center-right, making it slightly ambiguous which team scored without tracing back to the club crest. Align home team scorers to the left column and away team scorers to the right column directly under their respective clubs.

### Key Action Items

| **Area** | **Current Issue** | **Recommended Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Deadline Status** | Two separate cards for Squad Readiness and Availability waste vertical space. | Merge into a single compact horizontal pill banner above the pitch. |
| **Pitch xP Numbers** | Raw floats (`3.3`, `6.1`) have no explicit metric label. | Add small uppercase `xP` subtext directly beneath or beside the values. |
| **Price Changes** | Rises and drops share the same purple badge style. | Use green up indicators for price rises and red down indicators for price drops. |
| **Live Match Cards** | Scorer events are not cleanly mapped to home/away sides. | Left-align home events under the home club, right-align away events under the away club. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Done** — commits `5120260`, `d0f7bb0` on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-deadline`.
> 
> | Finding | Outcome |
> |---|---|
> | Two all-clear cards waste ~140px | One strip above the pitch; the cards render only when they have something to say |
> | Captain armband blends into the kit | **7.80:1 → 15.03:1** measured; vice 12.16 → 16.12 |
> | Pitch xP number unlabelled | Now `6.1 xP`, unit muted |
> | Amber comparison pushed down | Moved under the recommendation, accent border, and it now states `= +0.5 xP` |
> | Price rises/drops share a badge | Done in Sprint B via `FeedRowItem` — green ▲ / red ▼ with signed amounts |
> | Scorers not mapped to home/away | Club codes head each column, divider between |
> | "Value of waiting for news" lacks context | Unit surfaced: it is **expected points** |
> 
> ### Chip Call Clarity — not implemented as written
> 
> Flagged before the work started, resolving as promised.
> 
> The ask was to style a positive-but-not-worth-playing chip as `(Hold for DGW)`, because a double-gameweek Triple Captain "yields +12 to +18 xP". **That range is measured nowhere in this repo.**
> 
> I then checked what the page can actually support: `ChipValuation` (`lib/chip-plan.ts:64`) carries `gain` for *one* gameweek, `terms`, `explanation` and `blocked` — and **no signal about whether this is a good week to spend the chip**. `/deadline` calls `benchBoostAt`/`tripleCaptainAt` for the next gameweek only; the season-wide ranking lives in the chip engine, which this page deliberately does not run.
> 
> My earlier note proposed comparing against the best available gameweek from that ranking. Running the full engine here is exactly the work CLAUDE.md records as freezing the tab, and it is a computation change inside a presentation pass — so instead:
> 
> **The page stops implying a recommendation it cannot support.** The figure renders as a measurement in foreground (it had no unit at all before — a bare `+5.0`, itself a "say what the number means" violation), and the caption now reads *"These are this gameweek's values, not a recommendation to play — compare every gameweek to see whether this is the week."* linking to the ranking that is real evidence.
> 
> Same guidance the issue wanted, sourced from what the model knows rather than from a constant.
> 
> ### The "unified action bar" is partially done
> 
> Horizon and Free transfers sit on one row and the horizon is now a `SegmentedControl`. The `Plan path` CTA lives inside `components/transfer-path.tsx`, which `/transfers` also renders — hoisting it would change that page's layout too, so it is left for the `/transfers` pass (DSI-124) where both can move together.
> 
> ### Verification
> 
> Against a real 15-player squad built and saved in the preview browser: strip reads "Squad legal · 15/15 available", both passive cards suppressed, chip figure computes to foreground rather than emerald and reads `+14.1 xP`, scoreboard columns read `BOU | BRE` with home scorers left and away right, console clean, `tsc`/lint/build all pass.
> 
> Not verified: the live-gameweek BPS list (needs a match in progress) and the captain-comparison alert (the generated squad's captain already matches the recommendation, so `captainDiff` is null).

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-12
>
> **Flagging one item that will not be implemented as written, before the work starts.**
> 
> Under *Chip Call Clarity*, this issue asks to style a `+5.0 xP` Bench Boost as `(Hold for DGW)` on the grounds that a double-gameweek Triple Captain "can yield +12 to +18 xP".
> 
> That range is not measured anywhere in this repo, and CLAUDE.md is explicit: *an acceptance threshold you invented is not evidence*. Hardcoding it would put a number on screen that the model cannot defend — on the one screen whose whole job is to be defensible.
> 
> **The underlying complaint is valid and will be fixed.** A positive number rendered in primary green reads as "do this", whether or not the engine thinks so. That is exactly the accent-discipline defect [DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129) covers, and `components/ui/delta.tsx` now exists to stop it.
> 
> **The evidence-side version instead:** `components/chip-timing.tsx` already ranks every gameweek for each chip. So show this week's value *against the best available week from that existing ranking* — `Bench Boost: +5.0 xP · best available GW12 (+11.3)` — and let the comparison decide the tone. Same guidance, sourced from the model rather than from a constant, and it updates itself as the season does.
> 
> No change to `lib/chips.ts`; this is a rendering decision over output that already exists.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-120</b> — Design Audit - My Team</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`bd047d0`), driven signed-in against the new test account.
>
> **Real defect the audit half-saw:** it asked for a `pts` unit because `27` was ambiguous. The unit wasn't missing — it was *wrong*. `PlayerCard` hard-coded the suffix `xP`, so every played-gameweek card read “6 xP” above its own tooltip reading “GW4 points”. Fixed with `value_unit`. Same card also drew its own green/red venue ring, keeping the colourblind defect the FDR matrix had already moved off hue — now routed through `venueRing()`.
>
> **Corrected the audit's fix:** moving the Manager ID to `/settings` outright would strand signed-out visitors, who can't reach `/settings` behind its sign-in wall but *can* use `/team` off `localStorage`. It now lives in the onboarding card when there's no team, and behind “Change ID” once there is.
>
> **Verified false, left alone:** metric rail is already near-white, not all-accent; percentile bars already use `chart-1`; the rank arc already inverts its axis and says so in its aria-label (its *visible* caption said “lower is better” under a rising line — that part was fixed).
>
> **Declined:** moving the Telegram control to settings. It is already there; this copy is a deliberate duplicate, and the code comment explains that a linking control tucked into a settings tab is one nobody finds.
>
> Found while here, not in the audit: the signed-out view had a second “Connect your FPL team” empty state duplicating the card, and the rank arc stroked the CTA colour — the same complaint the audit made about the percentile bars, which had already been fixed.

---

### 1. The Not-Signed-In View (`/team`)

#### Layout & Hierarchy

* **Disconnected Input Bar:** The input field and the green `Connect` button sit high up on the left, while the explanatory heading `Connect your FPL team` and the URL snippet sit centered lower down. Because the eye follows a top-down reading flow, you see an orphaned input before you read why it exists.
* **Fix:** Center the entire card as a single unit in the viewport:
  1. Main title: `Connect your FPL team`
  2. Subtitle: `Enter your Manager ID from your points URL to sync your live squad.`
  3. Inline form: `[ Input: 274486 ] [ Connect → ]`
  4. Code snippet pill: `[fantasy.premierleague.com/entry/1234567/event/1](https://fantasy.premierleague.com/entry/1234567/event/1)`

### 2. Header Chrome & Top Bar (`/team` Signed-In)

#### Manager Identity Area

* **Persistent ID & Refresh Bar:** The `FPL Manager ID [ 274486 ] [ Refresh ]` controls sit above your squad name. You only change your Manager ID once, yet it commands high-priority real estate on every visit.
  * Move the Manager ID input into `/settings`.
  * Keep only a subtle icon button for `Refresh` (or an auto-sync indicator with the timestamp) next to the team name: `DS United (FPL) ⟳`.
* **Action Clutter:** The top-right row mixes several different concerns:
  * An action CTA: `Import as draft →`
  * A status indicator: `● Telegram linked`
  * A destructive/account action: `Unlink`
  * Separate account management (`Unlink`, `Telegram`) from squad analysis. Place the account actions in settings or a manager dropdown profile card. Keep `Import as draft` as a secondary ghost button right beside the Gameweek selector.

### 3. Squad View & Pitch Layout

#### Information Mapping on Pitch Cards

* **Fixtures vs Points Ambiguity:**
  * In the upcoming view (`/deadline`), the bottom pill shows fixture difficulty (`SUN(A)`, `CHE(H)`).
  * In this review view, the bottom pill shows the fixture difficulty, but a green pill with a number is placed on the left (`2`, `7`, `3`, `27`).
  * A new user has to guess whether `27` on Haaland is minutes, points, or xP. Add a small label above or inside: `27 pts`.
* **Captain Multiplier Representation:**
  * Haaland displays `27`. It is unclear at first glance if this is his raw score (9) multiplied by 3, or if 27 gets multiplied again.
  * Use clear math formatting inside the captain badge: `(C) 9 × 3 = 27 pts`.

#### Right-Hand Metric Rail

* **Tile Grid Structure:** The 2x3 grid (`Team Value`, `In the Bank`, `Overall Points`, `Overall Rank`, `GW3 Points`, `GW4 Deadline`) is readable.
* **Color Usage for Context:**
  * `£100.3m`, `214`, `52` are all bright green. When every metric is the same accent color, no single metric stands out.
  * Keep standard totals (`Team Value`, `Total Points`) in white text (`#FFFFFF`). Reserve `#00FF87` specifically for net-positive gameweek outcomes or green rank arrows.

### 4. Review & Gameweek Post-Mortem

#### Post-Mortem Cards

* **Overall Rank Delta:** In the `Gameweek 3` card, the rank movement `(▼ 117,103)` uses a clear red arrow and text. That is direct and readable.
* **Discrepancy Warning Styling:**
  * In the right card under `Transfers this season`, the warning is styled in bright yellow text: `"Your saved squad also changed by Konsa in / Hume out since the last save — check that against the ledger above if the two don't obviously match."`
  * Long raw text strings in bright yellow are difficult to read and look like an unhandled debug log.
  * Wrap it inside a formal warning banner: a dark amber background container (`rgba(245, 158, 11, 0.1)`), a warning icon (`⚠️`), a 1px border, and legible muted yellow text.

### 5. Decisions This Season & Career Profile

#### Decisions Cards

* **Captain Card:**
  * `"Best of your XI in 2 of 3 gameweeks (67%). Left on the table: 9 pts"`
  * The breakdown (`GW1: B.Fernandes (2 × 2) -9 vs João Pedro (11)`) is strong analytical feedback.
  * Tighten the typographic hierarchy: make `-9 pts` stand out in red, and `best call` in green pills instead of raw text.
* **Transfers Card:**
  * `"Over 5 GW: 5 in - 8 out - 0 hit = -3"`
  * The formula is clear to an FPL veteran, but the layout looks like plain terminal text. Format this as an itemized mini-table or stat bar:
    * Points gained by players brought in: `+5`
    * Points lost from players sold: `-8`
    * Transfer hits: `-0`
    * Net impact: `-3 pts` (highlighted in red)
* **Rank Progression Chart:**
  * The graph has no vertical axis labels, no grid lines, and only two endpoints (`GW1`, `GW3`).
  * In FPL, a rising line typically indicates rank gain (which means the rank number gets smaller). If the line goes up from 4.6M to 912K, explicitly add arrows or an axis label: `Top: 1 (Best) → Bottom: 10M` so users immediately understand the trajectory.

#### Manager Profile & Rivals

* **Percentile Bars:**
  * The bars for `Best — 2021/22`, `Median`, and `Worst — 2016/17` are informative.
  * The green bar color is identical to your primary interactive CTA button (`#00FF87`). Using your primary interactive color for static data visualizations dilutes the visual weight of your buttons. Use a distinct visualization green or purple-tinted cyan for chart elements.
* **Rivals Card:**
  * The `This season` vs `Career` toggle is clean.
  * The tabular alignment of `GW PTS`, `TOTAL`, `RANK`, and `GAP` is easy to scan.
  * The `+45`, `+18` gap column is styled in green. Ensure the sign is clear: if `+45` means you are leading by 45 points, green is correct. If it means you are 45 points behind the rival, it should be red.

### Summary Checklist for `/team`

| **Component** | **UX Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Not-Signed-In View** | Form controls and explainer text are split apart vertically. | Group into a single centered onboarding card. |
| **Top Bar Chrome** | Manager ID input and Unlink buttons take up daily analytical focus. | Move ID and account actions to Settings; keep only a quick refresh button. |
| **Pitch Review Numbers** | Points values (`2`, `7`, `27`) lack metric context. | Add a small `pts` unit label. Show `9 × 3 = 27 pts` for the captain. |
| **Warning Messages** | Yellow alert text renders as raw unstructured paragraphs. | Place inside a styled alert banner container with an icon. |
| **Transfer Net Ledger** | Formula strings (`5 in - 8 out = -3`) look like code output. | Structure into a clean 3-row metric card with red/green deltas. |
| **Rank Graph** | Line direction lacks an inverted axis indicator. | Add a `Higher is better` arrow or inverted axis ticks. |

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-121</b> — Design Audit - Builder</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

### 1. Header Controls & Projection Banner

#### Horizon and Save Bar

* **Redundant Draft Selectors:** The control bar displays `[ DS United (FPL) ˅ ]` and `[ DS United (FPL) ]` side by side right before the green `Save` button.
  * The first dropdown selects the draft; the second text field renames it.
  * Having two adjacent boxes with identical text looks like a duplicate UI bug. Combine them into an editable title field or an explicit `Draft: [ DS United ˅ ]` with a subtle rename icon (`✎`) beside it.
* **Button Style Competition:** The dropdown button `Save ▾` uses the primary neon green `#00FF87`. When `Save` uses the exact same neon green background as `Fill remaining` and `Apply GW4 XI`, all three compete for primary attention on one screen. Make `Save` a high-contrast ghost button with a 1px border, reserving the solid `#00FF87` fill for solver actions.

#### Projection Card (`299.0` vs `58.9`)

* **Layout Balance:** The numbers `299.0` (Projected 5 GWs) and `58.9` (Next GW) are clear, but the right half of the banner is mostly empty purple space with two small pills (`Haaland (C)` and `Szoboszlai (VC)`) floating high up.
* **Make Projections Actionable:**
  * Group the metrics horizontally: `5 GW Projection: 299.0 xP` | `Next GW: 58.9 xP` | `Captain: Haaland (+30.6 xP)`.
  * The link `Free Hit & Wildcard schedule →` looks like plain metadata. Style it as an interactive text link with a subtle border or an arrow hover effect.

### 2. Pitch Area & Budget Tracker

#### Budget Bar

* **High-Contrast Bar:** The bright green line spanning the width of the pitch shows `£99.7m of £100.0m` with `£0.4m left`.
  * The total bar is full green, but `£0.4m left` means the user has unspent money.
  * Use a proportional progress bar where spent budget (`£99.6m`) is filled and remaining bank (`£0.4m`) is a distinct unfilled dark track.
* **Redundant Status Pills:** Beneath the bar, you have `✓ Legal squad` and `15/15 · £0.4m left`. You already state `15/15 players` and `£0.4m left` 12px above it. Drop the duplicate text and keep just the `✓ Legal squad` badge.

#### Pitch Player Badges

* **Shirt Overcrowding:**
  * The kit graphic has multiple badges layered over it: position icon (`GK`), captain badges (`(C)`, `(VC)`), plus/minus status indicators, and substitution order numbers (`1`, `2`, `3` on bench).
  * For outfield players, these small circular badges clutter the kit graphic. Standardize the positions: captain indicator stays at the top-left of the shirt; action triggers (like swap or info) go below the fixture pill or trigger on player click.
* **Bench Usage Percentages:**
  * In the Bench box, you show `0% used`, `64% used`, `27% used`, `8% used` below the fixtures.
  * These numbers represent auto-sub probability, but without a label, users will confuse them with ownership or effective minutes played. Add a tiny prefix: `64% sub prob` or `64% auto-sub`.

### 3. Player Info Popover (`E.Le Fée`)

#### Information Hierarchy & Data Density

* **Modal Visual Anchor:** The large `4.2` expected points header stands out well.
* **Stat Grid Legibility:**
  * The 3x3 stat grid (`Price`, `Goals`, `Assists`, `Mins`, `Owned`, `Form`, etc.) shows em-dashes (`—`) for missing values (`Goals: —`, `Mins: —`).
  * Em-dashes clutter the card when multiple metrics are zero. If minutes or goals are 0, display `0` or omit empty fields to let actual metrics like `Form: 4.3` and `Start: 80%` stand out.
* **Action Missing on Card:** The popover gives statistics, but it lacks an immediate action. If a user opens a player on the pitch, they usually want to do one of two things: **Substitute** or **Transfer Out**. Add two clear bottom actions to this popover: `[ Swap with Bench ]` and `[ Find Replacement ]`.

### 4. Right-Hand Rail (Solver, Lineup, & Replacement)

#### Gameweek Lineup & Recommendations

* **"Apply GW4 XI & armband":** This action button sits right under the lineup header. Clicking it applies the model's suggested lineup to your pitch view.
  * Below it, you display a long bulleted list explaining why B.Fernandes is recommended (`Highest projected points`, `89% start probability`, `First-choice penalty taker`).
  * You also include internal model disclaimers: `Team attack strength is omitted — FPL publishes it as zero for every club...`
  * **UX Problem:** That disclaimer is technical backend jargon. Move technical weighting disclaimers into an informational tooltip (`ℹ️`) next to the section title. Keep the visible card focused solely on manager decisions: player name, projected xP, and confidence percentage.

#### Optimise Squad (The Knapsack Solver)

* **Strategy & Risk Dropdowns:** The two selectors (`Maximum points` and `Medium`) sit neatly above the two action buttons (`Fill remaining` and `Rebuild`).
* **Visual Hierarchy of Solver Actions:**
  * `Fill remaining` is bright green `#00FF87`.
  * `Rebuild` is a dark purple button with white text.
  * This is a good hierarchy: `Fill remaining` is the non-destructive standard action, while `Rebuild` resets the whole squad. Keep this contrast pattern.

#### Replacement Options Panel (`Replace E.Le Fée · 5 GW`)

* **Context Preservation:** When searching replacements, the panel lists candidate cards with rich analytical context:
  * `Anderson MCI · £6.3m`
  * `broadly equivalent`
  * `xP +0.3 · risk -2 · fit 0.4 · 99 exit routes`
* **Card Scannability:**
  * Each card places the `Swap` button on the far right, which makes scanning down the list quick.
  * The second line of text (`-2.4 xP — a downgrade`, `-3.8 xP — a downgrade · better fixtures · frees £0.5m`) is packed into dense single-string text.
  * Separate the tradeoffs into two distinct tags:
    * `xP Delta`: `+0.3 xP` (green) or `-2.4 xP` (red)
    * `Cash Delta`: `Frees £0.5m` (amber badge) or `Costs £0.4m`
  * This allows a manager to immediately find the specific replacement type they need (e.g., funding a second transfer vs maximizing points).

### Builder Action Checklist

| **Element** | **Usability Problem** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Draft Header** | Adjacent duplicate boxes `[DS United] [DS United]` look like a bug. | Combine into a single select dropdown with an edit icon for renaming. |
| **Model Disclaimers** | Raw algorithmic notes (`Team attack strength is omitted...`) take up decision space. | Tuck model disclaimers inside a small info tooltip (`ℹ️`). |
| **Player Popover** | Informational only; offers no direct action. | Add direct buttons: `[ Substitute ]` and `[ Replace ]` inside the popover. |
| **Bench Stats** | Raw numbers (`64% used`) lack context. | Change to `64% auto-sub` or `64% sub prob`. |
| **Replacement List** | Dense string lines (`-3.8 xP — a downgrade · better fixtures · frees £0.5m`). | Split into structured badges: `[-3.8 xP]` in red, `[+£0.5m]` in amber. |
| **Budget Bar** | Solid green bar does not visually show unspent cash. | Display filled track for spent money, empty dark track for remaining bank. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Done** — commits `704327e`, `6692217` on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-builder`.
> 
> | Finding | Outcome |
> |---|---|
> | Pitch reads as unactionable (owner note) | `+` is a filled button that morphs to `×`; eligible players open where you tapped — popover on desktop, bottom sheet on phone |
> | Filled slot | Same two surfaces; `PlayerDetail` gained an `inline` mode so the sheet owns the dialog role |
> | Duplicate draft selector + rename field | Renaming is a mode behind a pencil |
> | `Save` competes with the solver actions | `ActionMenu` gained `tone="outline"` |
> | `15/15 · £0.4m left` redundant | Removed; count shows only while illegal |
> | `64% used` ambiguous | `64% auto-sub`, plus an aria-label |
> | Replacement trade-offs dense | Lead with `Delta` (points) and `Badge` (cash) |
> | Model disclaimers in decision space | `CAPTAIN_MODEL_NOTE` moved to the heading (earlier commit) |
> 
> ### Budget bar — not changed, and here is why
> 
> The finding reads it as "full green despite £0.4m left". It is **already proportional**: `width: spent / totalSpend`. At £99.7m of £100.0m it is 99.7% filled, and it looks full because it *is* 99.7% full. No encoding makes 0.3% look like more than 0.3% without lying about the number. The bank is carried by the figure beside the bar, which states it exactly.
> 
> ### The morph does not become the panel
> 
> `References/Components/plus-to-menu.md` animates a 40px circle into a 183×172 panel. Two reasons that shape does not work here, resolving the open question from my earlier note:
> 
> 1. `components/pitch.tsx` sets `overflow-hidden`, so a panel growing out of a slot is **clipped at the touchline**.
> 2. 183×172 does not hold a searchable, filterable player list anyway.
> 
> So the morph stays in the slot and the panel opens separately, anchored to it. The picker renders the same `filtered` memo the pool table reads with the position lock `startAdding` already applies — a surface, not a second search, so the two can never disagree about eligibility.
> 
> ### Follow-up worth filing
> 
> The replacement badges initially duplicated the prose beneath them ("frees £0.5m" twice). Fixed by filtering the phrases the badges carry out of `rationale` — but that couples the component to the exact strings `lib/scoring.ts:569-576` builds. **The durable fix is for `Replacement` to expose xP delta, price delta and fixture delta as fields** and let each surface compose its own sentence; `/transfers` renders the same array and has the same latent problem.
> 
> ### Two bugs found by testing, not review
> 
> - The badge/prose duplication above.
> - That filter then silently did nothing, because a Python heredoc wrote `\b` into the source as a literal **backspace (0x08)** instead of a regex word boundary — so it tested `/\x08xP\x08/`. `grep` renders control characters invisibly, so the source looked right while the browser disagreed. Swept every touched file for stray control characters afterwards: 0 found.
> 
> ### Verification
> 
> Fresh tab after a dev-server restart and `.next` wipe: zero console errors on `/builder` and `/fixtures`; slot picker verified in both surfaces (desktop popover 320px, clamped, flipped above; mobile docked to the bottom edge); adding a player closes the panel and returns the squad to 15; rename toggles both ways; Save computes transparent; replacement cards read `−0.4 xP | frees £0.5m | risk -7 · fit -0.0 · 19 exit routes` with nothing repeated.
> 
> **Not verified:** the motion itself. The preview pane reports `document.hidden === true` and 0 `requestAnimationFrame` callbacks in 500ms, so CSS animations sit frozen at `currentTime: 0`. Geometry was checked by subtracting the frozen transform. Worth a human look at the `+`→`×` morph and the sheet rise.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Owner note (2026-09-12): the pitch board reads as unactionable. Spec for the C1 Builder pass.**
> 
> The empty-slot `+` should become an affordance that obviously does something, using the new `References/Components/plus-to-menu.md` (Transitions.dev morph: a 40px circular `+` that grows into a rounded panel, the plus rotating 45° into an `×` as the menu cross-fades in; `prefers-reduced-motion` guarded like the rest of that set).
> 
> Required behaviour:
> 
> | State | Desktop | Mobile |
> |---|---|---|
> | **Empty slot** | `+` morphs into a panel of eligible players for that position, with search and filter | Bottom sheet via `panel-reveal`, same options |
> | **Filled slot** | Player info via the `plus-to-menu` morph panel | Player info via `panel-reveal` bottom sheet |
> 
> ### What already exists to reuse
> 
> - `EmptySlot` and the `onAddToSlot` callback in `components/pitch-view.tsx` — the slot already knows its position and that it is empty.
> - The eligible-player list, search and filter are **already built**: `/builder`'s picker table plus `components/player-filters.tsx`, which `/players` shares. This is a relocation into a slot-anchored surface, not new filtering logic.
> - `components/player-detail.tsx` is the filled-state content, and already does viewport-clamped anchoring (`PANEL_WIDTH` / `PANEL_MAX_HEIGHT`, flip-above/below in `pitch-view.tsx:104-144`).
> - `components/ui/slide-over.tsx` for the mobile sheet — currently `side: "left" | "right"`, so it needs a `bottom` variant.
> - Motion tokens (`duration-*`, `ease-slide`, `ease-pop`) landed in Sprint A, so the morph's timings map onto the existing scale rather than adding a fourth set of magic numbers.
> 
> ### One thing to resolve before building
> 
> The reference hardcodes its open footprint at **183 × 172px**. That fits a short menu; it does not fit a searchable, filterable list of eligible players. Two readings:
> 
> 1. The morph animates to a much larger footprint — needs checking that the width/height transition still reads well at ~360 × 420px rather than looking like a panel inflating.
> 2. The morph is only the **trigger** affordance, and it hands off to the existing anchored panel at full size.
> 
> I'd lean (2) — it keeps one player-picker surface instead of two, and `useAnchoredPanel` already solves the clamping that a hand-sized morph panel would have to re-solve. Will confirm against the real pitch at Builder time rather than deciding it here.
> 
> Also worth noting for that pass: DSI-121's separate ask to add `[ Swap with Bench ]` / `[ Find Replacement ]` actions inside the player popover is filed under [DSI-137](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-137) (Sprint C2, feature-shaped). The filled-state surface above is where those buttons would land, so the two should be built together rather than the popover being restyled twice.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-122</b> — Design Audit - News</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`1b4640c`). Nearly all of this was already closed by Sprint B's change-feed work — verified against the running page, not re-implemented.
>
> **Already done:** emoji pills are gone (monochrome line SVGs), so the “yellow circle in a green active pill” clash can't occur; price rows already carry a directional arrow *and* a signed coloured delta badge; availability rows are already the exact two-line pattern asked for (name · transition · status badge with the percentage · timestamp, medical detail on a muted second line); no raw ISO renders anywhere — fixture changes read “Wed, Dec 30, 03:00 PM → Tue, Dec 29, 02:30 PM”, and a cross-round move already swaps the “Time change” pill for “GW20 → GW21”; RSS excerpts are already `line-clamp-2`.
>
> **Actually open, and built:** the entity chips. They carried the resolved player/club, looked like controls, and did nothing. Clicking one now narrows the feed to headlines mentioning that entity — `aria-pressed` on the chip, a named empty state, and a banner naming the active filter beside a clear button, because a filter with no visible state is a trap. Verified 134 items → 1 on `BHA`, back to 134 on clear.
>
> **Routed to** [DSI-137](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-137/m9-sprint-c2-feature-shaped-items-from-the-audit) (feature-shaped, not visual): the “My Squad Only” toggle, which needs squad state on a page that has none; and the BGW/DGW annotation on a fixture move, which needs per-team fixture counting this page doesn't do.

---

### 1. Header & Filter Pill Navigation

* **Mixed Emoji and Icon Metaphors:** The pill row pairs literal text with inconsistent emoji decorations: `📈 Prices`, `🟡 Availability`, `📰 News`, `🗓️ Fixtures`, `🗞️ Feeds`. The yellow circle emoji on "Availability" looks like an active warning state rather than a category label, while the rest are object icons.
  * *Fix:* Drop system emojis and use monochrome, fine-line SVGs (16px), or rely solely on clean text pills with an active background toggle.
* **Active Tab Styling Inconsistency:** In "Availability", the active pill has a yellow emoji inside a solid green background (`#00FF87`). That clashes visually. Keep the active pill background consistent with your primary brand accent or a clean elevated surface color, without nesting contrasting colored icons inside it.
* **Missing Squad Filter:** On a sitewide news page, an FPL manager's primary question is: *"Does this affect my 15 players?"*
  * *Fix:* Add a sticky sub-toggle or filter switch at the top right of the feed: `[ All Players | My Squad Only ]`. This cuts noise by 90% for a manager doing quick deadline maintenance.

### 2. Price Changes (`News-Prices`)

* **Row Scanning:** The price moves (`Scott (BOU) · £6.0m → £6.1m` vs `O'Reilly (MCI) · £6.5m → £6.4m`) are displayed in a clean single-line format. However, the icon treatment is too muted: a tiny pale purple tile with a light line chart.
* **Scannable Color Accents:**
  * For rises: Show a bold green up-triangle `▲ +£0.1m` and render the new price in `#00FF87` or crisp white.
  * For falls: Show a bold red down-triangle `▼ -£0.1m` and render the drop in light crimson (`#FF4B4B`).
  * Right now, users must read the raw decimal strings to determine if a player went up or down.

### 3. Availability & Injuries (`News-Availability`)

* **Semantic Dots vs Status Text:**
  * You use red and amber status dots on the left margin, which helps visual anchoring.
  * However, the text strings are redundant: `"Collins (BRE) · Doubtful → Injured (0% next round) — "Calf injury - Unknown return date""`.
  * `Doubtful → Injured` tells the story, but `(0% next round)` and the quoted subtext repeat the same premise three times in one continuous line.
* **Two-Line Card Pattern:** Break this into two scan-friendly rows:
  * **Row 1 (Entity & Status):** `Collins (BRE)` [Badge: `Injured 0%`] · `49m ago`
  * **Row 2 (News Detail):** `"Calf injury — Unknown return date"` (muted text at 70% opacity)
  * This lets the eye scan down player names on the left without getting lost in long transition sentences.

### 4. Fixture Rescheduling (`News-Fixtures`)

* **Raw ISO Timestamps:**
  * Current display: `SUN v LIV — kickoff 2027-01-06T20:00 → 2027-01-05T20:15`
  * Displaying raw machine ISO strings (`2027-01-06T20:00`) forces the user to parse characters mentally.
  * *Fix:* Format this into readable, colloquial dates and times:
    * `SUN v LIV`: `Wed 6 Jan, 20:00` → `Tue 5 Jan, 20:15 GMT`
* **Gameweek Context Missing:** The critical piece of FPL fixture information is whether a shift triggers a **Blank Gameweek (BGW)** or **Double Gameweek (DGW)**.
  * If a fixture shifts from Tuesday to Wednesday in the same round, it is just a kickoff time adjustment.
  * If it moves across gameweeks, it alters the chip strategy entirely.
  * Add a pill highlighting the impact: `[ Time Change ]` vs `[ Moves to GW21 - Double GW ]`.

### 5. News Feeds & RSS Headlines (`News-Feeds`)

* **Source Tabs:** The sub-filter pills (`All`, `Scout`, `BBC`, `Guardian`, `Sky`) provide clear filtering.
* **Headline vs Metadata Hierarchy:**
  * Headlines like *"Dedic + Fernandez-Pardo: Newcastle injury latest for FPL Gameweek 4"* are readable.
  * However, the secondary description below it repeats the headline almost verbatim (*"Tino Livramento replaced Amir Dedic during Tuesday's EFL Cup win The post Dedic + Fernandez-Pardo..."*).
  * Truncate the RSS summary text to a single clean excerpt line (e.g., CSS `line-clamp: 2`).
* **Entity Pills:** The player tags (`FFS`, `Dedic`, `Fernandez-Pardo`, `NEW`, `Livramento`) at the bottom of each story card are a strong feature.
  * Make these interactive: clicking `Livramento` should open his player card or filter the feed directly to stories mentioning him.

### News Page Action Checklist

| **View / Element** | **UX Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Top Filter Bar** | System emojis (`📈`, `🟡`, `📰`) look inconsistent with the UI design system. | Replace emojis with clean monochrome SVGs or text-only pills. |
| **Personal Context** | Feed lists all 600+ Premier League players without personal filtering. | Add a `[ My Squad Only ]` filter toggle at the top of the feed. |
| **Price Feed** | Rises and drops look identical; requires manual reading of decimals. | Use distinct green `▲ +£0.1m` and red `▼ -£0.1m` badges. |
| **Availability Rows** | Long single-string paragraphs make fast scanning difficult. | Restructure into two rows: Name + Status Badge on row 1, medical detail on row 2. |
| **Fixture Rescheduling** | Unformatted ISO strings (`2027-01-06T20:00`) look like raw backend logs. | Format as human dates (`Tue 5 Jan, 20:15`) and highlight if gameweek boundaries move. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Partly pre-delivered — don't redo when this is picked up.**
> 
> From Sprint B: the emoji filter pills are gone, replaced by monochrome `lucide` line icons inside a `SegmentedControl`, which also resolves *"the green-pill-with-yellow-emoji active state"* — the indicator is a neutral raised surface, so nothing fights the icon inside it.
> 
> From DSI-138 (`4cc20d7`): the **feed-source pills** (`All / Scout / BBC / Guardian / Sky`) were `Button variant="toggle"` sitting directly under that already-segmented filter row, so this one page had two different shapes for "pick exactly one". Both rows are now the same control.
> 
> Still open here: two-line availability rows, `line-clamp-2` on RSS summaries.
> 
> The **My Squad Only filter** is not this issue — it needs the signed-in squad on `/news`, which the page doesn't load. It lives in DSI-137 (Sprint C2).

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-123</b> — Design Audit - Scenarios</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`e2ae898`), driven signed-in against the test account with seven drafts created through the real Import-as-draft + Clone path.
>
> **Built:** `Open` demoted from solid accent to outline — seven neon fills in a grid is wallpaper, not a CTA. Draft names go from single-line `truncate` to two lines (a single-line ellipsis eats exactly the tail that distinguishes them). The compare checkbox was 13px, under the 24px minimum — the label already wrapped box and word, so padding it makes the pair one target. Metric labels routed through `AnnotatedLabel`. Tray button relabelled to what it does (it scrolls).
>
> **Sticky header — real, and the obvious fix was inert.** `overflow-x-auto` computes `overflow-y` to `auto`, making the wrapper the scrollport, and a wrapper with no height never scrolls. Measured at a 560px viewport: header left the screen at **−181px** with `sticky` applied. Capping the wrapper height makes the table its own scroll region — verified scrolling 224px with the header pinned.
>
> **Declined, with reasons:** disabling Horizon under “Points scored” — horizon still drives the SquadScore the cards are *ranked* by in that view; only the secondary line switches to two fixed windows, which already name themselves (“41 pts GW4 · 255 season”). And reserving green for the composite winner only — the row arrows are already direction-correct, so a down arrow on mean risk reads as “lowest”, and stripping per-row winners removes the comparison's actual content.
>
> **Already fixed, not re-implemented:** the inverted winner arrow (3 down arrows alongside 9 up, from Sprint B), and both toggles already being `SegmentedControl`s.
>
> Note: the audit's “make the whole card clickable to open” was not done — the card title is a rename-on-click button, and a surface-wide target would swallow it, the checkbox and three actions.

---

### 1. Scenario Lab Grid (`Scenarios-Squads`)

#### Top Controls & Global Consistency

* **Dual Toggle Confusion:** You have `Show: [ xP ] [ Points scored ]` sitting directly adjacent to `Horizon: [ 1 GW | 3 GW | 5 GW | 8 GW | 19 GW | Season ]`.
  * If a user selects "Points scored," the Horizon controls lose meaning because historical points are backward-looking (Gameweeks completed), while Horizons are forward-looking projections.
  * Separate them clearly: use a segmented control for the primary lens (`View: Projections (xP) vs Historical Actuals`), and disable or hide the forward Horizon pills when viewing historical scores.
* **Backup Banner Prominence:** The backup alert (`Export drafts / Import drafts — Drafts live in this browser only...`) takes up valuable vertical space above the card grid.
  * Browser storage loss is a real danger for client-side apps, but it shouldn't dominate the strategy workspace.
  * Move `Export / Import` into a clean utility button group at the top right of the page header, right next to the title or search bar.

#### Squad Card Architecture

* **Truncated Card Titles:**
  * The top-ranked card displays `Premium Def Improved +3 transfe...`.
  * Truncating the most descriptive part of the user's draft title forces them to guess what distinguishes this draft from `Premium Def Improved` in card #2. Allow two lines of text or truncate the middle of the string (`Premium Def...3 transfers`).
* **Selection Checkbox Placement:**
  * The `compare` checkbox sits pinned to the top right of each card.
  * The touch target is tiny and the label blends into the card frame.
  * Turn the entire card into an intentional selectable state when in compare mode, or enlarge the checkbox to a distinct 20px hit area with an active border.
* **Card Primary Metric Hierarchy:**
  * The large green numbers (`334.9`, `317.4`, `300.1`) represent `SquadScore`, with `335.3 xP` in small grey text beside them.
  * To an FPL manager, raw `xP` is the actual currency of the game, whereas `SquadScore` is your proprietary composite index.
  * Stack these cleanly:
    * `334.9` **SquadScore** (Bold, white or green)
    * `335.3` **xP** · `£0.7m ITB` (Secondary line, crisp light text)
* **Action Button Row:**
  * Each card has four adjacent buttons: `[ Open ]`, `[ Clone ]`, `[ Timeline ]`, `[ Delete ]`.
  * `Open` is bright neon green `#00FF87`. In a 6-card grid, you have 6 bright green squares screaming at the user simultaneously, destroying visual focus.
  * Make clicking the card anywhere open the draft by default. Keep `[ Clone ]`, `[ Timeline ]`, and `[ Delete ]` tucked into a 3-dot context menu (`⋮`) or subtle secondary icon buttons at the top right of each card.

### 2. Side-by-Side Draft Comparison (`Scenarios-Compare`)

#### Metric Scanning & Table Layout

* **Sticky Header Visibility:**
  * The comparison table layout (`METRIC`, followed by 4 draft columns) is the right model for multi-variable evaluation.
  * When scrolling down through 15+ rows of metrics, the draft titles at the top disappear off the screen. Freeze/pin the top header row so the user never loses track of which column is which.
* **Green Up-Triangles Overload:**
  * Almost every single winning metric in a row has a bright green value and a green triangle: `▲ 334.9`, `▲ 335.3`, `▲ 61`, `▲ 218`, `▲ +0.1`, `▲ 2.6`, `▲ +7.9`, `▲ 24`.
  * This creates visual noise. If every row has a green triangle pointing in a different column, the user cannot tell which draft actually wins the overall trade-off.
  * **Fix:**
    * Reserve the solid green badge strictly for the primary composite winner (`SquadScore` or `Total xP`).
    * For individual row winners, use a subtle bold white font with a muted highlight cell background (`rgba(0, 255, 135, 0.08)`) instead of neon text on every line.

#### Logic & Semantic Clarity

* **Inverted Metrics (Risk and FDR):**
  * `Mean player risk`: The table displays `24 ▲` in green for the first column, while the others are `28`, `31`, `36`.
  * A lower risk score is better, but an upward-pointing arrow (`▲`) traditionally signals a higher number. An upward arrow next to a lower number causes a cognitive double-take.
  * Use a directional downward arrow for metrics where lower is better (`▼ 24 (Lowest Risk)`), or drop the arrow and rely on a "Best" label.
* **Underline Styling on Metric Labels:**
  * Rows like `Points scored · season to date` and `Fixture quality` have dotted underlines beneath them.
  * Dotted underlines typically indicate an interactive tooltip. If they are tooltips, ensure there is a clear hover cursor (`cursor: help`). If they are not interactive, remove the dotted border to avoid false signifiers.

#### Bottom Sticky Tray

* **Bar Redundancy:**
  * The bottom tray displays `Comparing 4 drafts` on the left, `Clear` in the middle, and a green button `Compare 4 drafts ↓` on the right.
  * You are **already** looking at the comparison table. A button labeled `Compare 4 drafts ↓` is confusing: does clicking it scroll down, recalculate, or open a modal?
  * If the user is already on the compare view, that button should be `[ Back to Grid ]` or `[ Open Selected in Builder → ]`.

### Scenario Lab Action Checklist

| **Element** | **Current Issue** | **Recommended Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Draft Cards** | 6 bright green `Open` buttons create massive visual distraction. | Make the card surface clickable to open; hide secondary actions behind a `⋮` menu. |
| **Card Titles** | Important differentiator names get truncated (`+3 transfe...`). | Wrap to 2 lines or use middle-truncation (`Premium...3 transfers`). |
| **Comparison Table** | The table header scrolls out of view when reading down the list. | Make the draft names header row sticky (`position: sticky; top: 0`). |
| **Inverted Metrics** | Lower risk (24) uses an upward arrow `▲`. | Use down indicators `▼` or a clean "Lowest" pill for inverted metrics. |
| **Visual Overload** | Too many competing green values across every single row. | Use cell background shading for secondary metric winners; reserve `#00FF87` for the overall winner. |
| **Bottom Action Bar** | Button says `Compare 4 drafts ↓` while already on the comparison page. | Relabel button to `[ Back to Squads ]` or `[ Edit in Builder ]`. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Partly pre-delivered by DSI-138** (commit `4cc20d7` on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-builder`) — don't redo these when this issue is picked up.
> 
> Done:
> - **"Dual Toggle Confusion"** — `Show: [xP] [Points scored]` and the Horizon strip are now the same `SegmentedControl` shape, so they read as two parameters rather than two unrelated widgets. The audit asked to *separate* them; they are still adjacent, because the underlying objection was that they looked like different kinds of control, not that they were near each other. Re-judge that on the screen before moving anything.
> - **`Delta` fixes the inverted risk arrow** — landed in Sprint B.
> - The disabled "Points scored" state now explains itself in visible text instead of a `title=`.
> 
> Still open here: card surface opens the draft + secondary actions into a `⋮` menu (6 competing green buttons), 2-line card titles, sticky compare header via `DataTable`, bottom-tray button relabel, Export/Import into the page header.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-124</b> — Design Audit - Transfers (Transfers Strategy)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`20082ba`). Three findings here are false on measurement, and two of the prescribed fixes would have been wrong.
>
> **Built:** step cards now close their sum (`+15.8 xP + 1.2 risk = +17.0`) instead of listing terms and leaving the reader to subtract the hit — via a new exported `stepNet()`, not a second copy of the formula. `Load` → `Load into basket`. Replacement rationales became tone-coded badges.
>
> **The badge work needed the durable fix /builder had already noted and deferred:** `Replacement.rationale` was `string[]`, so `/builder` filtered it with `t.includes("xP")` — a wording change in `lib/scoring.ts` would have silently broken it. Notes now carry `kind` and `tone`; `/builder` filters on `kind`; the typechecker caught the old filter the moment the shape changed.
>
> **Verified false:** the step summary is “green despite a negative net” — it is `text-zinc-500` and has never been green. `Replace` is “dark purple filled” — it is a transparent outline, 36px. The 5 GW column is “all neon green” — most values are near-white and `#00FF87` is already reserved for top performers, which is what the audit asks for.
>
> **Prescriptions corrected:** `Open in Builder` would be wrong — Load populates the basket further down this same page, as the sibling “In basket” label says. And hiding `Replace` behind row hover is declined: this project already migrated nine hover-only tooltips to touch-accessible `TapToReveal`, and putting a primary action back behind hover would undo that.
>
> **Left for** [DSI-137](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-137/m9-sprint-c2-feature-shaped-items-from-the-audit)**:** filling the right rail's empty state with transfer targets — a new feature, not a layout fix.

---

### 1. Header Controls & Context Sync (`Transfers-Path`)

* **Sub-Tab Visual Hierarchy:** The `[ Transfer path ]` and `[ Chip timing ]` pills sit directly adjacent to the squad selector `Squad [ DS United (FPL) ▾ ]`.
  * The tabs control page mode, while the dropdown chooses the target draft.
  * Grouping them together creates ambiguity. Separate the tab navigation from the draft selector: place `[ Transfer path | Chip timing ]` on the left as a segmented button group, and float the `Squad:` dropdown right-aligned under the Horizon bar.
* **Free Transfers & Wildcard Pill:**
  * `Free transfers [ 0 ▾ ]` and `[ ] Apply as Wildcard (no hit)` float on their own line without clear bounding.
  * Make this a dedicated settings strip or place it inside the Transfer Path card where the calculations actually occur.
  * When `Apply as Wildcard` is checked, the hit calculation below (`- 8 hit`) changes to zero. Ensure this state change gives an immediate visual cue (e.g., display a gold `Wildcard Active` badge) so users don't wonder where hit penalties went.

### 2. Multi-Gameweek Transfer Path Card

* **Action Trigger vs Result Placement:**
  * The green outline button `Re-plan path` sits inside the top card, but the actual algorithm output (`+82.8 xP over holding today's squad...`) appears below it.
  * The `+82.8 xP` delta is the headline result. Make it the primary focal point:
    * **Header:** `Projected Gain: +82.8 xP` (Bold `#00FF87`)
    * **Subtext:** `Outperforms roll/hold strategy through GW9 by +0.1 xP`
    * **Action:** Keep `Re-plan path` aligned right on that same header row.
* **Gameweek Step Cards (**`GW4 — 2 transfers`):
  * The detail rows (`Kinsky → Leno: +14.0 xP, price-neutral`, `Calafiori → Tarkowski: +11.0 xP, costs £0.4m`) are practical and informative.
  * However, the net trade-off summary (`+4.9 xP - 8 hit`) sits isolated at the top right next to a green `Load` button.
  * `+4.9 xP - 8 hit` is mathematically a net **negative** immediate gameweek impact (`-3.1 net`), even if it pays off over 5 GWs.
  * Clearly present both the immediate hit cost and the horizon payoff: `Immediate net: -3.1 pts (after -8 hit) · 5-GW Horizon: +25.0 xP`. Using green for a string containing an uncalculated `-8 hit` is misleading.
* **The "Load" Action:**
  * What does `Load` do? Does it open these transfers in the Builder, or apply them to the current browser draft?
  * Rename it explicitly: `[ Apply to Builder → ]` or `[ Stage Transfers ]`.

### 3. Squad Transfer Table (`Transfers-Players`)

* **Right-Rail Blank Space:**
  * Before clicking `Replace` on a player, the right-hand panel is 90% empty space with three passive text lines (`Choose a player to transfer out...`, `Team xP · 5 GW: 299.0`, `In the bank: £0.4m`).
  * Use this empty state productively: display **Top Transfer Targets** or a quick snapshot of the **Gameweek Watchlist** instead of blank background space.
* **Button Repetition on Table Rows:**
  * Every single player row has an identical dark purple `[ Replace ]` button.
  * Repeating 11 identical buttons down the table creates visual clutter.
  * Make the entire row clickable to select the player, and show the `[ Replace ]` action clearly upon row hover or selection.
* **Metric Column Readability:**
  * The `5 GW` projected points (`17.1`, `13.8`, `18.5`) are all styled in neon green `#00FF87`.
  * If every number is green, none of them stand out as top performers. Use crisp white text for standard projection numbers, reserving `#00FF87` only for top quartile assets (such as Haaland's `30.6` and B.Fernandes' `31.5`).

### 4. Player Replacements Drawer (`Transfers-PlayerReplacements`)

* **Candidate Row Structure:**
  * Selecting `Verbruggen` opens the replacement candidates on the right (`Leno`, `Petrović`, `Pope`, `Tzolakis`).
  * The xP numbers on the right margin (`17.8`, `16.0`, `14.0`) are clean and easy to scan vertically.
* **Scannability of Rationale:**
  * The subtext strings are packed together: `-3.1 xP — a downgrade · better fixtures · more risk · 16 exit routes`.
  * Break this dense string into three distinct visual tags:
    * **Performance:** `-3.1 xP` (Muted red badge)
    * **Tradeoff:** `Frees £0.4m` (Amber badge) or `Better Fixtures` (Muted blue badge)
    * **Fit & Exit:** `16 exit routes · fit -3.2`
  * This allows a manager scanning for budget enablers to instantly spot the green/amber price badges without reading every line of text.

### Transfers Page Action Checklist

| **Component** | **UX Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Sub-Tabs** | Strategy tabs and draft selectors are mixed together on one line. | Move tabs to the left; right-align the draft selector. |
| **Hit Calculation** | `+4.9 xP - 8 hit` is styled in green despite immediate negative net value. | Calculate and display net value: `Immediate: -3.1 pts · Horizon: +25.0 xP`. |
| **"Load" Button** | Ambiguous verb; destination unclear. | Change label to `[ Open in Builder → ]`. |
| **Squad Table** | 11 identical `Replace` buttons repeat down the table. | Make full rows clickable; highlight action on hover. |
| **Right Rail Empty State** | Large vacant container before selecting a player. | Display top transfer targets or squad health summary by default. |
| **Replacement Badges** | Dense comma-separated strings for fixture/price/risk trade-offs. | Break into modular color-coded badges (`xP delta`, `budget delta`, `fixture quality`). |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Partly pre-delivered by DSI-138** (commit `4cc20d7` on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-builder`) — don't redo when this is picked up.
> 
> Done: **"Sub-Tab Visual Hierarchy."** `[Transfer path | Chip timing]` is now a `SegmentedControl`, so the tabs and the `Squad [▾]` selector beside them are visibly different kinds of control. The audit asked to move the tabs left and the selector right; they are still on one row, because the real objection was that a pill group and a dropdown looked alike. Verified on screen — worth re-judging before repositioning anything.
> 
> Also carried into this issue from the Builder pass: the **`Plan path` CTA unification into the action bar** was deferred out of DSI-121 because `components/transfer-path.tsx` is shared with this page. It belongs here.
> 
> Still open: immediate net *and* horizon payoff (keep CLAUDE.md's `+8.5 xP − 8 hit = +0.3` form while fixing the all-green rendering), rename `Load`, timeframe tags on `+291.4 (Season)` vs `+18.6 (single GW)`, neutral pin icons, and the **clipped Free Hit card** — a real overflow bug, not a styling preference.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-125</b> — Design Audit - Transfers (Chip Strategy)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`b29a0f3`).
>
> **The pin emoji was the whole story.** Every pin button already styled itself `text-zinc-400 → hover:text-primary`, and 📌 rendered in the platform's own colours and ignored all of it — the exact defect [DSI-122](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-122/design-audit-news) fixed on `/news`, with the rationale written down there verbatim. Replaced with lucide's `Pin`, which inherits `currentColor`.
>
> **On** `/scenarios` **the same emoji was hiding a functional bug**, not an aesthetic one: that button styles amber-when-pinned against grey-when-not, and the emoji made both states look identical. Pinned vs unpinned is now distinguishable (verified: amber pin + `PINNED` badge on the pinned card, grey on the rest).
>
> **Also built:** one-off chip values gained “this GW” beside the number — they sit across from a Wildcard reading +297.4 and the only thing making +20.5 comparable was a footnote under the *other* card; the Wildcard row already named its window, so this is the matching half. Icon-only pin buttons gained real accessible names.
>
> **The ✕ beside each chip-plan dropdown is gone.** The audit asked for the clear to live inside the dropdown — it already did: “Not planned” has always been an option and always cleared the pin. The ✕ was a second, unlabelled ~12px path to the same thing next to a 36px control, which is the misclick described.
>
> **Not reproduced:** the Free Hit card cut off mid-sentence. The text renders complete.
>
> **Already in** [DSI-137](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-137/m9-sprint-c2-feature-shaped-items-from-the-audit)**, not done here:** `Apply to Plan` on the recommendation banner, and `Pin → Apply Sequence` on the narrative cards.
>
> Unrelated bug found while driving this page signed in — filed as [DSI-139](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-139/draft-snapshots-cloud-sync-fails-silently-upsert-hits-a-missing-update): `draft_snapshots` cloud sync fails silently because the upsert takes an UPDATE path the table has no policy for.

---

### 1. Collapsible Chip Plan Form (`Transfers-ChipPlan`)

#### Form Layout & Interaction Targets

* **Column Grid Organization:** Laying out the four chips (`Wildcard`, `Free Hit`, `Bench Boost`, `Triple Captain`) in four neat vertical columns works well for desktop scanning.
* **Dropdown Clutter & Clear Targets:**
  * For active selections (`GW6`, `GW9`, `GW7`), the dropdown sits right beside an unstyled `✕` icon button.
  * The `✕` has a tiny hit area that risks misclicks.
  * Replace the adjacent `✕` with a native `Clear` option directly inside the dropdown list itself (`GW6`, `GW7`, ..., `None / Unplanned`) to eliminate visual clutter and avoid accidental resets.
* **Gameweek Split Grouping (**`GW2-19` vs `GW20-38`):
  * Having two stacked rows of dropdowns under each chip makes sense for FPL (handling first-half and second-half chip windows).
  * However, labels like `GW2-19` and `GW20-38` are rendered in very low contrast, small grey text.
  * Add a subtle horizontal divider between the first half of the season and the second half, or clearly label them: `Part 1 (GW 1-19)` and `Part 2 (GW 20-38)`.
* **Impact Feedback:**
  * Notice that selecting chips changed your transfer path projection from `+82.8 xP` down to `+81.9 xP`.
  * There is no visual feedback showing *why* setting a chip reduced or adjusted the transfer path value.
  * Add a small inline status tag under the re-plan bar: `Path adjusted for Wildcard in GW6` so users directly connect the chip change to the projection calculation.

### 2. Chip Timing & Optimization Strategy (`Transfers-ChipStrategy`)

#### High-Impact Hero Callout

* **Banner Visual Weight:**
  * `"Best single play: Wildcard in GW20 — +291.4 vs. holding it."`
  * This is an enormous insight, but it is rendered as plain paragraph text with a green inline float.
  * Turn this into a dedicated recommendation banner card:
    * **Tag:** `Model Top Recommendation`
    * **Headline:** `Play Wildcard in GW20`
    * **Value Delta:** `+291.4 xP projected gain over season hold`
    * **Action:** `[ Apply to Plan ]` button right inside the banner

#### Narrative Strategy Sequences ("Early-information anchor" & "Break pivot")

* **Action Ambiguity ("Pin"):**
  * Each narrative sequence card has an outline button labeled `Pin`.
  * Does clicking `Pin` save this to your settings, lock the gameweek in the transfer optimizer, or set the dropdowns in the `Chip plan` view?
  * Rename the button to reflect the concrete action: `[ Apply Sequence ]` or `[ Set as Active Plan ]`.
* **Typography:**
  * The titles (`Early-information anchor`, `Break pivot`) are clear, but the explanatory text below them (*"Wildcard GW7 — 3 gameweeks of real starts/minutes/form before committing..."*) uses long, dark grey sentences.
  * Emphasize the core strategic reason using bold inline callouts: **Why GW7:** 3 gameweeks of actual starting minutes; avoids small-sample bias.

#### Right-Hand Single-Chip Rankings (`Wildcard`, `Free Hit`)

* **Metric Realism & Contextual Framing:**
  * In the `Wildcard` card, the numbers read: `+291.4`, `+252.2`, `+242.1`.
  * In the `Free Hit` card, the numbers read: `+18.6`, `+18.5`, `+18.3`.
  * To someone glancing at both cards, `+291.4` looks like an impossible point explosion compared to `+18.6`.
  * The subtext explains that Wildcard projects through GW38 (full season horizon), while Free Hit is a single gameweek score.
  * Add the timeframe explicitly to the column header or metric tag:
    * Wildcard: `+291.4 xP (Full Season)`
    * Free Hit: `+18.6 xP (GW38 Only)`
* **The Red Pin Icons (**`📌`):
  * Each row has a small red pushpin icon on the far right.
  * Red typically indicates danger, errors, or hits in fantasy football interfaces.
  * Using a red pin icon for an action that sets an optimal gameweek sends mixed signals. Switch to a neutral grey/white pin icon that shifts to green `#00FF87` once pinned.
