* **Bottom Cutoff & Truncation:**
  * At the very bottom of the Free Hit card, text is cropped mid-sentence: *"Top 3 gameweeks are within 0.3 points of each other — this is not"*.
  * Ensure containers adjust height dynamically or provide proper bottom padding so critical insights do not get cut off by the viewport frame.

### Chip Strategy Action Checklist

| **Area** | **Current Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Chip Plan Dropdowns** | Tiny `✕` icon button invites misclicks and clutters the UI. | Move `None / Unplanned` into the dropdown options. |
| **Hero Insight** | `+291.4 xP` Wildcard callout is buried in a plain text paragraph. | Promote to a styled recommendation banner with an `Apply` button. |
| **"Pin" Action** | Ambiguous button label across sequence cards. | Rename to `[ Apply Sequence ]` or `[ Set Active ]`. |
| **Horizon Context** | Wildcard full-season delta (`+291.4`) sits beside single-week Free Hit delta (`+18.6`) without time tags. | Append timeframes directly to metric values: `(Season)` vs `(Single GW)`. |
| **Pin Icon Color** | Red pushpins look like error or hit penalty indicators. | Change pins to monochrome, highlighting green only when active. |
| **Text Truncation** | Right rail note gets clipped off at the bottom card edge. | Fix container padding and set flexible auto-height |

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-126</b> — Design Audit - Players</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`ab35d83`). Most of this audit was already closed by earlier M9 work, and several findings don't survive measurement against the running page.
>
> **Verified false / already done:** every numeric *cell* is already right-aligned with `tabular-nums`; the subtitle is already condensed with definitions in column `?` tooltips; `Price watch: unknown` renders nowhere (already `—`/`0%`); selected rows already carry both the 0.06 tint *and* an inset left strip, under a comment citing this issue; the slider thumb is 14px visually but **26px effective** and hit-tests 4px outside its edge (above the 24px asked for, with a hover halo); the comparison footnote is already behind a `ModelNote`; the score is already `text-xs`/`zinc-500` against a semibold name. Row heights measure 79–80px, so the stacked xP range is not making rows ragged.
>
> **Declined:** moving the xP range to hover. It's the model's uncertainty, and CLAUDE.md wants that disclosed beside the number — not behind a hover a touch user cannot perform.
>
> **Real, and built:** the comparison table marked the best cell with ▲ in *every* row including the low-wins ones, so the cheapest price got an up-arrow saying both that low is up and that cheap is better — price is a cost, not a performance score. Low-is-best rows now point down with a title naming the superlative. Six special filters became toggle pills with `aria-pressed`. Two headers (Price watch, XD) were left-aligned over right-aligned data. The filter popover already sat on a lighter surface, but `shadow-lg` at 10% alpha is invisible on this background — dark theme got a real one. Verdict reasons became Badges.

---

### 1. Data Table & Column Architecture (`Players-Base`)

#### Horizon & Subtitle Overload

* **Subtitle Instruction Clutter:**
  * `xP = model-projected points (next gameweek, and over the horizon below) · xG/xA are per 90 minutes · stats from 25/26 · green ring = home · top 100 shown`.
  * This five-part explanation runs horizontally as dense, unformatted text right below the title.
  * Move data definitions (such as `xG/xA per 90` or `green ring = home`) into the respective column header tooltips (`?`).
  * Keep only the active scope visible: a subtle count tag like `Showing top 100 players`.

#### Horizontal Density & Alignment

* **Column Overcrowding:** The table displays 18 columns (`PLAYER`, `TEAM`, `POS`, `PRICE`, `PRICE WATCH`, `XP GW`, `XP 5 GW`, `PTS`, `G`, `A`, `MINS`, `XG/90`, `XA/90`, `XMINS`, `XD`, `XP/£M`, `OWN %`, `PTS 25/26`, `XG 25/26`).
* **Left-Aligned Numerics:** Several number columns are left-aligned or lack fixed fractional decimals (`1.97`, `2.63`, `0.82`, `25.5` vs `10.79`).
  * Right-align all numeric data and pad numbers to consistent decimal lengths (`10.8` or `10.79`) so decimals line up vertically.
* **Projected Range Formatting (**`XP 5 GW`):
  * Haaland displays:
    * `30.6` [OR badge]
    * `27.7–33.5`
  * This is useful model uncertainty, but the tiny stacked text creates uneven row height. Keep row heights uniform by rendering the median in bold white (`30.6`) and moving the range `[27.7 – 33.5]` into a row hover state or column popover.
* **"Price Watch: unknown":** Several rows display the literal text `unknown`.
  * If a price prediction is unavailable or flat, render a neutral dash (`—`) or `0%` rather than the word `unknown`, which looks like an unhandled database `null`.

### 2. Multi-Select & Sticky Bottom Bar (`Players-Selected`)

* **Active Row Highlighting:**
  * When Haaland and B.Fernandes are checked, the green checkboxes turn on, but the rows themselves have barely any background distinction from unchecked rows (`Palmer`, `Saka`).
  * Give selected rows a persistent tinted background fill (`rgba(0, 255, 135, 0.06)`) and a subtle green left-border strip so selected players remain obvious when scrolling.
* **Bottom Action Tray:**
  * The sticky tray (`2 of 4 players selected`, `Clear`, `[ Compare 2 ]`) anchors the screen well.
  * However, clicking `Compare 2` opens a heavy slide-over modal that completely covers 60% of the viewport.
  * If the comparison panel is open, keep the underlying table interactive or dim the background with a proper scrim to signal a focused overlay.

### 3. Filter Popover (`Players-Filter`)

* **Popover Contrast & Elevation:**
  * The dropdown card uses the exact same dark purple color as the main background, separated by a thin 1px border.
  * Give this popover elevation: a slightly lighter surface shade (e.g., `#1E1035` vs `#120824`) and a defined outer shadow (`box-shadow: 0 10px 25px rgba(0,0,0,0.5)`) to cleanly separate it from the table behind it.
* **Range Slider Usability:**
  * The slider for `£4.0m – £16.0m` uses two bright green draggable thumbs.
  * The touch target for these circular thumbs is small (roughly 14px). Enlarge the touch target to at least 24px with an active hover halo to prevent misclicks on touchscreens or trackpads.
* **Special Tag Checkboxes:**
  * Options like `Defcon defender`, `Defcon midfielder`, `Breakout attacker`, and `Corner taker` are helpful filters.
  * However, grouping them in a two-column plain checkbox list looks like an administrative checklist.
  * Convert these into clickable multi-select filter pills (e.g., `[ + Corners ]`, `[ + Penalties ]`, `[ + Breakout ]`). Pills make toggling faster and scan better than small checkboxes.

### 4. Slide-Over Player Comparison Panel (`Players-Compare` & `Players-CompareSuggestion`)

#### Comparison Table Layout

* **Header Cards:**
  * The column headers (`Haaland MCI · FWD`, `B.Fernandes MUN · MID`, etc.) have small unstyled `✕` remove buttons sitting right next to the names.
  * These `✕` buttons lack hover states and crowd the player names. Give each player a distinct card header with the remove button positioned consistently in the top-right corner of each column.
* **Winning Attribute Highlighting:**
  * Winning rows use green triangles and green text: `▲ £9.5m` (Saka), `31.5 ▲` (B.Fernandes), `2.64 ▲` (Palmer).
  * Price: Saka is highlighted in green because he is the cheapest (`£9.5m`). However, for price, a lower number is not inherently "better" if the user has ample bank and wants the highest projected points.
  * Use green triangles strictly for performance metrics (`xP`, `xG`, `Bonus`, `Minutes`). Label price neutrally as `Lowest Cost` rather than a positive performance delta.
* **Fixture Strip Clutter:**
  * The `Fixtures` row at the bottom displays 5 colored fixture pills per player (e.g., `MUN`, `SUN`, `LIV`, `IPS`, `AVL`).
  * Because 4 players have 5 fixtures each, 20 bright orange, green, and yellow pills compete for attention simultaneously at the bottom of the table.
  * Stack the pills horizontally with tighter padding, or provide an average FDR badge (e.g., `FDR 2.4`) with an expandable fixture list on click.

#### Ranking Over 5 GW (The Model Recommendation)

* **Algorithmic Verdict Cards:**
  * This is an effective section: it explicitly tells the user which player the engine prefers (`1. B.Fernandes`, `2. Haaland`, `3. Palmer`, `4. Saka`).
  * The reason badges (`✓ Highest xP (31.5)`, `✓ Most secure minutes`, `! 9.9 xP behind the best`, `! Only 61% likely to start`) give clear, actionable justification.
  * **Visual Polish:**
    * Make the score numbers on the right (`score 0.743`, `score 0.529`) secondary; right now they have equal visual weight with the player's name.
    * For warnings (`! Only 61% likely to start`), use an amber badge background rather than raw yellow text so it reads as a caution flag.
* **Technical Footnote Block:**
  * The paragraph at the bottom (`"Effective ownership is omitted — nothing reads the field-wide top-1k sample this term needs yet..."`) takes up 6 lines of dense text.
  * This is internal model documentation. Move this entire explanation behind an informational tooltip (`ℹ️ Model methodology`) to eliminate cognitive drag from the primary decision screen.

### Player Explorer Action Checklist

| **Element** | **Current Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Subtitle Text** | Long unformatted instruction string runs across the screen. | Move metric definitions into column tooltips; keep only active player count visible. |
| **Table Numerics** | Unaligned decimal points and left-aligned numbers create scanning friction. | Right-align all numeric columns; pad decimals to uniform lengths. |
| **Selected Rows** | Checked rows have minimal contrast against unselected rows. | Add a visible background tint (`rgba(0,255,135,0.06)`) to active rows. |
| **Filter Popover** | Blends directly into the page background with zero elevation. | Lighten card background slightly and add a prominent drop shadow. |
| **Filter Checkboxes** | Special player categories look like an unstyled checkbox list. | Replace checkboxes with interactive multi-select pills. |
| **Comparison Footnote** | 6 lines of algorithmic defense text crowd the bottom of the card. | Move text into an info icon modal (`ℹ️ Model weights`). |

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-127</b> — Design Audit - Fixtures</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`b7d38a5`). Verified against the running app before implementing — six findings: **3 already closed** by earlier M9 work (match-event split layout landed with [DSI-119](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-119/design-audit-deadline-hub); venue rings fixed in the colourblind pass; Rating toggle already a SegmentedControl), **1 declined** (FDR desaturation — repeats a neutral-palette recommendation already rejected on the record, and would undo a measured WCAG fix on the 4-band orange), **2 built** plus the Table and Clubs items. Completed gameweeks now collapse into their own section below the upcoming ones rather than stacking above them, which is the version that still works at GW38. One item not done: the badge treatment for `xp_impact`, whose field is free-form source prose and sometimes `rotation_risk_factors` instead, so a badge would invent structure the data lacks.

---

### 1. Match Schedule Accordion (`Fixtures-ScheduleNoGw` & `Fixtures-ScheduleMatch`)

#### Visual Weight & Collapse States

* **Completed Gameweek Hierarchy:** Completed rounds (`GAMEWEEK 1`, `GAMEWEEK 2`, `GAMEWEEK 3`) receive identical visual weight and container height to upcoming rounds (`GAMEWEEK 4`).
  * Stack completed gameweeks into an understated, compact list: reduce row height by 30%, lighten the background, and drop the right-side deadline metadata for finished weeks.
  * Keep the upcoming round (`GAMEWEEK 4`) expanded by default with an active `#00FF87` left-accent border.
* **Match Kickoff Time Box:**
  * The time pills (`10:00 AM`) use a dark outline box centered between clubs.
  * The outline makes them look like interactive inputs rather than passive timestamps. Remove the rounded border; render the time in plain, clean tabular numerals (`10:00` or `15:00 BST`).

#### Detailed Match Events (`ARS 3 - 0 COV`)

* **Event Alignment Ambiguity:**
  * When `ARS 3 - 0 COV` expands, events are stacked in a single vertical column down the center (`Saka`, `Ødegaard`, `Havertz` under `GOALS`; `Gabriel`, `Yirenkyi` under `YELLOW CARDS`).
  * Em-dashes (`—`) indicate zero events for the opposing club, but because they float without column boundaries, the user cannot easily map which player belongs to which side.
  * **Fix:** Use a classic split scoreboard grid:
    * **Left Column (Home / ARS):** Right-aligned names (`Saka 12'`, `Ødegaard 44'`, `Havertz 81'`)
    * **Center Column:** Event icon (`⚽`, `🟨`, `🧤`)
    * **Right Column (Away / COV):** Left-aligned names (or blank/empty for zero events)

### 2. Fixture Difficulty Matrix (`Fixtures-FDR`)

#### Grid Scannability & Color Saturation

* **Visual Vibration:**
  * The FDR matrix displays 8 gameweeks (`GW4` to `GW11`) across 20 clubs. That produces **160 bright saturated pills** (`#00FF87`, orange, bright red, dark maroon) on screen at once.
  * High-saturation backgrounds on every cell cause visual fatigue.
  * Mute the palette: use dark, desaturated cell backgrounds with high-contrast text, or use neutral dark cells with a subtle 3px colored left-border or corner badge indicating FDR difficulty (1 to 5).
* **Home vs Away Indicators:**
  * The legend indicates `green ring = home, red ring = away`.
  * Applying a thin red or green ring border around an already brightly colored pill (such as a green border around an orange pill for `NFO` in GW5) creates low contrast and clashes visually.
  * Stick to standard FPL notation: uppercase with `(H)` or lowercase with `(A)` (e.g., `BHA (H)` vs `nfo (a)`). Text markers are colorblind-safe and instantly readable without decoding border rings.
* **Sorting & Window Controls:**
  * The window selector (`5 GWs`, `8 GWs`, `10 GWs`, `All`) and sort toggle (`Easiest run ▾`) sit neatly above the matrix.
  * The `Rating: [ Official | Strength ]` toggle uses a solid green button next to an unselected dark button. Ensure this is styled as a unified segmented pill switch so users understand it as a binary data view.

### 3. League Standings Table (`Fixtures-Table`)

#### Disclaimer Box Styling

* **Brown/Orange Alert Banner:**
  * The note *"FPL's own standings feed doesn't publish P/W/D/L/Pts during the season, so this table is computed from finished fixture results instead..."* sits in a large container with a heavy brown border.
  * This banner draws focus before the user sees the table itself.
  * Condense this to a single inline sentence in muted text right above the table header: `Standings computed from official match results` with an `ℹ️` tooltip for users wanting the API background details.

#### Table Structure & Data Flow

* **Form Column Format:**
  * Current form is rendered as raw monospaced character strings: `WWW`, `WWD`, `WWL`, `WDD`.
  * Convert these into distinct 18px circular status badges:
    * `W`: Deep green circle with white text
    * `D`: Muted grey circle with white text
    * `L`: Dark crimson circle with white text
  * This matches universal football UI patterns and makes team streaks immediately scannable.
* **Next 5 Fixtures in Table:**
  * Showing `GW4` through `GW8` right inside the league table is a strong feature that saves cross-referencing tabs.
  * Align the table column headers (`P`, `W`, `D`, `L`, `PTS`) strictly to the right, keeping team names left-aligned.

### 4. Club Tactical Profiles (`Fixtures-Clubs` & `Fixtures-ClubView`)

#### Card Grid & Expansion UX

* **3-Column Grid Layout:** The 3x4 card layout (`Arsenal`, `Aston Villa`, `Bournemouth`, etc.) organizes club metadata cleanly.
* **Header Balance & Visual Weight:**
  * Club badges, club names, and head coach names (`Mikel Arteta`, `Unai Emery`, `Marco Rose`) establish immediate context.
  * In the collapsed state, the two metrics (`Formation` and `Buildup`) are right-aligned cleanly against the labels on the left.

#### Deep Tactical Details (`Arsenal` Expanded)

* **Dense Italicized Text:**
  * The tactical breakdown uses long blocks of italicized text:
    * `Wingers: inverted inside scorer`
    * `*+10% npxG for wingers isolating 1v1 in wide/half-spaces*`
    * `Fullbacks: inverted pivot`
    * `*Reduced crossing xA; increased clean sheet & baseline minutes security*`
  * Reading multiple lines of italic text on dark purple creates optical fatigue.
  * Drop italics. Use regular font weight for descriptions, and highlight the actionable FPL fantasy impact in a structured badge:
    * **Wingers:** Inverted Inside Scorer → `[ +10% npxG in 1v1s ]` (Subtle green badge)
    * **Fullbacks:** Inverted Pivot → `[ +CS Floor / -xA ]` (Subtle blue badge)
* **Source & Model Multipliers:**
  * *"Source figures, not applied to xP — low block ×1.05, high press ×1.15, set pieces ×1.2"*
  * Stating "not applied to xP" right above model multipliers creates confusion: are these numbers active in the solver or just theoretical notes?
  * Clarify the label: `Tactical Context Only (Not factored into core projection algorithms)`.

### Fixtures & FDR Action Checklist

| **Component** | **UX Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Schedule Events** | Center-stacked event rows make it hard to tell which club scored. | Implement a two-column home/away split scoreboard layout. |
| **FDR Matrix** | 160 fully saturated cells cause visual fatigue. | Desaturate backgrounds; use text markers `(H)` and `(A)` instead of ring borders. |
| **Standings Form** | Plain text characters (`WWD`, `WWL`) look unstyled. | Replace with colored circular badges (`W`, `D`, `L`). |
| **Standings Note** | Heavy brown banner distracts from table data. | Condense into a one-line subtitle with an info tooltip. |
| **Tactical Profiles** | Long italicized descriptions are hard to scan. | Use standard type; display fantasy tradeoffs as concise pills. |
| **Completed GWs** | Finished rounds dominate vertical page space. | Compress completed rounds into dense single-line summary rows. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-12
>
> **The FDR half is shipped (`04e4298`, `268cc9f`). One recommendation is not implemented as written, and the reason is measured rather than argued.**
> 
> Method: Machado 2009 CVD simulation at severity 1.0, separation as linear-RGB distance; WCAG contrast from relative luminance.
> 
> ### "Desaturate the matrix" — rejected, the ramp passed
> 
> Adjacent-step separability of the five fills:
> 
> | | 1v2 | 2v3 | 3v4 | 4v5 |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | normal | 0.445 | 0.981 | 0.350 | 0.752 |
> | deuteranopia | 0.345 | 0.798 | 0.534 | 0.503 |
> | protanopia | 0.483 | 0.462 | 0.557 | 0.343 |
> | tritanopia | 0.539 | 1.017 | 0.320 | 0.698 |
> 
> The ramp is luminance-ordered, so every step stays ≥0.32 — sometimes *wider* than at normal vision. A blanket desaturation would have discarded a working channel to fix a broken one. Kept.
> 
> ### The venue rings — this was the real defect
> 
> green-400 vs red-400: **1.039 normal → 0.579 protanopia → 0.204 deuteranopia.** An 80% collapse, on a channel drawn on top of a fill that is itself red or green. Roughly 1 in 12 men could not read home from away.
> 
> Now: home = neutral ring (near-black light / near-white dark), away = **purple-400**. Re-measured across five candidates against two constraints — separation from the home ring, and from all five fills the ring sits on:
> 
> | | vs home (light/dark) | vs worst fill |
> |---|---|---|
> | **purple-400** | **0.676 / 0.903** | **0.505** |
> | fuchsia-500 | 0.764 / 0.997 | 0.274 |
> | violet-500 | 0.347 / 1.164 | 0.287 |
> | purple-500 | 0.432 / 1.096 | 0.265 |
> | purple-600 | 0.290 / 1.212 | 0.155 |
> 
> purple-400 is the only one clearing 0.35 on both axes in both themes.
> 
> **Red was checked and fails worse than the original.** Against a dark home ring it drops to **0.058 under protanopia**; against the orange `4 Hard` fill, **0.029** — an away ring that disappears into the cells it is drawn on.
> 
> The issue suggested `(H)`/`(A)` text markers instead. Not taken: at `text-[10px]` with a 3-letter code across 8 columns × 20 rows, the added width is significant, and the ring encoding measures safe without it.
> 
> ### A contrast bug found on the way
> 
> `4 Hard` in light mode was `text-white` on `orange-500` = **2.80:1**, failing WCAG AA even at the 3.0 large/bold threshold. Dark mode already paired `slate-950` with the identical fill at **7.20:1**. Both themes now use `slate-950`; both pass.
> 
> ### Also done
> 
> `Rating: [ Official | Strength ]` is now one `SegmentedControl` (`semantics="radio"` — it picks a parameter, not a view), as is the `Window` group and the four page tabs, whose emoji became monochrome lucide glyphs.
> 
> ### Still open on this issue
> 
> Completed-gameweek compression · kickoff numerals · home/away split scoreboard for match events · `WWD` → W/D/L badges · the standings banner → one-line + `ModelNote` · the italic tactical blocks.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-128</b> — Design Audit - Settings</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-136

> **Built 2026-09-13** (`817f22c`), verified in both auth states.
>
> **Built:** `Sign in` now leads the signed-out account menu as a filled button instead of a text link sitting last under the theme picker — verified menu order is now Sign in → Import squad → Pipeline health → Theme. `Status` → `Pipeline health` (in an FPL app, “status” most naturally reads as a player's or a team's). The Manager ID `Update` button becomes an outline once a manager is linked and stays inert until the value actually changes — verified disabled on load, enabled after an edit. `Unlink` picks up a danger-toned hover, since it drops the chat-id allowlist row that is the bot's access boundary and looked like every neutral button around it.
>
> **Verified already satisfied:** the theme buttons measure **51px**, above the 40px minimum the audit asks for.
>
> **Declined:** the two-column settings layout. The container is already `mx-auto` centred; the space either side of a `max-w-xl` column is a readable measure for a form, and widening it to fill 1280px would make it worse to read, not better.
>
> **Already in** [DSI-137](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-137/m9-sprint-c2-feature-shaped-items-from-the-audit)**:** the import helpers (paste-from-clipboard, visual DevTools walkthrough).

---

### 1. Account & Settings Pages (`Account-Details`, `Account-Import`, `Account-Notifications`)

#### Settings Shell & Sub-Tabs

* **Sub-Navigation Structure:** The tabs (`Account details`, `Import squad`, `Notifications`, `Pipeline`) sit inside an enclosed pill container. The active tab styling (`Account details`) uses green text inside a thin green outline, which gives it clear visual signifiers.
* **Dead Canvas Space:** On desktop viewports, the actual settings forms use a fixed narrow column width while the entire right half and lower 60% of the screen are completely blank canvas space. Centering this single-column container or pairing it side-by-side with an account overview sidebar will balance the layout.

#### Form Patterns & Component Fixes

* **Manager ID Update Flow (**`Account-Details`):
  * The field shows the ID `274486` with a bright green `#00FF87` `Update` button.
  * Because changing a Manager ID happens infrequently, an assertive primary green button over-emphasizes a rare setup task.
  * Use a secondary outline button or an inline checkmark state (`✓ Saved`) that only enables when the value has been edited.
* **Network Tab Instructions (**`Account-Import`):
  * The 5-step instructions explain: `Open DevTools (F12) and select the Network tab...`
  * This is an honest, functional fallback for an auth block, but asking users to parse raw JSON from network logs carries very high interaction cost.
  * Add a 1-click `[ Copy Curl / Fetch snippet ]` button or an inline visual GIF/screencast demonstrating the exact 3 clicks in browser DevTools.
  * In the JSON textarea, provide a dedicated `[ Paste from clipboard ]` button directly over the top-right corner of the input to remove manual keyboard shortcuts.
* **Notification Preferences (**`Account-Notifications`):
  * The toggle options (`Deadline reminder`, `Injury & availability`, `Team news`, `Price changes`, `Fixture changes`) use green checkboxes with em-dash subtext explanations.
  * The list format is clean and readable.
  * The `Unlink` button right beside `● Telegram linked` is styled identically to a neutral button. Give `Unlink` a subtle warning or red outline (`#FF4B4B`) to prevent accidental disconnection of the webhook.

### 2. User Profile Dropdown Flyout (`Profile-NotSigned`, `Profile-SignedIn`)

#### Flyout Placement & Visual Hierarchy

* **Anchor Alignment:** The dropdown aligns to the right edge of the header avatar (`DS` circle / user icon), which is the standard placement for profile chrome.
* **Menu Item Grouping:**
  * In the signed-in state, the menu items are stacked:
    * User header (`DS United`, email)
    * `Manage account`
    * `Import squad ›`
    * `Status`
    * `THEME` segmented controls (`Light`, `Dark`, `System`)
    * `Sign out`
  * "Status" is the former `/status` page merged into `/settings?tab=status` (Pipeline). Labeling it simply as "Status" is ambiguous: users might confuse it with their FPL team status or rank. Change this link to `System Status` or `Pipeline Health`.
* **Theme Selector:**
  * The three-part theme toggle (`Light`, `Dark`, `System`) features clean icons and clear active states (green outline on `System`).
  * Ensure the icon touch targets adhere to a minimum of 40px height to prevent misclicks on touchscreens or laptop trackpads.
* **Sign-In Callout in Signed-Out Menu (**`Profile-NotSigned`):
  * In the signed-out menu, `Sign in` sits at the very bottom below the Theme selector in bright neon green text.
  * For an unsigned visitor opening the user menu, signing in is the primary goal. Move `Sign in` to the top of the menu as a prominent primary button, and keep Theme/Status as secondary utility options below it.

### Settings & Profile Action Checklist

| **Component** | **UX Issue** | **Practical Fix** |
| -- | -- | -- |
| **Settings Layout** | Form controls leave 60% of the right and lower viewport empty. | Center the card container or use a balanced two-column settings layout. |
| **DevTools Import** | Text-heavy technical instructions create interaction friction. | Add a visual screenshot callout and a `[ Paste from Clipboard ]` helper button. |
| **Telegram Unlink** | Destructive action looks identical to neutral navigation buttons. | Add an amber or red outline hover state to signal unlinking. |
| **Profile Menu "Status"** | Generic name could be confused with team or player status. | Rename to `Pipeline Health` or `System Pipeline`. |
| **Signed-Out Flyout** | Primary `Sign in` action is buried at the bottom below Theme controls. | Place a prominent `Sign in` CTA at the top of the flyout menu. |

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Partly pre-delivered by DSI-138** (commit `4cc20d7` on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-builder`) — don't redo when this is picked up.
> 
> Done: the four sub-tabs (`Account details / Import squad / Notifications / Pipeline`) are now a `SegmentedControl` instead of a bespoke `tabButton` helper that was copied verbatim into `/transfers` and `RivalTable`.
> 
> ⚠️ **Needs a signed-in look before this issue closes.** The tab strip only renders for a signed-in user, so it could not be verified this session. One consequence is known from the code: the old buttons used `flex-1` to fill the container, while segmented controls are natural-width — so the strip will now sit **left-aligned rather than spanning the card**. That is consistent with every other control in the app, but it interacts directly with this issue's *"Dead Canvas Space"* item, so decide both together rather than separately.
> 
> Still open: centre or two-column the settings shell, `Update` → outline and enabled only when dirty, `Unlink` → `variant="destructive"`, rename `Status` → `Pipeline Health`, `Sign in` to the top of the signed-out flyout, ≥40px theme-toggle targets.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-129</b> — Design Audit - Overarching Recommendations</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-11 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Improvement · **Parent:** DSI-135

> **Closed out 2026-09-13** (`e9b1d76`), after checking each of the four items against the code rather than assuming C1 had covered them.
>
> **1. Reserve** `#00FF87` — the four named utility actions: `/builder`'s **Save** was already `tone="outline"` *and* disabled when nothing is dirty; **Open** (`/scenarios`) and **Update** (`/settings`) moved in C1; **Sync this league** was the last one and is now an outline matching `/team`'s Refresh. I then swept every remaining `dark:bg-primary` in `app/` and `components/`: all are progress-bar fills, selected-state card backgrounds, a status badge, the slider track, or the signed-out `Sign in` button — fills and states, not utility triggers, so they keep the accent.
>
> **2. Disclaimers into tooltips** — done by the `ModelNote` / `AnnotatedLabel` primitives this issue produced. C1 found and migrated the last hand-rolled call site (`/scenarios`' metric labels, which drew the dotted underline without the `cursor-help` that pairs with it).
>
> **3. Standard status badges** — `Badge` with `positive`/`warning`/`negative`/`neutral` tones; C1 adopted it at the remaining raw-coloured-text sites (compare-panel verdicts, transfer rationales, the import-discrepancy Alert).
>
> **4. ISO strings and table alignment** — fully covered by C1: no raw ISO renders anywhere (`/news`), numeric cells already right-aligned with two stray headers fixed (`/players`), `WWD` became W/D/L pips (`/fixtures` table), and `unknown` no longer appears.

---

### Global Design System Patterns to Unify Across [fpldecision.com](<http://fpldecision.com>)

1. **Reserve** `#00FF87` for True Decisions and Triggers:
   * Restrict your signature neon green to primary user actions (such as `[ Apply GW4 XI ]`, `[ Plan Path ]`) and top-tier positive deltas.
   * Tone down utility actions (`Save`, `Update`, `Sync this league`, `Open`) into refined ghost buttons with subtle borders.
2. **Move Algorithmic Disclaimers into Standard Tooltips:**
   * Across `/deadline`, `/builder`, `/fixtures`, and `/players`, large multi-line notes explain internal model logic.
   * Retain transparency while cutting visual clutter by placing these disclaimers inside consistent `ℹ️` tooltip popovers.
3. **Standardize Visual Status Badges:**
   * Establish a unified badge style for all price movements, injury flags, and transfers:
     * Positive / Rise / Optimal: `#00FF87` or subtle green container
     * Warning / Hit / Uncertainty: Amber container with yellow text
     * Danger / Drop / Injured: Subtle crimson container (`#FF4B4B`)
4. **Format ISO Strings and Alignment in Tables:**
   * Right-align all numeric data across Players, Leagues, and Fixtures.
   * Convert machine timestamps (`2027-01-06T20:00`) and unstyled text (`WWD`, `unknown`) into localized dates and status tokens.

</details>

Added some references and components in the References folder. Refer to them and utilize them whenever required. Do not commit this folder and Screens folder, gitignore them.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-12
>
> Verified and closed — no change was needed.
> 
> `/References/` and `/Screens/` were **already** gitignored, as the last two entries in `.gitignore`:
> 
> ```
> # Design-review inspiration/assets and current-app screenshots — working
> # material for a later design-review phase, not repo content.
> /References/
> /Screens/
> ```
> 
> Confirmed neither is tracked. Both have been read and used:
> 
> **`References/Components/` splits into two kinds, and they get used differently.**
> 
> *The 11 Transitions.dev files* (`accordion`, `tab-sliding`, `checkbox-check`, `number-pop`, `panel-reveal`, `tooltip-hover`, `shimmer-text`, `spinning-counter`, `streaming-text`, `text-state-swap`, `thinking-states`) are framework-agnostic CSS driven by custom properties, every one guarded with `prefers-reduced-motion`. Their timings became the motion scale in `app/globals.css` (`duration-*`, `ease-slide`/`ease-emphasis`/`ease-pop`), and `tab-sliding` became `components/ui/segmented-control.tsx`.
> 
> Worth recording: `accordion.md` uses the **exact** `grid-template-rows: 0fr↔1fr` technique `CollapsibleCard` and `ClubTacticsGrid` already used, on the same curve — independent confirmation that one was already right.
> 
> *The 9 React files* (`buton`, `hero`, `diff-table`, `filter-table`, `fine-tune-filter`, `insights-cards`, `context-cards`, `search`, `flowchart`) are demo components with hardcoded `ROWS`/`FILTERS` arrays, written against **a different design system** (`text-ink-2`, `bg-inset`, `bg-field`, `border-line`, `rounded-control`, `shadow-hairline`, `@/components/atoms/Button`). Installing them verbatim would import a second token vocabulary — precisely what Sprint A's 167 substitutions removed. They are mined for pattern, not copied.
> 
> Two were adapted and scoped to the landing page, where they genuinely fit: `hero.md` → `components/ui/background-paths.tsx` (framer-motion dropped; its `pathLength`/`pathOffset` animation is `stroke-dashoffset` in CSS) and `buton.md` → `components/ui/interactive-hover-button.tsx`.
> 
> **One reference deliberately not adopted:** `tooltip-hover.md` is hover-only, pure CSS. That contradicts a standing decision — `TapToReveal` exists *because* hover-only tooltips are unreachable on touch, and the 2026-08-22 audit response converted nine of them for that reason. Its timing asymmetry (150ms in / 50ms out / 80ms delay) was taken; its hover trigger was not.
> 
> `Screens/`' 47 PNGs are the before-picture for the C1 gate — each frame re-shot after its page changes and compared against the one the audit was written from.

<details><summary><b>DSI-134</b> — M9 Sprint A — Design foundations (primitives + tokens)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-12 · **Completed:** 2026-09-12 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Improvement

**Shipped 2026-09-12.** Commits `85ba01a`, `04e4298`, `268cc9f`.

The audit's page-level findings mostly share one cause: the token layer and the primitives existed, and almost nothing used them. `components/ui/button.tsx` was a complete 7-variant CVA Button with **one** importer. `lib/semantic-colors.ts` defined one treatment per semantic role with **zero** importers, against ~49 hand-rolled status colour tokens. This sprint built what was missing and made the token layer enforceable.

## Built

| Primitive | Replaces |
| -- | -- |
| `components/ui/badge.tsx` | ~49 ad-hoc status colours; four private colour maps sharing a byte-identical shell |
| `components/ui/alert.tsx` | 15–25 hand-built `border-amber-300 bg-amber-50 …` blocks |
| `components/ui/data-table.tsx` | 14 copy-pasted table shells. Adds a sticky **header** (every copy froze only the first column) and a numeric right-alignment convention |
| `components/ui/delta.tsx` | A signed number that knows which direction is good — takes `goodDirection` as an input |
| `components/ui/model-note.tsx` | Three inconsistent renderings of 26 `lib/` `*_NOTE` constants |
| `components/ui/segmented-control.tsx` | Five separate takes on "pick one of these" |

Plus a **motion scale** in `app/globals.css` (`duration-*`, `ease-slide`/`ease-emphasis`/`ease-pop`), from the [Transitions.dev](<http://Transitions.dev>) recipes in `References/Components/`.

## Raw hex in className: 167 → 0

Nearly all token-exact and dark-scoped (`#1E0234` = `--card`, `#2A0A45` = `--surface-3`, `#00FF87` = `--primary`) — a substitution, not a restyle. Four sites were not: three ad-hoc dark hover greens collapsed onto `--primary-hover`; the fixture day divider moved to `--surface-1`; the selected draft card adopted the app's one selected tint; and the pitch turf got a real `--pitch-turf` token.

**The ESLint rule is now** `error`**, not** `warn` — it shipped as a warning only because 169 call sites already violated it.

## Also

* [DSI-126](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-126/design-audit-players)'s slider: thumb stays a 14px dot, hit target to 26px.
* Two invalid-nesting defects fixed (a real `<p>`-in-`<p>` hydration error, and `<div>`s inside `TapToReveal`'s `<span>` panel).

## Gate

`tsc` clean · lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings, unchanged from baseline) · static export builds · verified in both themes on `/players`, `/fixtures`, `/builder`, `/`.

## Deliberately not done here

`Button` adoption across the ~100 hand-rolled call sites. Only **17** have a static `className`; the rest are conditional template literals encoding toggle/selected state. Converting them blind is a large diff whose only verification is "it still looks the same" — moved into Sprint C1, where each page is open and being visually checked anyway.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-135</b> — M9 Sprint B — Global sweeps (apply the primitives app-wide)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-12 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Improvement

Sprint A built the primitives; adoption is currently 1–2 files each. **This sprint is what makes them app-wide.** Each item is one pattern applied everywhere, cheap only because A landed.

Adoption at the start of this sprint: `Badge` 1 file · `Alert` 0 · `DataTable` 0 · `Delta` 1 · `ModelNote` 0 · `SegmentedControl` 2 · `Button` 1.

> **Measured 2026-09-13** (files importing each primitive), because the line above is what the sprint started from and had stopped describing the code: `Button` **20** · `Badge` **9** · `SegmentedControl` **9** · `Alert` **6** · `ModelNote` **5** · `Delta` **2** · `DataTable` **0**. Two primitives have been added since this issue was written and are fully adopted: `Checkbox` (5 sites, every checkbox in the app) and `NoteDisclosure` (3 sites, see [DSI-140](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-140/long-methodology-explainers-still-render-inline-collapse-them-behind)).

## Status per item, measured rather than remembered

| # | Item | State |
| -- | -- | -- |
| 1 | Accent discipline | **Largely done.** Save/Update/Sync/Open/Refresh are `variant="outline"`; `/settings` Update is `variant={entryId ? "outline" : "default"}` so the first link keeps the filled treatment. Metric tiles and the data-viz greens still want a pass. |
| 2 | Status badges | **Largely done** — `Badge` in 9 files. |
| 3 | Numeric alignment via `DataTable` | **Untouched.** 0 importers; ~15 files still hand-roll `<table>`. |
| 4 | Date/time formatting | **Done.** `change-feed.ts`'s `slice(0, 16)` is gone; `formatDateTime`/`formatDay`/`formatTime`/`signed` live in `lib/utils.ts`. |
| 5 | `title=` → `TapToReveal` | **Barely started.** 87 attributes left of 88. `TapToReveal` appears in 14 files, so it has been *added alongside* `title=` rather than replacing it. |
| 6 | `ModelNote` adoption | **~1/5 done.** 5 importers against 27 `*_NOTE` constants in `lib/`. |
| 7 | Empty-value hygiene | **Done.** The literal `unknown` is gone from `app/players/page.tsx`. |
| 8 | `Alert` adoption | **Largely done** — 6 files. |

So the remaining weight of this sprint is items **3, 5, 6** and the tail of **1**.

## Scope

1. **Accent discipline** ([DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations) #1). `--primary` reserved for primary actions and the single top-tier winner. Demote to outline: `Save`, `Update`, `Sync this league`, `Open`, `Refresh`. Demote to plain foreground: `/team`'s metric tiles, `/transfers`' `5 GW` column, `/players`' `xP GW` column, `manager-profile-card`'s percentile bars (a data-viz green, not the CTA green).
2. **Status badges** ([DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations) #3). Every price move, injury flag and xP delta through `Badge`/`Delta`. Sites: `app/players/page.tsx:830-847`, `/news` price rows, `/deadline` price watch, `player-status-icons.tsx`.
3. **Numeric alignment** ([DSI-126](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-126/design-audit-players)/127/129 #4). Right-align every numeric cell via `DataTable`; keep `tabular-nums` (already consistent at ~135 sites, just never tied to alignment).
4. **Date/time formatting** ([DSI-122](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-122/design-audit-news)/127/129 #4). `lib/change-feed.ts:65` does `String(d.new).slice(0, 16)` — the literal source of the `2027-01-06T20:00` the audit flags. Shared formatter in `lib/utils.ts`, used by the kickoff-change feed and `/fixtures`' kickoff pills.
5. `title=` **→** `TapToReveal` ([DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations) #2). 88 attributes across 28 files, 0 `aria-describedby` today. Heaviest: `chip-timing.tsx` (8), `/builder` (8), `/transfers` (6).
6. `ModelNote` **adoption.** All 26 `lib/` `*_NOTE` constants, anchored to the figure each qualifies — which is how [DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations)'s tooltip ask and CLAUDE.md's "surface it next to the number" rule both hold.
7. **Empty-value hygiene** ([DSI-126](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-126/design-audit-players)). `app/players/page.tsx:833` renders the literal word `unknown` for `PriceVerdict === "unknown"`; render `—` and put the explanation in the column's `ModelNote`. Same for `player-detail.tsx`'s em-dash clutter.
8. `Alert` **adoption.** The hand-built callouts, including [DSI-120](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-120/design-audit-my-team)'s yellow discrepancy string and [DSI-127](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-127/design-audit-fixtures)'s brown standings banner.

## Gate

`tsc` · lint 0 errors · build · `responsive-check` matrix on every page touched, both themes · signed-in **and** signed-out for `/team`, `/settings`, `/`, `/news`.

**No engine behaviour may move.** Presentation only; anything landing in `lib/` beyond formatters and `semantic-colors.ts` gets the `engine-verify` harness.

## Swept 2026-09-13 — items 3, 5, 6 and the tail of 1

**#3 numeric alignment.** All sixteen tables now use the DataTable primitives; raw `<td>` count across `app/` and `components/` is **0**, importers 0 → 14. Three tables take the full shell (pipeline status, accuracy scoreboard, league table); the rest take cells only, because they sit inside cards that already own a border and a background and DataTable's sticky header is inert without a scroll container — inert but present reads as done.

**#6 ModelNote.** The issue's premise no longer held and its measurement counted the wrong thing. All 22 model notes that are actually used were *already* anchored in a disclosure (InfoTooltip / ModelNote / TapToReveal / NoteDisclosure), so "surface it next to the number" already held. What was real: ModelNote is a thin wrapper over InfoTooltip that owns the note typography, and 17 call sites re-typed `text-xs leading-relaxed` by hand. Fourteen now go through ModelNote; importers 5 → 12.

**#5 title= → TapToReveal.** Scoped here as "88 attributes across 28 files"; measured, only **14** titles exceed 40 characters and most of those are a truncation fallback (full name, email, source filename — where `title` is the right idiom) or a hint on a button that already has a visible label. Four carried reasoning available nowhere else and were converted: transfer-plan's 152-character "value of waiting" label, compare-panel's best-cell marker, manager-leagues' rank movement, and player-detail's club-system line, which truncates exactly where the caveat starts. Left alone deliberately: the score pill's "bonus tbc" (expanded on screen beside it) and the pinned badge (its aria-label already carries the sentence).

**#1 accent tail.** The Past Seasons bar on /team was a hand-copy of `PercentileBar` and still had `bg-purple-700 / dark:bg-primary` — the fix [DSI-120](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-120/design-audit-my-team) asked for had been made once and missed once. Now `--chart-1` (verified: renders rgb(34,211,238)). /team's metric tiles and /players' xP column were checked and are already plain foreground.

### One conflict left for a human, not resolved unilaterally

This issue asks to demote **/transfers' 5 GW column** to plain foreground. [DSI-125](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-125/design-audit-transfers-chip-strategy) has already been here: that column *used* to paint every projection `--primary`, and [DSI-124](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-124/design-audit-transfers-transfers-strategy) narrowed it to the squad's own top quartile (via `quantile` in lib/stats.ts) precisely so the accent meant something again. Demoting now would delete the distinction [DSI-124](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-124/design-audit-transfers-transfers-strategy) built, and the obvious middle path — moving it to `--chart-1` like the bars — fails contrast in light mode (3.7:1 on white, against 5.1:1 on the dark card), so it would trade one defect for a worse one. Left as [DSI-124](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-124/design-audit-transfers-transfers-strategy) set it; worth an explicit decision rather than a sweep.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **The one conflict left for a human is resolved** (DSI-141, commit `df431d7`), and the premise it was parked on was wrong in a way worth recording.
> 
> The note rejected `--chart-1` as the middle path because it *"fails contrast in light mode (3.7:1 on white)"*. But `/transfers`' top-quartile column is `text-purple-800 dark:text-primary` — **light mode was never `--chart-1` and was never the complaint.** Only the dark half borrowed the CTA accent. Measuring the wrong half is what made the conflict look unresolvable.
> 
> So: light stays `purple-800`, dark moves to `--chart-1`. Measured in the running app rather than taken from the token — the cell computes `rgb(34, 211, 238)` against the painted ground `rgb(14, 1, 24)`, which is **11.21:1**, not the 5.1:1 the note assumed against the card (rows are transparent; the card colour is not what it sits on).
> 
> DSI-124's top-quartile distinction survives, DSI-129's accent discipline holds, and nothing was traded for it. The picker-open row ring at `app/transfers/page.tsx:1152` keeps `dark:ring-primary/50` deliberately — that is a selected-state fill, which DSI-129's own sweep exempted.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Sprint B status — 6 commits on `feat/m9-sprint-b-global-sweeps`.**
>

> ### Done
> 
> 1. **Accent discipline** — `/team`'s six metric tiles, `/players`' Pts and xP GW columns, `manager-profile-card`'s percentile bars. `/transfers`' squad xP keeps the accent but only for the top quartile, via `lib/stats.ts`'s `quantile` — the squad's own P75, not an invented cutoff. Fixing the bars required fixing `--chart-1`, which was byte-identical to `--primary` in dark and a near-invisible grey in light.
> 2. **Status badges** — price moves and availability through `Badge`/the new `FeedRowItem`.
> 3. **Numeric alignment** — `/players` (16 cells + header helper), league table, draft comparison.
> 4. **Date formatting** — shared formatters in `lib/utils.ts`; `lib/change-feed.ts:65`'s `String(d.new).slice(0, 16)` was the literal source of `2027-01-06T20:00`.
> 5. **`ModelNote`** — the two worst footnote blocks, anchored to the heading they qualify.
> 6. **Empty-value hygiene** — `/players`' literal `unknown` → em-dash + Badge.

> 
> ### Two items resolved differently from the scope
> 
> **`title=` → `TapToReveal` was the wrong fix and is not implemented as written.** The badges carrying most of those 88 titles render *per row and per cell* — 160 fixture cells in the FDR matrix alone, plus a status flag and up to three set-piece icons on every `/players` row. Converting them would add several hundred focusable buttons to one page and make keyboard traversal worse than the hover gap it closed.
> 
> The real defect was different and worse: `player-status-icons.tsx` had **no `aria-label`, no `role`, no `aria-hidden` anywhere**, and `title` on a `<span>` is not reliably announced — so an injury flag and a fixture's difficulty were invisible to a screen reader on *every* device, not just touch. Fixed with `role="img"` + `aria-label`, `title` kept for hover. Verified: 165 elements expose an accessible name on `/fixtures`, and matrix cells now read `GW5 · COV away at NFO · FDR 3 — Medium` rather than `NFO`. The titles that genuinely warrant a popover are multi-sentence reasoning on one-off controls — per-page work, moved to C1.
> 
> **`/players` pagination** was not in scope and turned out to be needed: the page hard-capped at `rows.slice(0, 100)` with no pager, so 557 of 657 players were unreachable on the page whose job is exploring them. `Pager` lifted out of `/builder` rather than copied; `/builder` now renders it too.

## From [DSI-122](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-122/design-audit-news) (News), 2026-09-13

Both deferred out of the C1 visual pass because they need data the page doesn't currently hold, not styling:

* **"My Squad Only" filter on** `/news`**.** The audit's highest-value News item: an FPL manager's first question is "does this affect my 15?". `/news` has no squad state today — it would need the `TeamState`/claimed-entry wiring that `/team` and `/deadline` have. Applies to the change feed and the RSS feed alike.
* **BGW/DGW annotation on a fixture move.** A cross-round move already renders `GW20 → GW21` rather than "Time change", so the *fact* is shown; what's missing is whether that leaves a team blank or doubled in either round. Needs per-team fixture counting per gameweek, which this page doesn't do — `fixtureCellsByTeam` in `lib/fdr.ts` already handles blanks and doubles and is the obvious thing to reuse.

## Done 2026-09-13

All four remaining items shipped.

1. **My squad only on /news.** The page now resolves a squad the way /deadline and /transfers do and filters both halves — change feed and RSS headlines. Matching is by player *code*: `change_feed.player_code` and `news_feed`'s player entity ids are codes, a draft stores element ids, and one id-scoped query bridges them. A fixture move carries no player, so it matches on the squad's clubs instead. The toggle only appears when there is a squad to filter to. Measured on the test squad: feed 200 rows → 5, headlines 145 → 17.
2. **Player panel actions on /deadline.** Set C / Set VC write to the same draft the chip plan and free-transfer count already write to. Remove is deliberately *not* offered — on that page the squad is the real team before a deadline, and a removal with no replacement is not a move FPL allows; Replace hands off to /transfers via a new `?replace=<playerId>`. /team stays read-only: its squad is historical FPL picks, not a draft, so there is nothing there to edit.
3. **Apply to plan.** "Best single play" was the headline recommendation and the only thing on the page you could not act on. It now has a button. Separately, every control on that page wrote `source: "shortlist"` — including ones applying a whole schedule, which is a decision, not a maybe. The rule is now: a bare pin icon shortlists, a labelled Apply button decides. No behaviour moves (nothing branches on manual vs shortlist; only `"fpl"` is load-bearing).
4. **Import helpers.** Paste-from-clipboard, feature-detected after mount, plus a drawn schematic of the DevTools Network panel with the four steps numbered on it. Drawn rather than screenshotted so it themes with the app and does not go stale with Chrome's UI.

Also closed the second [DSI-122](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-122/design-audit-news) deferral: a cross-round fixture move now reports the blank or double it creates. Not verifiable on screen — all 60 fixture rows in the live feed are kickoff-time changes — so it was checked by running the real module in a tsx harness over fabricated schedules.

<details><summary><b>DSI-138</b> — M9 Sprint B — Unify every pick-one control on SegmentedControl</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-13 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** No priority · **Parent:** DSI-135

Owner-raised during the C1 Builder pass: *"the horizon switcher in builder looks different to the season switcher in fixtures->FDR, but essentially they have the same function."* Then: *"do this for the xP/Points one and also for any sort of toggle."*

Landed on `feat/m9-sprint-c1-builder` as two commits — `18a9af1` (horizon) and `4cc20d7` (every other toggle).

## What the app had

Four different affordances for one job.

**Horizon picker, 6 call sites:**

| Screen | Was |
| -- | -- |
| `/builder` | bordered strip, `--primary` fill |
| `/players`, `/scenarios` | `bg-purple-950` variant |
| `/transfers` | a third border treatment |
| `DecisionAnalyticsPanel` (`/team`) | a plain `<select>` |
| `/fixtures`, `/deadline` | already `SegmentedControl` |

**Other pick-one controls, 6 more:** `/scenarios` xP ↔ Points scored · `/builder` 5/10/20 replacement count · `/news` feed-source pills (`Button variant="toggle"`, sitting directly under an already-segmented filter row, so one page carried two shapes for the same job) · `/settings` four tabs · `/transfers` sub-tabs · `RivalTable` season/career.

The last three were byte-identical `tabButton` helpers — same `bg-purple-950` / `dark:bg-emerald-950/60` selected state, defined three times.

## Decisions

1. **New** `components/horizon-control.tsx`, not five `SegmentedControl` calls. `Horizon` is `1 | 3 | 5 | 8 | 19 | "season"`, so it can't be a segment value directly; the string round-trip and the `"season"` special case live in one place. `semantics="radio"` — the horizon re-scores what is on screen, it does not swap panels.
2. **The FDR** `Sort` `<select>` **is deliberately NOT converted.** Three options, one conditionally disabled with a `"(not published yet)"` suffix. That is a select's job — a segmented control has nowhere to put the explanation. Do not "finish" this in a later pass.
3. `manager-leagues` **rows carry** `aria-pressed` **but are not a toggle group** — a list you pick from. Left alone.
4. **Per-segment** `title=` **could not survive**, since `SegmentedOption` has no title. Both losses were re-homed as visible text: `/scenarios` now renders `seasonHorizonNote` below the control (matching `/players` and `/transfers`) and says why "Points scored" is disabled when no gameweek has finished. A disabled control that never says why is a dead end.

## Bug introduced and fixed in-session

`HorizonControl` put the control in a flex row. A flex item defaults to `min-width: auto`, which refuses to shrink below its content — so `SegmentedControl`'s own `max-w-full overflow-x-auto` could never engage. Measured **395px of body inside a 375px viewport** on `/transfers`, `/builder` and `/scenarios`, with "Season" clipped off the right edge.

Fix: `min-w-0` on the row, on the control, and on the two page-level flex containers that hold one. Re-measured 375 = 375 on all four pages.

**Generalise this:** the whole ancestor chain has to permit shrinking. Anything else adopting `SegmentedControl` inside a flex row needs the same treatment — the component's own `max-w-full` is not sufficient on its own.

## Pre-delivers part of four C1 issues

Recorded as comments on each so they are not redone: [DSI-122](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-122/design-audit-news) (source pills), [DSI-123](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-123/design-audit-scenarios) (dual toggle), [DSI-124](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-124/design-audit-transfers-transfers-strategy) (tab nav distinct from the draft selector), [DSI-128](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-128/design-audit-settings) (settings sub-tabs).

## Verification

`tsc` clean · lint 0 errors (4 pre-existing warnings, 2 fewer than before — dead `HORIZONS`/`horizonLabel`/`Button` imports went with the buttons) · `npm run build` exports. Clicked through `/scenarios` (indicator slides, data switches to GW1–GW3 actuals), `/transfers`, `/news` feeds, `/builder`, `/players` — zero console output on a fresh tab, dark and light, 375px and desktop.

**Not verified — both signed-in only:** the `/settings` tab strip and `RivalTable`. One known consequence there: the old settings tabs used `flex-1` to fill the container width; segmented controls are natural-width, so that strip will now sit left-aligned. Consistent with everything else, but a visible change worth a look on the next signed-in pass.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-139</b> — draft_snapshots cloud sync fails silently — upsert hits a missing UPDATE policy</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-13 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Priority:** High

> **Fixed 2026-09-13** (`5b1e432`) with option 1 — `ignoreDuplicates: true`, per your call.
>
> **Verified against the live database, not by reading the diff.** With a row guaranteed to conflict:
>
> | Prefer | Result |
> | -- | -- |
> | `resolution=merge-duplicates` (old behaviour) | **403** — `42501 new row violates row-level security policy (USING expression)` |
> | `resolution=ignore-duplicates` (new behaviour) | **201** |
>
> Then end to end through the app, signed in: renaming a draft took the test account from **7 snapshots across 7 drafts to 8 across 7** — a second snapshot on a single draft, which was impossible while the statement was aborting. That 7-and-7 baseline is itself the fingerprint of the bug: the first insert per draft landed, and every push after it died on the conflict.
>
> `team_drafts` immediately above keeps `merge-duplicates` and its UPDATE policy — a draft's payload genuinely does change, so that one is correct as written.
>
> **Left open deliberately:** the second half of this report — the failure is discoverable only in the console. A sync path that cannot write should say so, the way `/team`'s `SignedOutSyncError` does. Worth its own issue if you want it.

---

Found 2026-09-13 while auditing `/scenarios` signed in as the test account, which is the first time anything has exercised repeated draft sync for a signed-in user.

## What happens

Every push of an existing snapshot is rejected by RLS. The browser console shows:

```
draft_snapshots push failed: new row violates row-level security policy
(USING expression) for table "draft_snapshots"
```

with a 403. It is only ever `console.error`'d (`lib/draft-sync.ts:126`), so nothing surfaces in the UI — draft history simply stops replicating to the cloud, and the user is told nothing.

## Why

`lib/draft-sync.ts:124` does:

```ts
.from("draft_snapshots").upsert(snapshotRows, { onConflict: "draft_id,at" })
```

An upsert that hits the conflict takes the **UPDATE** path. `draft_snapshots` has no UPDATE policy — confirmed against the live database:

| policy | cmd | using | with check |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| Insert own | INSERT | — | `auth.uid() = user_id` |
| Select own | SELECT | `auth.uid() = user_id` | — |
| Delete own | DELETE | `auth.uid() = user_id` | — |

That is deliberate — `20260809190000_sprint14_auth_ownership.sql` gives snapshots select/insert/delete and no update, because a snapshot is an immutable point in time. The unique index on `(draft_id, at)` came later (`20260809192335_sprint14_draft_snapshots_unique.sql`), and the upsert written against it assumes an UPDATE path the policies never granted.

## Two candidate fixes, and they differ in meaning

1. **Keep snapshots immutable** and stop trying to update them — `.upsert(..., { onConflict: "draft_id,at", ignoreDuplicates: true })`, which emits `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` and needs no UPDATE policy. This matches the intent of the original migration: the same `(draft_id, at)` is by definition the same snapshot, so there is nothing to overwrite.
2. Add an owner-scoped UPDATE policy. Only correct if a snapshot's payload can legitimately change after the fact — which, if true, means `at` is not really identifying the snapshot.

Option 1 looks right, but it is a statement about what a snapshot *is*, so it wants a decision rather than a patch.

## Also worth fixing either way

The failure is silent. A sync path that cannot write should not be discoverable only by opening the console — compare `SignedOutSyncError` on `/team`, which surfaces.

## Verifying

Sign in (`/signed-in` skill), create a draft, edit it twice so two snapshots share a `(draft_id, at)` key on re-push, and watch the console. After a fix, the push should succeed with no error and `select count(*) from draft_snapshots where user_id = …` should grow.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Second half closed** (DSI-141, commit `df431d7`) — the failure now has a user-visible surface.
> 
> Two things, because the report understated the first:
> 
> 1. The snapshot push set **no** `result.error` at all, only a `console.error` — so the failure could not reach `SyncResult` even in principle, and the caller had nothing to render. It now sets one (`Draft history did not sync: …`) without failing the sync, since the drafts themselves did push.
> 2. `lib/draft-sync.ts` publishes the last sync outcome (`getSyncError` / `onSyncStatusChanged`), and `DraftSyncProvider` renders a dismissible `Alert tone="warning"` when it is set: cloud sync failed, this device is the only copy, nothing has been lost locally. A module-level value with listeners rather than a second context — `watchAndSync` is already a module-level loop started once at the root, and threading a provider through it would be two mechanisms for one fact.
> 
> **Not looked at on screen.** Forcing a real failure needs a session for the test account, and minting one was refused by the sandbox (credential materialization). Typechecked, linted and built; the render path itself is unexercised. Worth a look on the next signed-in pass before this is called done.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-140</b> — Long methodology explainers still render inline — collapse them behind the card the app already has</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-13 · **Completed:** 2026-09-13 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Improvement

Reported 2026-09-13 from `/transfers`. The transfer path closes with ~150 words of methodology set as one unbroken 10px paragraph — bounded search, per-gameweek valuation, top-3 replacements per slot, frozen prices, decision margin, "the shape of a plan, not an instruction". Every sentence is worth keeping; none of it is worth reading on the way to the next thing.

## The pattern already exists

`components/chip-timing.tsx:786` does this correctly:

```tsx
<CollapsibleCard title="Fixture flatness" tier="amber" summary={result.noteSummary}>
  <p className="text-xs leading-relaxed …">{result.note}</p>
</CollapsibleCard>
```

Collapsed it reads *"Fixture flatness — Every gameweek through GW38 currently plays once, values read comparatively flat"*, and the full paragraph is one click away. `components/ui/collapsible-card.tsx` already takes `title`, a one-line `summary`, `tier` and `defaultOpen`.

So this is adoption, not new design.

## Sites that still render inline

| Site | Content |
| -- | -- |
| `components/transfer-path.tsx:212` | `Ran N simulations…` + `result.note` — the one in the screenshot |
| `app/transfers/page.tsx:1430` | `TRANSFER_MODEL_NOTE` |
| `app/transfers/page.tsx:1433` | price-watch explainer + `PRICE_WATCH_MODEL_NOTE` |

Each wants a one-line `summary` that carries the load-bearing sentence, so the collapsed state still says the thing a reader must not miss. For the transfer path that is roughly *"a bounded search over frozen prices — the shape of a plan, not an instruction"*; the rest can sit behind the toggle.

## Why this is not just [DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations) again

[DSI-129](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-129/design-audit-overarching-recommendations) moved the `*_MODEL_NOTE` constants into `ModelNote` / `InfoTooltip`, and that sweep is done — the notes on `/leagues`, `/builder`, `/deadline`, `/scenarios` are all inside disclosures now. These three are the case that sweep did not cover: prose composed in the component rather than a named constant, and too long for a tooltip even if it were one. A tooltip is the wrong container at this length; a collapsed card is the right one.

## Checking it

Sign in (`/signed-in`), open `/transfers`, re-plan a path. The methodology should be collapsed with a summary that still names the bound. Then `node scripts/capture-screens.mjs transfers` and compare `Screens/Transfers-Path.png` against the frame this issue was raised from.

## Resolution (2026-09-13)

All three sites now collapse. Done with a new `components/ui/note-disclosure.tsx` rather than `CollapsibleCard` — a deliberate deviation from the plan above, worth recording:

* `components/transfer-plan.tsx` had **already** solved this for its own note with a compact inline disclosure (chevron + one truncated line, no card chrome). Adopting `CollapsibleCard` at the other three sites would have left two shapes for the same job; lifting transfer-plan's shape into `NoteDisclosure` leaves one, which is what "one quantity, one implementation" asks for.
* A footnote under a table is not a card. `CollapsibleCard` brings a title, a tier and a border that reads as a section of its own — heavier than a methodology note that should sit quietly under the thing it qualifies.

The one thing the plan asked for that this does **not** do is a hand-written one-line `summary` per site: the collapsed state truncates the note's own first line instead. For the transfer path that reads "Ran N simulations beyond the deadline's own search. This is a bounded search, not the best possible sequence…", which does carry the bound — but it is the note's opening, not a curated summary. If the truncated line turns out to bury the load-bearing sentence anywhere, the fix is a `summary` prop on `NoteDisclosure`, not a second container.

Verified by re-shooting `Screens/Transfers-Path.png` against the frame this issue was raised from.

</details>
