> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Signed-in pass done** (`7b4f09b`), using the test account — and it found two real defects, both of which a signed-out pass structurally could not see.
> 
> ### 1. The career rivals table was still widening the page
> 
> Item 3 was **not** actually fixed by the first commit. Giving the table its own `DataTable` scroll container was necessary and not sufficient: below `lg` the grid has no `minmax(0,…)`, so its item kept the default `min-width: auto`, refused to shrink under its content, and the wrapper's `overflow-x: auto` never had anything to engage against.
> 
> | | body.scrollWidth |
> |---|---|
> | This season | 529 (= viewport) |
> | → Career, before the fix | **593** |
> | → Career, after | **375 = 375**, table scrolling inside a 310px box |
> 
> This is precisely the lesson DSI-138 wrote down for `SegmentedControl` — *the whole ancestor chain has to permit shrinking, the component's own rule is not sufficient* — and it has now cost two sprints. Worth treating as a checklist item rather than a memory.
> 
> ### 2. The selected segment never scrolled into view on a hidden document
> 
> `/settings`' Pipeline tab sat at `scrollLeft 0` with the tab off-screen, while the identical call with `behavior: "auto"` landed at 92. Cause: a hidden document runs no rAF callbacks, so `scrollTo({behavior: "smooth"})` is silently dropped. The preview pane reports `document.hidden === true`, which is how it surfaced — but any backgrounded tab is the same, and a control that is only reachable if an animation ran is not reachable. Now instant when hidden or when motion is reduced, smooth otherwise. Same rule `SlideOver` already states about its own entry animation.
> 
> ### Answering the two questions directly
> 
> - **Rivals → Career:** fixed and measured, see above. Only the data section scrolls; the card does not stretch.
> - **The unlinked Telegram icon, signed in: yes, it shows.** Muted paper-plane at the end of the `Refresh · Change ID · Import as draft →` row, `aria-label="Telegram not linked — open Telegram settings"`, `title="Telegram not linked"`. Tapping it opens the panel with *"Link a Telegram chat to get deadline, price and availability alerts."* and the **Link Telegram →** button, anchored at x=8 w=320, fully inside a 375px viewport.
> 
> Also verified in the same session:
> 
> - **`/settings`' tab strip** — the left-aligned strip DSI-138 predicted, now with a working scroll affordance; the left fade shows once you move off the first tab, both fades mid-scroll.
> - **DSI-139's alert renders.** It fired for real during this pass — a `TypeError: Failed to fetch` on the sync — and read: *"Cloud sync failed, so this device is the only copy of your recent draft changes. Nothing has been lost locally."* That is the surface the issue asked for, exercised rather than reasoned about, though by a network failure rather than the original RLS one.
> 
> The dev session file and the minting script were deleted afterwards; nothing from either is in the commit.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-13
>
> **Built 2026-09-13** — commits `df431d7`, `850ee04` on `fix/dsi-141-mobile-bugs`. All six, plus M9's two open items folded in on your call.
> 
> Every one of the six is the same shape, which is worth naming: **a control that fits by scrolling or wrapping silently.** Nothing is clipped, `document.body.scrollWidth` equals the viewport on all of them, and the options are unreachable anyway — so M9's own responsive matrix, whose bar was "nothing overflows", kept passing them.
> 
> | # | Finding | Outcome |
> |---|---|---|
> | 1 | Pill strips hide options | Fixed in `SegmentedControl` itself, so all nine call sites inherit it: edge fades that render only when a side actually has something hidden, and the selected segment scrolled into view on mount and on change. Measured on `/news`: 521px of segments in a 343px scroller, right fade on; after selecting the last segment, scrollLeft 177 of 178 and the fade flips to the left side alone |
> | 1.3 | "My squad only" is a page setting | Moved beside the **What Changed?** title |
> | 2 | Header actions askew | Telegram is now one icon button (`--primary` linked, muted unlinked, with the state in its `aria-label` and `title` — colour is never the only channel), opening the status and Link/Unlink in an anchored panel. Refresh + Change ID + Import + icon now sit on one row at 375px |
> | 3 | Career rivals scrolls the page | Both rival tables take the `DataTable` shell (`overflow-x-auto` + a `minWidth`), so the table scrolls and the document does not |
> | 4 | Squad dropdown crowds Save | Capped to 144px, matching the rename field, so the row does not reflow when you switch into rename mode. Verified: `[New draft ▾] [✎] [Save ▾]` on one line |
> | 5 | Frozen panes too wide | `/players`' pinned cell capped to 9.5rem below `sm` with the name truncating (full name already on `title`); `/compare`'s metric column 9rem → 7.5rem |
> | 5 | Multiple set-piece roles | Top duty plus a `+N`, in the priority `StatusBadge` already encodes (penalty → free kick → corner). The extras open in a `TapToReveal`, not a `title`: it is the only place they exist, and hover is unreachable on the device this was filed from |
> | 6 | FDR filters before the data | Search, Sort, Rating and the legend move behind the existing `FilterDisclosure`, with `activeCount` and a plain-text note outside it for every non-default choice. **Window stays out** — it changes what the table *is*. The matrix now starts ~340px down instead of below the fold |
> 
> ### Two bugs that only running it could find
> 
> Both under the new `+N` chip, both invisible in a diff:
> 
> 1. `/builder`'s picker makes the **whole row a `<button>`**, so the chip nested a button inside a button — invalid HTML and a React hydration error. The chip is now a plain titled span unless a caller opts in with `revealable`; `/players` and `/compare` opt in, `/builder` and `player-detail` do not.
> 2. `TapToReveal`'s panel is `fixed z-30` and was still painted over by the **next row's** `sticky z-10` cell. `fixed` positions against the viewport but does not escape a stacking context, and equal z-indexes are settled by DOM order. It portals to `document.body` now — which is what `fixed z-30` already claimed to mean.
> 
> ### The lookback "doing nothing" — measured, not assumed
> 
> You reported 1 GW and 3 GW returning the same figures. A `tsx` harness against entry 274486 before touching anything:
> 
> ```
> horizon 1: in 5 out 8 hits 0 net -3
>   GW4: 1 transfer · window 0/1 scored
>   GW3: 2 transfers · window 1/1 scored
> horizon 3 / horizon 5: identical totals
>   GW4: window 0/3 (0/5) scored · GW3: window 1/3 (1/5) scored
> ```
> 
> The window construction is correct: only *scored* gameweeks count, and only GW3 has any, so every lookback sums the same football. The defect was that the unchanged number explained nothing. It now states its own coverage — *"1 of 10 gameweeks in these lookback windows have been scored, so the verdicts are not final — and a longer lookback cannot change them until more gameweeks finish."* Renamed **Transfers lookback** per your note; "horizon" is this app's word for a projection.
> 
> ### Not verified — and why
> 
> The signed-in half. Minting a session for the dev test account was refused by the sandbox twice (credential materialization), and I stopped rather than route around it. So these are built and typechecked but not looked at:
> 
> - `/team` **Rivals → Career** (item 3's actual surface)
> - The Telegram **icon and panel** signed in (signed out, compact correctly renders nothing — unchanged behaviour)
> - `/settings`' tab strip — which DSI-138 had already flagged as unverified and expected to sit left-aligned now
> - The draft-sync alert from DSI-139's second half
> 
> Everything else was driven at 375px in both themes and at 1280px: body width equals viewport on every page touched, no fades and the frozen column back to auto width on desktop, console clean apart from a pre-existing `player_predictions` CORS failure unrelated to this change.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-142</b> — generate-predictions 401s on ~1 run in 3, and writes no sync_runs row when it does</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-14 · **Completed:** 2026-09-21 · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Bug · **Parent:** DSI-182

Found 2026-09-14 while verifying DSI-59a's `verify_jwt` deploy. **It is not caused by that deploy** — see below — but the deploy is what made anyone look at these logs.

## What happens

`fpl-generate-predictions` fires on `5,35 * * * *`. Roughly one run in three comes back **401** and the xP tables simply do not refresh. Last six hours, from `function_edge_logs`:

| Time (UTC) | Status |
| -- | -- |
| 18:05 | 200 |
| 18:35 | 200 |
| 19:05 | 200 |
| **19:35** | **401** |
| 20:05 | 200 |
| 20:35 | 200 |
| **21:05** | **500** |
| 21:35 | 200 |
| **22:05** | **401** |
| 22:35 | 200 |
| 23:05 | 200 |
| **23:35** | **401** |
| **00:05** | **401** |

Nine successes, four 401s, one 500 out of thirteen scheduled runs.

## Why nothing surfaced it

The function refuses **before** writing its `sync_runs` row, so a failed run leaves no trace anywhere the app looks. `/settings` → Pipeline shows only the successes and reports the function healthy. `cron.job_run_details` also says `succeeded` for every one of these, because pg_cron only records that the dispatch statement ran, not what the endpoint answered.

This is the third instance of the shape Sprint 36 named: **work that silently does not happen and renders as absence.**

## What it is not

* **Not the** `verify_jwt` **flip.** Three of the four 401s (19:35, 22:05, 23:35) happened while `verify_jwt = false`. Same rate before and after.
* **Not a missing or wrong key.** `request.sb.apikey.authorization.prefix` is `sb_publishable_EyX73` and the user agent `pg_net/0.20.4` on the 200s and the 401s alike — identical caller, identical header.
* **Not a gateway rejection.** The 401s carry `execution_time_ms` of **6295, 6425, 7891** — the function booted and ran for 6–8 seconds before answering 401. A gateway refusal returns in ~0ms and never starts the function. (This is also the positive proof that `verify_jwt = true` is accepting the cron caller.)

## So what is it

The 401 is the function's own `_shared/cron-auth.ts` refusing after several seconds of work — which points at the `x-cron-secret` comparison or the Vault read behind it failing intermittently, not at the caller. The successful runs take 18–20s; the failures die at 6–8s, so whatever it is happens partway through a path that normally completes.

Worth checking first: whether `invoke_sync` is reading the secret from Vault per-call and that read can time out, and whether two overlapping invocations can collide (the successes take 18–20s against a 30-minute schedule, so overlap seems unlikely but has not been ruled out).

## Verifying a fix

`function_edge_logs` is the only honest source here:

```sql
select timestamp, log_attributes['response.status_code'] as status
from logs
where source = 'function_edge_logs'
  and log_attributes['request.pathname'] like '%generate-predictions%'
order by timestamp desc
```

Fixed means a full day of scheduled runs with no 401. Separately, and regardless of the cause: **a refused run should leave a** `sync_runs` **row**, or the Pipeline page will go on reporting a function healthy while a third of its runs never happen.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-143</b> — Decide whether to keep graft's UserPromptSubmit prompt hints</summary>

**State:** In Review · **Created:** 2026-09-14 · **Priority:** Low

Graft was wired into the repo on 2026-09-14 (commits `d829f05`, `33ea613`). Its `UserPromptSubmit` hook prepends up to 5 ranked "starting points" — symbol, `file:line`, signature — to each prompt. Keep it on probation and decide with evidence rather than impression.

## Why this is open rather than already decided

The cost is small enough that it isn't self-evidently wrong, and the observed value so far is zero. Both halves need more samples.

**Measured cost**

* \~410ms added latency per prompt (measured locally).
* \~150–250 tokens when it fires — cap is 5 hits, pointers only, no code bodies.
* Fresh full-price input every time; graft's own note: "per-prompt injected tokens are always fresh full-price input, so the pack stays tiny."
* Not a flat tax. A relevance floor (`INJECT_MIN_COVERAGE`, plus a two-clause `STRONG_FLOOR`/`HIGH_FLOOR` gate) and a per-session novelty gate suppress it on weak matches.

**Claimed benefit** — graft's source documents the failure it exists to prevent: with packs skipped more often, "the agent did not pull. It grepped 38 times."

## Evidence so far (4 samples, all from the setup session)

| Prompt | Injected | Relevant? |
| -- | -- | -- |
| "do we have the update hooks?" | `syncDrafts`, `telegram-webhook`, `sync.ts:finish` | No — matched nothing meaningful |
| "restart claude code" | `mintLinkCode`, `loadPositionByCode`, `flagCode` | No — lexical match on "code" |
| "restarted, check now" | `checkRateLimit`, `checkFirst`, `TeamPage` | No — lexical match on "check" |
| "strip the promo instruction and monitor prompt hints…" | *abstained* ("no strong match, 0.09") | N/A — gate worked correctly |

0 for 3 on packs actually injected; 1 correct abstention.

**Session 2 (2026-09-17, real feature work — team page header edit + M9 skill-review issue filing):**

| Prompt | Injected | Relevant? |
| -- | -- | -- |
| "Couple of changes: 1. In my team, replace refresh/edit text with icons..." | `listDrafts` (lib/drafts.ts) | No — unrelated to the header edit |
| (post plan-approval, same task) | `useAnchoredPanel`, `useDismissablePopover` (use-anchored-panel.ts) | No — topically adjacent to popovers but not consulted; the actual edit never touched this hook |
| (mid-edit, editing app/team/page.tsx's Telegram/Import/tooltip row) | `TapToReveal`, `CompactTelegram`, `FilterDisclosure` | **Yes** — these are exactly the components `TelegramLink`/`InfoTooltip` wrap in the row being edited |
| "after you are done, check [DSI-143](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-143/decide-whether-to-keep-grafts-userpromptsubmit-prompt-hints)" | `checkRateLimit`, `checkFirst`, `done` (use-error-shake.ts) | No — lexical match on "check", same failure mode as the "restarted, check now" sample above |

1 hit / 4 firings this session (all firings, not just genuine coding prompts this time — 3 of 4 were during actual feature work, 1 was a meta-instruction). Running total across both sessions: 1 hit / 7 firings injected, 1 correct abstention.

**The sample is biased and that matters.** All four were meta-prompts about tooling, not coding tasks. Graft indexes code, so it had nothing useful to offer and matched on stray words. The real question is whether it helps on *actual* work — "why is bank drifting on /team", "the beam drops the funding leg" — which this sample contains none of.

## What to do

Collect \~10 hint firings during genuine FPL feature or debugging work, then judge:

* **Hit** — a pointer named a file or symbol that was actually edited or read that turn.
* **Miss** — noise, or a lexical match on a common English word.

## Decision rule

* **≥30% hit rate** → keep as is.
* **10–30%** → keep, but consider raising `INJECT_MIN_COVERAGE` (currently 0.15; graft's own comment records that 0.15 let through a 0.165-coverage prompt that injected three irrelevant test files).
* **<10%** → drop the hook. Two-line change in `.claude/settings.json`: remove the `UserPromptSubmit` block. Post-edit re-index and `SessionStart` stay either way — those are the freshness mechanism and are not in question.

Note that raising the floor is only tunable by patching the global dist, which any graft upgrade wipes — same fate as the `tree-sitter-kotlin` shim and the promo patches. If the answer is "tune it", that is an argument for dropping instead, since the tuning will not survive.

## Upgrade reverts every customisation (verified 2026-09-14)

An earlier version of this issue said only that the global-dist patches are wiped. That understated it. `dist/upkeep.js` also auto-repairs the **repo-side** wiring.

`runUpkeep` is called from `claude/hooks.js` (the SessionStart hook) and `mcp/server.js` (MCP boot). It calls `reconcileWiring`, which compares `graft/.cache/wiring-stamp.json` against the running binary and, on any mismatch, re-runs init's writes for the already-wired hosts — replaying the recorded flags, not the current file contents. graft's own source concedes the effect: "`reconcileWiring` then keeps them *up to date*, which is worse than stale" (`cli.js:955`).

So one `graft upgrade` (or `npm i -g`), then one new session, undoes all of this:

| What | How it reverts | Loud? |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `tree-sitter-kotlin` shim | npm replaces `dist/` and `node_modules/` | **Loud** — CLI won't start at all, not even `--version` |
| Promo suppression in `savings.js` + `format.js` | npm replaces `dist/` | Silent — the tally directive simply returns |
| `.claude/skills/graft/SKILL.md` local-policy note | `reconcileWiring` rewrites from the new skill template | Silent — one banner line |
| `.claude/settings.json` stripped hooks | rewritten from `hooks: true` in the stamp; `tool-savings` and `Stop` come back | Silent |
| `.mcp.json` global-binary command | rewritten to `npx -y @nanonets/graft`, which re-fetches an unpatched copy **and** hits the Kotlin build failure | Silent until the MCP server fails to start |

The statusline stays off — `opts.statusline: false` is recorded in the stamp and replayed.

**Mitigation:** updates are never automatic. graft only ever runs `npm view` for a version check and prints "run graft upgrade"; it never self-installs. So the reverts cannot happen without an explicit `graft upgrade` / `npm i -g @nanonets/graft`. Before running either, plan to re-apply all five items — ideally via a `scripts/` re-apply script written first.

## Related

* `.claude/skills/graft/SKILL.md` — "Local policy: no savings tally"
* Commits `d829f05` (wiring) and `33ea613` (promo suppression) carry the details.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-145</b> — [Animation] Replace transition-all on the shared Button primitive</summary>

**Source:** emil-design-eng scope review.

`components/ui/button.tsx:7` uses `transition-all` — on the single most-reused primitive in the app, this forces the browser to watch every animatable property for change each frame.

3. Pair it with the existing duration/easing tokens already defined elsewhere in the design system (`--duration-fast`, `--ease-out` or equivalent Tailwind arbitrary values) rather than inventing new ones.
4. Grep for any other `transition-all` usage in `components/ui/` while in this area — this primitive is reused everywhere, so a quick sweep is cheap here.
5. Verify no visual regression: click/hover/focus a handful of button variants (primary, ghost, outline, icon) across a few pages in the browser preview, both themes.
6. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

<details><summary><b>DSI-146</b> — [Animation] Animate the shared TapToReveal/FilterDisclosure popover panels</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-17 · **Completed:** 2026-09-18 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Parent:** DSI-176

**Source:** emil-design-eng + find-animation-opportunities scope review (both flagged the same gap independently).

`components/info-tooltip.tsx:73-91` (`TapToReveal`, backing `InfoTooltip` and every "?" affordance — 5+ call sites) and `components/ui/filter-disclosure.tsx:45-54` mount/unmount their panel via a bare `{open && (...)}` with zero transition — pure teleport, unlike every other popover in the app (`action-menu.tsx`, `nav-links.tsx` dropdowns), which fade/scale in from their trigger using `--ease-slide`/`--ease-emphasis` tokens and `scale-95` (not `scale(0)`) starting points.

**Fix:** match the existing dropdown pattern — enter via `@starting-style { opacity: 0; transform: scale(0.95) }` → `opacity:1; transform: scale(1)`, `transform-origin` set to the trigger edge, \~150ms with the shared ease token. One change to the shared hook/wrapper (`useAnchoredPanel`/`TapToReveal`) covers every call site — highest-leverage item found in this pass.

## Fix plan

**Skill:** `animate` — this is a shared-hook change, so read the existing correct pattern in `components/action-menu.tsx`/`nav-links.tsx` dropdowns first and mirror it exactly rather than inventing new curves.

1. Open `components/ui/use-anchored-panel.ts` (the shared `useAnchoredPanel`/`useDismissablePopover` hook) and `components/info-tooltip.tsx:73-91` (`TapToReveal`) plus `components/ui/filter-disclosure.tsx:45-54`.
2. Identify how `action-menu.tsx`/`nav-links.tsx` already animate their dropdown panel (likely `@starting-style` or a `data-state`/`data-mounted` attribute driving `scale-95`/`opacity-0` → `scale-100`/`opacity-100`, with a named `--ease-*` token).
3. Apply the same mechanism to `TapToReveal`'s panel and `FilterDisclosure`'s panel — set `transform-origin` to the trigger edge (the anchored-panel hook likely already computes this for positioning; reuse it), duration \~150ms.
4. Since this is a shared component, changing it once fixes all 5+ call sites (every `InfoTooltip` "?" affordance, filter strips) — no per-call-site edits needed.
5. Verify in the browser: open/close a few different `InfoTooltip` instances and a `FilterDisclosure` on different pages, both themes, confirm no layout shift or clipping at panel edges (mobile width too, given this app's history of overflow bugs).
6. Check `prefers-reduced-motion` fallback if the dropdown pattern already handles it.
7. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-147</b> — [Animation] Animate SlideOver left/right variants like the bottom sheet already does</summary>

`components/ui/slide-over.tsx:79-101` — the `side="bottom"` variant correctly rises in with `motion-safe:[animation:sheet-rise_var(--duration-slower)_var(--ease-slide)]` and a fading backdrop, but `side="left"`/`side="right"` (used by the desktop `/compare` panel and the mobile nav drawer) get no animation at all — they just appear.

Respect the file's existing `motion-reduce` convention.

## Fix plan

3. Add equivalent `left`/`right` keyframes or `@starting-style` rules: `translateX(-100%)`→`translateX(0)` for left, `translateX(100%)`→`translateX(0)` for right, same `--duration-slower`/`--ease-slide` tokens.
4. Fade the backdrop on the same duration for both new variants, matching bottom.
5. Preserve the file's existing `motion-reduce` guard for all three variants.
6. Verify in the browser preview: open the desktop `/compare` panel (right-side) and the mobile nav drawer (left-side), confirm slide-in/out feels consistent with the bottom sheet, test at 375px width per `responsive-check` skill, both themes.
7. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

<details><summary><b>DSI-149</b> — [Animation] Fade in transfer-plan and decision-analytics results instead of popping in</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-17 · **Completed:** 2026-09-18 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Low · **Parent:** DSI-176

**Source:** find-animation-opportunities scope review.

Same pattern in two places — a multi-second loading state swaps straight to the result with no bridge:

* `components/transfer-plan.tsx:162-168` — `{loading && <Spinner/>}` → `{!loading && result && (...)}`, and `result` here is the headline recommendation (e.g. `+8.5 xP − 8 hit − 0.2 risk = +0.3`).

**Fix:** fade the result block in on mount — `@starting-style { opacity: 0 }` → `opacity: 1`, `transition: opacity var(--duration-base) var(--ease-emphasis)` (\~250ms). Opacity only, no transform — this is a content swap, not a spatial move.

**Skill:** `animate` (simple opacity-only fade, no transform — per the framework, this is a content swap not a spatial move).

Wrap the `{!loading && result && (...)}` result block so it fades in on mount: `@starting-style { opacity: 0 }` → `opacity: 1`, `transition: opacity var(--duration-base) var(--ease-emphasis)` (\~250ms). If `@starting-style` browser support is a concern for this app's target, use the existing `data-mounted` attribute + `useEffect` fallback pattern already documented in the `animate` skill.
2. Repeat the identical treatment in `components/decision-analytics-panel.tsx:186-202` for its loading→table swap.
3. Since both are the same pattern, consider extracting a tiny shared wrapper/utility class if it doesn't overreach the fix (don't over-engineer — two call sites doesn't necessarily need a new abstraction; a shared Tailwind utility class is enough).
4. Verify in the browser preview: trigger a transfer-plan search and a decision-analytics load, confirm the result fades in rather than popping, both themes.
5. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

<details><summary><b>DSI-152</b> — [Writing] Map raw OAuth error codes to friendly messages on the sign-in callback</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-17 · **Completed:** 2026-09-18 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Parent:** DSI-176

**Source:** better-writing scope review.

`app/auth/callback/page.tsx:32-35,61-63` — on failure, shows `Sign-in failed: {error}` where `error` is the raw `error_description`/`error` query param straight from the OAuth provider (e.g. `access_denied`). This reads as a system error rather than "you clicked cancel," and an unmapped provider error could be arbitrarily technical — violates this app's own "say what the number/word means" convention.

**Fix:** map known codes (`access_denied` → "You cancelled sign-in at Google.") and fall back to a plain "Something went wrong signing you in." for anything unrecognized.

Related, same file: the "Signing you in…" state (line 72) has no timeout/fallback if the token exchange silently stalls — worth a follow-up ("Taking longer than usual — try again") while touching this code.

## Fix plan

**Skill:** `better-writing` for the message copy; no animation/interface skill needed, this is logic + text.

1. Open `app/auth/callback/page.tsx:32-35,61-63`.
2. Add a small mapping function (e.g. `friendlyAuthError(code: string)`, consistent naming with this repo's existing `friendlyAuthError` helper referenced elsewhere in CLAUDE.md's sign-in notes — check if one already exists before writing a new one) that maps known OAuth error codes to plain language: `access_denied` → "You cancelled sign-in at Google.", with a generic fallback "Something went wrong signing you in." for anything unrecognized.
3. Replace the raw `Sign-in failed: {error}` interpolation with the mapped message.
4. While in this file, add the related follow-up noted in the review: a \~10s timeout on the "Signing you in…" state that surfaces "Taking longer than usual — try again" if the token exchange stalls (use a `setTimeout` cleared on success/unmount).
5. Verify via the `signed-in` skill's real sign-in path (magic-link) is overkill for this; instead simulate failure by navigating directly to `/auth/callback/?error=access_denied` (or whatever query shape the page expects) in the browser preview and confirming the friendly message renders.
6. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-156</b> — [Interface] Fix text-zinc-400 contrast failure in light theme across 5 components</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-17 · **Completed:** 2026-09-18 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** Medium · **Parent:** DSI-176

**Source:** better-interface scope review.

`components/club-tactics.tsx:173`, `components/chip-timing.tsx:899`, `components/confidence-badge.tsx:87`, `components/compare-panel.tsx:408`, `components/accuracy-scoreboard.tsx:181` — all render real content (a source filename, a time-window caption, a rate figure, a placeholder value) in `text-zinc-400` with no `dark:` pairing. On a white/light card that's `#a1a1aa` on `#ffffff`, ≈2.35:1 contrast — below the WCAG AA minimum (4.5:1, or 3:1 for large text).

**Fix:** bump to `text-zinc-500` — already used correctly elsewhere in these same files for equivalent captions — or add an explicit light-mode-safe pairing.

## Fix plan

**Skill:** `better-colors` (contrast checking/validation) combined with `better-accessibility`.

1. Load the `better-colors` skill to check contrast ratios against WCAG AA before and after the change (don't eyeball it).
2. Open each of the five locations: `components/club-tactics.tsx:173`, `components/chip-timing.tsx:899`, `components/confidence-badge.tsx:87`, `components/compare-panel.tsx:408`, `components/accuracy-scoreboard.tsx:181`.
3. Change `text-zinc-400` → `text-zinc-500` at each (this repo already uses `zinc-500` correctly for equivalent captions elsewhere in the same files — confirm the value with a quick grep first so the fix is consistent app-wide, not just at these five spots).
4. Verify with `better-colors`'s contrast validator (or a manual contrast check against the light-theme card background `#ffffff`/`#f4f4f5`) that `zinc-500` clears 4.5:1 for body text or 3:1 if any of these are large text.
5. Verify visually in both themes via the browser preview — confirm the dark-theme appearance is unaffected (zinc-500 should still read fine against the app's dark purple cards) since only the light-theme case was failing.
6. `npx tsc --noEmit` + `npm run lint`.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-176</b> — Sprint 37 — M9 design-audit Todo sweep (interface, writing, animation)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-18 · **Completed:** 2026-09-18 · **Milestone:** M9 - Design Audit · **Priority:** No priority

Takes the whole Linear Todo column as of 2026-09-18: 13 independent follow-ups from the M9 design audit's `better-interface`/`better-writing`/`animate`/`emil-design-eng` scope reviews. No comments changed any issue's scope; no issue has an unmet gate. Three phases, each a stopping point:

**Phase 1 — Interface/accessibility** ([DSI-156](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-156/interface-fix-text-zinc-400-contrast-failure-in-light-theme-across-5), [DSI-155](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-155/interface-add-missing-aria-label-to-icon-only-pin-button-then-sweep), [DSI-154](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-154/interface-add-focus-ring-and-typebutton-to-club-tactics-expand-control)): light-theme contrast fix, missing `aria-label` on an icon-only button (plus a sweep for the same gap), focus ring + `type="button"` on the club-tactics expand control.

**Phase 2 — Writing** ([DSI-153](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-153/writing-drop-changelog-language-from-the-compare-panels-form-weight), [DSI-150](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-150/writing-clarify-the-ambiguous-last-recorded-baseline-in-the-league), [DSI-152](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-152/writing-map-raw-oauth-error-codes-to-friendly-messages-on-the-sign-in), [DSI-151](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-151/writing-explain-what-std-dev-means-not-just-define-it)): drop changelog language from the compare panel's form-weight hint, clarify the league-rank tooltip's "last recorded" baseline, map raw OAuth error codes to friendly messages, explain what Std dev means rather than just naming it.

**Phase 3 — Animation** ([DSI-145](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-145/animation-replace-transition-all-on-the-shared-button-primitive), [DSI-144](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-144/animation-add-press-feedback-to-pitch-view-player-slot-button), [DSI-149](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-149/animation-fade-in-transfer-plan-and-decision-analytics-results-instead), [DSI-148](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-148/animation-animate-player-detail-expand-content-to-match-its-own), [DSI-147](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-147/animation-animate-slideover-leftright-variants-like-the-bottom-sheet), [DSI-146](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-146/animation-animate-the-shared-taptorevealfilterdisclosure-popover)): replace `transition-all` on the shared Button primitive, add press feedback to the pitch-view player slot, fade in transfer-plan/decision-analytics results, animate player-detail's expand content to match its own chevron, animate SlideOver left/right variants like the bottom sheet, animate the shared TapToReveal/FilterDisclosure popover panels.

Tracked in [docs/sprints/sprint-37.md](<../../docs/sprints/sprint-37.md>).

<details><summary><b>DSI-178</b> — Expected-minutes discrimination — the pooled bias hides two cancelling cohorts</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-21 · **Completed:** 2026-09-21 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Model · **Parent:** DSI-182

Spun out of the [DSI-50](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-50/gw5-check-in-read-the-accuracy-scoreboards-bias-sign-at-n4) GW5 check-in (see its 2026-09-21 comment for the full read).

## The evidence

At locked GW2–5 data, n=2,587, the pooled bias is **−0.080** (SE 0.044, t=−1.82) — near enough to zero to look calibrated. It is not. It decomposes into two large opposite biases that nearly cancel:

| cohort | n | bias | mean xP | mean actual | mean exp. mins | mean actual mins |
| -- | -- | -- | -- | -- | -- | -- |
| appeared | 1,223 | **+0.627** | 2.384 | 3.011 | 47.1 | 64.2 |
| no-show | 1,364 | **−0.714** | 0.714 | 0.000 | 15.8 | 0.0 |
| all | 2,587 | −0.080 | 1.504 | 1.424 | 30.6 | 30.4 |

Expected minutes are **right in aggregate** (30.6 predicted vs 30.4 actual) and **wrong per player** (47.1 vs 64.2 on players who appeared; 15.8 vs 0 on players who did not). That is a discrimination failure, not a calibration offset — the model spreads minutes too evenly across the squad instead of concentrating them on the players who actually start.

## Why this is not [DSI-53](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53/gw10-refit-positioncalibration-if-the-gw5-sign-held)

A multiplicative points scale applies the same factor to both cohorts, so `positionCalibration` cannot reach this. Refitting it would shrink the appeared cohort's already-positive bias and deepen the no-show cohort's negative one. This is why [DSI-53](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53/gw10-refit-positioncalibration-if-the-gw5-sign-held) was written out of the GW10 plan.

The work belongs on `expectedMinutes` / `startProbability` / `p60` — the terms in `player_prediction_archive` that produced the 47.1 and 15.8 above.

## Gate

The existing backtest, not a new threshold: bias, MAE and Pearson r via `accuracyStats` (`lib/stats.ts:108`), which `scripts/backtest-walkforward.ts:162` already shares with the live scoreboard so the figures are comparable. Score **per cohort as well as pooled** — a change that improves the pooled number by trading one cohort against the other is the failure mode this issue exists to catch.

Add a secondary discrimination metric (AUC or Brier on "started", say) since the pooled bias is demonstrably blind to the problem.

## Also open

**GKP bias −0.319** (n=281, t=−2.78) is the one position-level bias that survives the cohort split. DEF −0.033, MID −0.052, FWD −0.095 are all inside noise. Worth checking whether it is the same minutes story (goalkeepers either play 90 or 0, so the no-show cohort should be cleanly separable there) or something in the saves/clean-sheet terms.

## Timing

Needs more gameweeks before a refit is worth running — GW6 lands 2026-10-10 after a three-week international break. Sized for the M7 GW10 batch.

---

**2026-09-21 — one hypothesis eliminated.** [DSI-179](<https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-179>) was briefly filed as a candidate contributor to the +0.627 here, on the claim that the xP model excluded forwards from defensive-contribution points. **That claim was wrong** — the model already scored forwards at threshold 12, and `player_xp_horizons` confirms they get a value. The defect was in the UI, not the model, so **model behaviour never changed and the full +0.627 remains unexplained.**

Worth stating because it narrows the search: whatever drives the appeared-cohort under-prediction, it is not a missing scoring term for forwards.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-179</b> — xDC: the UI hid forwards' defensive-contribution column (the model was never wrong)</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-21 · **Completed:** 2026-09-21 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Model · **Parent:** DSI-180

> **Retitled and closed 2026-09-21.** The original title and premise below were wrong: I filed this as "the xP model excludes forwards from points they earn", and it doesn't. `supabase/functions/_shared/xp-model.ts:137` already carried `dcThreshold: { 1: 0, 2: 10, 3: 12, 4: 12 }`, and `player_xp_horizons` confirms forwards get a value (1 of 79 non-zero, max 0.01 — near zero because forwards rarely reach 12 defensive actions, which is a low expectation rather than an exclusion).
>
> The real defect was narrower and entirely on the presentation side: `XDC_MODEL_NOTE`'s prose, and `XDC_POSITIONS = new Set([2, 3])` declared **independently** in `app/players/page.tsx` and `components/compare-panel.tsx`, which hid the column for forwards. Fixed by giving the fact one home — `DC_THRESHOLD_BY_ELEMENT_TYPE` and `scoresDefensiveContribution()` in `lib/scoring.ts` — which `lib/fpl-scoring-rules.ts` also reads from rather than keeping a third copy.
>
> **No backtest re-run was needed**, because model behaviour did not change, and [DSI-178](<https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178>) keeps its full +0.627 appeared-cohort bias to explain. Breakdown reconciliation re-run after the change: still 3,218 / 3,218 exact.
>
> The measurement itself stands and is what the fix is built on — see it below. The FWD cell is still n=1 at the threshold, so the *magnitude* is worth re-checking as more gameweeks land; the direction is settled by `scoring_rules` agreeing independently.

---

Found while verifying the player card's derived points breakdown (Sprint 38).

## The measurement

`XDC_MODEL_NOTE` (`lib/scoring.ts:85-105`) states the defensive-contribution threshold is "10 for DEF, 12 for MID — 0 for GKP/FWD, who never score it". **For 2026-27 that is wrong for forwards.**

Measured over 677 player-gameweeks of 2026-27 with >=60 minutes and no goals/assists/cards/penalties/own-goals/bonus — so the score reduces to appearance + clean sheet + goals conceded + saves + DC. Residual after subtracting appearance(2) + CS(4 GKP/DEF, 1 MID) − floor(GC/2) − floor(saves/3):

| element_type | DC band | n | residual (min = max = avg) |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| 2 DEF | <10 | 203 | 0 |
| 2 DEF | 10–11 | 33 | **+2** |
| 2 DEF | =12 | 39 | +2 |
| 3 MID | <10 | 205 | 0 |
| 3 MID | 10–11 | 30 | 0 |
| 3 MID | =12 | 36 | **+2** |
| 4 FWD | <10 | 46 | 0 |
| 4 FWD | =12 | 1 | **+2** |
| 1 GKP | <10 | 84 | 0 |

**Zero variance in every cell.** So: DEF threshold 10, MID threshold 12, **FWD threshold 12 and forwards do score it.**

`public.scoring_rules` — FPL's own values, season-keyed and synced from bootstrap — independently gives `defensive_contribution` FWD = 2, GKP = 0. Two independent sources agree.

The FWD cell is n=1 at the threshold, so confirm the magnitude as more gameweeks land. The *direction* is not in doubt.

## Impact

The xDC term (`player_xp_horizons.xdc_*`) excludes forwards, so **forward xP is systematically missing points they earn.** A candidate contributor to the +0.627 under-prediction on the appeared cohort recorded in [DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling).

`app/players/page.tsx` carries the same assumption independently: `XDC_POSITIONS = new Set([2, 3])`.

## Work

1. Find what computes xDC — grep `xdc_` across `lib/`, `supabase/functions/`, `supabase/migrations/`.
2. Extend it to forwards at threshold 12, and read the thresholds from `scoring_rules` where practical rather than hardcoding (the table is season-keyed, so a rule change needs no code change). `lib/fpl-scoring-rules.ts` already does this and carries the measured thresholds with their evidence.
3. Correct `XDC_MODEL_NOTE` and `XDC_POSITIONS`.
4. Check whether GKP should be excluded outright or merely has no qualifying rows yet.

## Gate

The existing backtest (`scripts/backtest-walkforward.ts`, `accuracyStats` in `lib/stats.ts:108`) — confirm forward bias improves and nothing else regresses. Per CLAUDE.md, verify with an `npx tsx` harness against live data before trusting the UI, and keep the harness out of the commit.

Evidence and method: `docs/sprints/sprint-38.md` §4.

> **Deepayan Sinha**, 2026-09-21
>
> ## Correction — the model was never broken. This is a display and documentation bug.
> 
> I filed this claiming the xP model excludes forwards from xDC. **That is wrong**, and the claim that it contributes to DSI-178's +0.627 appeared-cohort bias is wrong with it.
> 
> `supabase/functions/_shared/xp-model.ts:137` already has:
> 
> ```ts
> dcThreshold: { 1: 0, 2: 10, 3: 12, 4: 12 }
> ```
> 
> Forwards at 12 — correct. The scoring at `:1619-1623` gates on `threshold > 0`, which excludes only goalkeepers, and reads the points value from `scoring_rules` (FWD = 2). So the model has been computing forwards' xDC correctly all along.
> 
> Confirmed against `player_xp_horizons`:
> 
> | element_type | n | rows with xdc_5 > 0 | max xdc_5 |
> |---|---|---|---|
> | 1 GKP | 73 | 0 | 0.00 |
> | 2 DEF | 217 | 120 | 5.13 |
> | 3 MID | 298 | 101 | 4.43 |
> | 4 FWD | 79 | **1** | 0.01 |
> 
> Forwards do get a value. It is near zero because forwards rarely reach 12 defensive actions — **a low expectation, not an exclusion.**
> 
> ### What was actually wrong
> 
> 1. **`XDC_MODEL_NOTE` prose** — "forwards and goalkeepers never score it" is false for forwards, and it is user-facing tooltip text.
> 2. **`XDC_POSITIONS = new Set([2, 3])`, declared independently in `app/players/page.tsx` and `components/compare-panel.tsx`** — the UI hid the xDC column for forwards. A forward who *did* clear the threshold showed "—" next to a real computed number. Two copies of one fact, both wrong.
> 
> ### Fix
> 
> - `DC_THRESHOLD_BY_ELEMENT_TYPE` and `scoresDefensiveContribution()` now live in `lib/scoring.ts` as the single source, documented as mirroring `MODEL_PARAMS.dcThreshold` (duplicated rather than imported because the model file is Deno and excluded from tsconfig — if they ever disagree, the model's copy is the truth).
> - Both `XDC_POSITIONS` sets deleted; both call sites use the shared helper.
> - `lib/fpl-scoring-rules.ts` derives its own thresholds from the same constant rather than redeclaring them — that was a third copy I introduced earlier this sprint.
> - `XDC_MODEL_NOTE` corrected, and it now says explicitly that a near-zero figure for a forward is a low expectation rather than an exclusion.
> 
> Breakdown reconciliation re-run after the change: **3,218 of 3,218 player-gameweeks still reconcile exactly, zero unattributed.**
> 
> ### What remains open
> 
> The measurement itself stands — DEF 10, MID 12, FWD 12, zero variance over 677 player-gameweeks — and it now has one home. The FWD cell is still n=1 at the threshold, so the *magnitude* is worth re-checking as more gameweeks land, even though the direction is settled by `scoring_rules`.
> 
> **No backtest re-run is needed**, because the model's behaviour has not changed. DSI-178 keeps its full +0.627 to explain.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-180</b> — Sprint 38 — the player profile, the price reading, and a shortlist</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-21 · **Completed:** 2026-09-21 · **Milestone:** M7 · GW10 model batch · **Priority:** No priority

Created retroactively: this sprint was built from direct requests rather than through `/start-sprint`, so it had no parent issue while it ran. Merged as [Dsinha97/fpl-app#31](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/sprint-38-the-player-profile-the-price-reading-and-a-shortlist-9433c2f7d644) (`0c63cd2`), 20 commits, 47 files.

Three threads that shared a spine — the app knew a great deal about a player and had nowhere good to show it. `/players`, the page built for researching a player, opened nothing at all.

Full write-up: [docs/sprints/sprint-38.md](<../../docs/sprints/sprint-38.md>).

## What shipped

**The player profile** — `components/player-modal.tsx`, three tabs (Overview / Gameweeks / History) reachable from `/players` and, via a "Full profile" link on the pitch popover, from `/builder`, `/team` and `/deadline`. Deliberately *not* a replacement for `PlayerDetail`: that popover's never-fetch contract is what lets four pages render it with no data coupling. Its "Show full details" disclosure is gone (578 → 398 lines), its content folded into the modal, which loads its own context so the card reads identically from every page.

The Gameweeks points breakdown is **derived**, because FPL itemises a score only for the live gameweek. Values come from the `scoring_rules` table, not a constants file, and every breakdown is reconciled against the stored total with any difference shown as "Unattributed". Verified: 3,218 / 3,218 player-gameweeks reconcile exactly.

**The price reading**, which turned out to have been broken since Sprint 29 — twice over. `loadPriceProgress` was unpaged against the 1000-row cap and read \~2% of its data, which *looked quiet rather than broken*; fixing that exposed a second bug, differencing `transfers_in_event` across gameweek resets, which inverted the sign for 89% of players. Thresholds are now fitted on measurement: falls `3,577 + 31,870` per 1% owned (R²=0.811), rises flat 378,000 (R²=0.022). The verdict ladder was renamed to describe position rather than predict tonight, because crossing a threshold is only \~10% predictive.

**The shortlist** — `user_shortlist`, owner-scoped, `auth.uid() = user_id` in both directions, verified in a rolled-back transaction rather than merely enabled. Named "shortlist" because "watchlist" already meant the price-sampling set.

## Issues touched

* [DSI-50](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-50/gw5-check-in-read-the-accuracy-scoreboards-bias-sign-at-n4) — GW5 check-in re-read at locked data; unchanged, stays open for GW6 (2026-10-10)
* [DSI-54](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-54/price-change-prediction-step-3-fit-a-classifier-may-never-ship) — falls classifier gate run; **failed at every budget**, so the heuristic ships. Threshold work gated separately (MIXED). Rises still held to \~GW10–12
* [DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling) — opened: expected-minutes discrimination
* [DSI-179](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-179/xdc-excludes-forwards-from-defensive-contribution-points-they-actually) — opened and closed: the xDC display/documentation fix

## Two corrections made inside the sprint

Both recorded rather than quietly fixed, since the reversing commits are in the history either way:

1. **xDC and forwards** — filed as a model bug; the model was never wrong (`dcThreshold` already had FWD at 12). The real defect was the UI hiding the column and two independently-declared `XDC_POSITIONS` sets. [DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling) keeps its full +0.627 to explain.
2. **The falls classifier** — reported as passing its gate; it doesn't. That run used a feature computed across gameweek resets, which handicapped the naive baseline sharing the same input.

## Open after this

* A server-side price rollup — `loadPriceProgress` ships \~42,000 rows to produce 667 readings
* The rise threshold's level is a mean over 55 events, not a mechanism
* Re-run the threshold gate as more nights accumulate (23 scored nights is underpowered)
* The three `toPlayerData` copies (`builder:1030`, `deadline:750`, `team:989`) are still three

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-183</b> — Swipe-to-dismiss gestures for nav, player card, and compare panel</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-22 · **Completed:** 2026-09-22 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** No priority

Built 2026-09-21, prompted by the owner asking for the interface to behave like a mobile app. `components/ui/slide-over.tsx` — the shared primitive already backing `MobileNav`, the mobile player-card sheet, and the compare panel — now uses `motion` for real `AnimatePresence` exit animations (it previously unmounted instantly) plus direction-aware swipe-to-dismiss: the nav drags left, the compare panel drags right, the player-card sheet drags down, each closing the way it opened. Commits past 35% of the panel's extent or a 500px/s flick, otherwise springs back via an explicit `animate()` call on a real-range `dragConstraints` (a degenerate point constraint measurably misbehaved in this repo's environment). Touch-gated so desktop mouse interaction is untouched; the compare panel's header (not the whole panel) is the drag handle since its table scrolls on the same axis as the close-drag.

Docs: `docs/sprints/swipe-to-dismiss.md`. PR [Dsinha97/fpl-app#34](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/swipe-to-dismiss-gestures-for-nav-menu-player-card-and-compare-panel-2bcacef08671), merged into `main`.

</details>

Headline results:

Tracked as [DSI-187](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-187/latency-the-price-watch-scan-downloads-the-whole-ownership-history).

> 
> - Fixed with serial paging and a total order; a failed page produces no alerts rather than partial ones.
> - A harness on live data showed the old code computed 6 of 15 totals, all wrong. The new code computes all 15, each matching the site's `loadPriceProgress` exactly.
> - One alert already sent (215136, reported −67,848, true ≈ −9,419) was false.
> - **Deployed as `notify` v7**, which also picked up DSI-142's cron-auth retry that the v6 bundle lacked.

<details><summary><b>DSI-185</b> — Expected-minutes model — fix the appeared/no-show discrimination failure (gradient-boosted minutes/injury)</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-24 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Medium · **Labels:** Model · **Parent:** DSI-73 · **Relations:** blocks DSI-186

Split out of [DSI-73](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-73/sprint-17-part-2-predictive-models-gated-on-the-positioncalibration) on 2026-09-24. This is the follow-up [DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling) left open: it measured the failure but shipped no fix.

**The problem.** The model's overall bias is \~0 (−0.080 at GW5) only because two large errors cancel. Players who don't play are over-predicted by −0.714 (n=1,364); players who do play are under-predicted by +0.627 (n=1,223). Expected minutes are right in aggregate (30.6 vs 30.4) and wrong per player (47.1 vs 64.2 among players who appeared). That's a discrimination failure in `expectedMinutes`/`startProbability`, and no multiplicative scale can reach it, which is why [DSI-53](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-53/gw10-refit-positioncalibration-if-the-gw5-sign-held) was canceled. Goalkeepers carry a separate bias (−0.319 to −0.352, ≈3 SE) that may or may not be the same story.

**Scope.** A minutes/start model (gradient-boosted, or anything simpler that wins) that replaces or corrects the current `mpg`/`start_share` terms. Check whether goalkeepers are the same minutes story or something in the saves/clean-sheet terms.

**Gate: the standing one, not narrowed.** Walk-forward in `scripts/backtest-walkforward.ts` against the named naive baseline (the player's own last 5 gameweeks), reported the same way whether or not it passes. It must improve, and not just shift:

* the per-cohort bias/MAE (`byAppearance`, `gkpByAppearance`), not only the pooled figure;
* the start-probability Brier score (`startBrier`, added by [DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling)).

**Not gated on data to *build*.** It can be built and backtested on the four historical seasons now. Scoring it on 2026-27 needs the last-5 baseline, which first exists at GW6 (2026-10-10); a meaningful current-season read comes with the M7 GW10 batch (\~2026-11-09).

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-39.md` ([DSI-178](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-178/expected-minutes-discrimination-the-pooled-bias-hides-two-cancelling)), `docs/sprints/gw5-check-in.md` §2, `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`, `docs/phase-4-model.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-186</b> — Accuracy claims — only once the model beats the last-5 baseline out-of-sample</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-24 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** Low · **Labels:** Model · **Parent:** DSI-73 · **Relations:** related DSI-50

Split out of [DSI-73](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-73/sprint-17-part-2-predictive-models-gated-on-the-positioncalibration) on 2026-09-24. This is the half of [DSI-73](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-73/sprint-17-part-2-predictive-models-gated-on-the-positioncalibration) that stays blocked: xP accuracy claims and recommendation accuracy, beyond the descriptive scoreboard Sprint 34 shipped (`lib/prediction-accuracy.ts`, `/settings` → Pipeline). Reporting a residual is fine today; *claiming the model is accurate* is not.

**Why it's blocked.** Sprint 17a found that out-of-sample, the model loses to a naive average of each player's last 5 gameweeks in every season tested. The GW5 check-in then showed the \~0 pooled bias is two cancelling cohort errors, so a pooled number can't certify accuracy either.

**Gate. None of these are narrowed, and all must hold:**

1. [DSI-185](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-185/expected-minutes-model-fix-the-appearedno-show-discrimination-failure) (the expected-minutes fix) has shipped, or has been run and reported as a null result.
2. Walk-forward on 2026-27 beats the last-5 baseline on **MAE and Pearson r**, pooled **and** per cohort (appeared/no-show, and GKP separately).
3. Run no earlier than the M7 GW10 batch (\~2026-11-09). The baseline first exists at GW6 (2026-10-10), and a handful of gameweeks is too thin.

**Explicitly not a gate:** [DSI-50](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-50/gw5-check-in-bias-sign-read-at-n4-re-read-at-locked-data-next-read-gw6)'s GW5/GW6 bias-sign read. It reads the pooled bias, which is \~0 *because* the cohorts cancel, so a clean reading there says nothing about accuracy.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-17a.md`, `docs/sprints/gw5-check-in.md` §2, `docs/phase-4-model.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-187</b> — Latency — the price-watch scan downloads the whole ownership-history table on every /players or /transfers visit</summary>

**State:** Backlog · **Created:** 2026-09-24 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** High · **Labels:** Improvement

Found in flight by Sprint 40 ([DSI-184](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-184/sprint-40-latency-the-shared-bundle-and-the-read-waterfalls)), not fixed. The largest latency cost measured so far. It only grows from here.

**What happens.** `loadPriceProgress` (`lib/price-watch.ts`) narrows its `player_ownership_history` read to `earliestAnchor`: the *oldest* last-price-change across all requested players. The first price sync leaves every player an initial `player_price_history` row, and players who have never been repriced keep it as their anchor, so the earliest anchor is 2026-08-03 (pre-season). The filter therefore excludes nothing, and every visit downloads the whole table.

**Measured 2026-09-24:**

* 73,287 rows, as \~74 concurrent 1000-row pages (148 requests in dev under StrictMode, averaging \~8 s each, last finishing at \~19 s). It doesn't block first paint, but it saturates the connection for 15+ seconds, so anything else the page requests queues behind it.
* A per-player anchor would still ship **46,938 rows**, so moving the anchor filter server-side only removes about a third.
* The table grows \~15,000 rows a day (23 samples × 667 players).

**Constraint: one implementation.** The reading depends on two pieces of arithmetic that must not fork into a TypeScript copy and a SQL copy: the reset-aware net-transfer accumulation (`netTransfersSinceLastPriceChange`; the per-gameweek counters reset at every deadline) and the fitted, ownership-scaled thresholds (`thresholdsFor`). Candidate shapes to weigh; none is chosen:

1. An RPC or view that returns only each player's post-anchor rows (the windowing moves server-side; the arithmetic stays in `lib/`). Bounded, but still \~47k rows and growing.
2. Precompute the per-player accumulated net transfers since each player's last change on write (in the sync that samples ownership) and read a \~667-row table. Smallest payload, but the accumulation then lives in Deno, and the site's copy must become the one that reads it rather than a second one.
3. Cache per season in the browser (`lib/player-pool.ts` pattern) so `/players` → `/transfers` doesn't refetch. Cheap and complementary, but it doesn't fix the first visit.

**Gate.** Measured like Sprint 40: signed-in settle time and request count on `/players` and `/transfers`, before and after, same session. **The price-watch readings must be identical** for every player (a harness like Sprint 40's, comparing `netTransfers`/`progress` per player code).

**Related, fixed 2026-09-24:** `notify`'s price-proximity alert had the same unpaged-read bug in its own copy of this read (asked for 4,284 rows and got the oldest 1,000). It's paged now; a fix here should consider whether that function reads the same precomputed source.

Docs: `docs/sprints/sprint-40.md` §3, `docs/wiki/performance.md`, `docs/wiki/data-pipeline.md`

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-202</b> — Scoped MIT licence for the public repo</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-26 · **Completed:** 2026-09-26 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** No priority

Built 2026-09-25, modelled on the portfolio repo's licence. The repo went public ([DSI-55](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/issue/DSI-55/confirm-outside-collaborator-fork-pr-workflow-approval-setting)), so it needed a licence stating what may be reused.

* **MIT:** the source code (`app/`, `components/`, `lib/`, `scripts/`, `supabase/`, `.github/`, config and styles).
* **All rights reserved:** `docs/`, README prose, and the FPL Decision name, logo and icons.
* **Excluded as third-party:** FPL/Premier League data and imagery, and FootyStats-derived data.

Docs: `LICENSE`; the scope is summarised under the privacy note in `docs/wiki/deployment.md`. Merged in [Dsinha97/fpl-app#38](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/add-scoped-mit-license-81ea6469de6b). Recorded retroactively: it had no issue when it merged.

</details>

<details><summary><b>DSI-203</b> — Search indexing: sitemap, robots.txt, per-route metadata, preview card</summary>

**State:** Done · **Created:** 2026-09-26 · **Completed:** 2026-09-26 · **Milestone:** M8 · Beyond this season · **Priority:** No priority

Built 2026-09-26, prompted by the owner's Google Search Console submission. Before this, `/sitemap.xml` returned 404, `robots.txt` was Cloudflare's comment-only file, and all 18 routes shared one title and description.

* `app/sitemap.ts` and `app/robots.ts`, generated at build time.
* A title, description, canonical, Open Graph and Twitter tags on every indexable route, all driven by `SITE_ROUTES` in `lib/seo.ts`. The sitemap reads the same list, so the two can't drift.
* `noindex, follow` on personal, auth and redirect-stub routes.
* `public/og-image.png`, a 1200×630 preview card.

Outside the repo: the sitemap is submitted in Search Console (10 pages discovered) and imported into Bing, and Cloudflare Crawler Hints is on.

Still to check: Search Console → Indexing → Pages in 1–2 weeks.

Docs: `docs/sprints/seo.md`. Merged in [Dsinha97/fpl-app#39](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/seo-sitemap-robots-per-route-metadata-og-card-36b78a87a51b); the wiki ingest is in [Dsinha97/fpl-app#40](https://linear.app/dsinha-org/review/docs-wiki-ingest-for-the-licence-and-seo-prs-38-39-a412c79d5f7b).

</details>
